+# Final harness (branch `final-harness`)
+
+This branch is `main` with `start-pose-live-guard` and `hybrid-harness` merged in, the LiveKit observer from
+`codex/hill-climb-livekit`, and two fixes the merge needed. This file records what was kept, what was left out and
+why, and how to run a match. The how-to for every flag is in [HARNESS_GUIDE.md](HARNESS_GUIDE.md).
+
+## Run a match
+
+```bash
+python -m harness plans/hill_climb_v4.yaml --live --match --detect plans/table_colors.yaml --live-guard
+```
+
+`--match` refuses to start (exit 2) with `--budget`, a `manual` / `+manual` verifier, or a stage a person would
+answer. It turns the operator console off, reads practice lessons without writing new ones, and records
+`match: true` in the manifest. It warns if `--detect ... --live-guard` is missing. Without the live guard, nothing
+stops an 8-ball between monitor verdicts.
+
+For base Dream-1 (no fine-tune), use `plans/hybrid_v4.yaml` instead. It has specific colour and position prompts,
+monitor-written prompt rewrites, `reset_first` and the "no power" prefix. Drop its cable 3 and 4 stages, which
+cost about 18 points a match in the sim:
+
+```bash
+python -m harness plans/hybrid_v4.yaml --live --match --skip cable_extra --detect plans/table_colors.yaml --live-guard
+```
+
+For the LiveKit points, show the referee `python -m harness.livekit_observer` before the match (guide §6h). It only
+subscribes and is never part of the run.
+
+## What's in, and where it came from
+
+| Piece | From | Why it's in |
+|---|---|---|
+| Runner state machine, `confirm: 2`, monitor verifier, `guard:` with `max_blind_s` | main | The core. A false "done" is a DQ, so every gated stage needs two agreeing verdicts. |
+| Local colour detector `--detect` | main | Local, so it answers in milliseconds where the monitor takes seconds. It feeds both guards and draws boxes on the monitor's frames. |
+| Live guard `--live-guard` (10 Hz on `/frame/<wrist>.jpg`, 3 frames in a row, sends `/stop` itself) | start-pose-live-guard | The only thing fast enough to stop an 8-ball. See the stress results below. |
+| Start-pose gate (`start_pose:` + `plans/start_poses.json`) | start-pose-live-guard | It won't send a prompt from a pose the policy never started from. |
+| `decide.py` | start-pose-live-guard | Kept in shadow mode only: it logs what it would do and decides nothing. |
+| Stitched history grid + `parallel: 2` (now in `hill_climb_v4.yaml` too) | hybrid-harness | Measured on the station: a verdict every ~4.9 s instead of ~9.7 s. Judge speed is the main lever in the sim. |
+| Light frames (`/frame/<cam>.jpg?w=640`, ~35 KB) | hybrid-harness | Dream's round trip went from 2–6 s to ~0.8 s once the harness stopped pulling ~2 MB debug-view trips. |
+| Gripper sensors (`GET /arms`) overrule the monitor's "holding" | hybrid-harness | A gripper closed on nothing is not holding a cable, whatever the image seems to show. |
+| `rewrite_prompts` (monitor `next_prompt`, 15 s cooldown, max 3 per attempt) | hybrid-harness | It gets base Dream-1 off the wrong object. Use `--no-rewrite` for a fine-tune, which only follows its recorded texts. |
+| `prompt_prefix` "no power" sentence | hybrid-harness | Dream refuses cable work without it. **Keep it only while it is literally true** (the station C notes say the strip is unplugged). |
+| `reset_first`, auto `/reset` after `SVD did not converge` (max 2) | hybrid-harness | Both grippers start open, and it recovers from Dream's IK error. Now goes through the motion checks (below). |
+| `requires_stage`, relative ball stages, `ARCHIVE_WAIT_S` 4 | hybrid-harness | Balls only after the tower passes. "One more ball" stages are counted from the start of that stage. |
+| Operator console | hybrid-harness | **Practice only.** Off under `--match`. |
+| LiveKit observer | codex/hill-climb-livekit | +10 for showing it to the referee. Read-only. Ported without that branch's plan rewrites. |
+| `--match` | new here | It makes the DQ rules something the harness checks, so nobody has to remember them on the day. |
+
+## Fixes the merge needed
+
+- **`/reset` bypassed the motion checks.** hybrid's `_reset_and_wait` posted `/reset` directly, so an operator stop
+  or a tripped guard didn't block it. `/reset` moves both arms and can't be cancelled once it starts. It now goes
+  through `_motion`, and it is skipped (`reset_skipped` event) while the live guard is armed.
+  `tests/test_final_harness.py` covers both.
+- Conflicts in `cli.py`, `plan.py` and `runner.py` were additive. `_PLAN_KEYS` is the union of both branches.
+
+## Left out
+
+| Idea | Where | Why not |
+|---|---|---|
+| `hybrid_v4_long.yaml` as the match plan | hybrid-harness | In the sim it lost 34 of 185 matches to an 8-ball and ended early in 134 of 185. Keep it for practice comparisons. |
+| Operator console, `--budget`, `monitor+manual` in a match | hybrid / main | Any human input after go is a DQ. `--match` refuses them. |
+| `hill_climb_full` / `hill_climb_sprint` rewrites | codex/hill-climb-livekit | `hill_climb_v4.yaml` and `hybrid_v4.yaml` already follow rubric v4. |
+| `decide.py` making decisions | start-pose-live-guard | No match evidence that it beats the plan order yet. Shadow mode first. |
+| The LiveKit branch's `required_cameras` plan field | codex/hill-climb-livekit | It needs that branch's plan and frames changes. The observer works without it. |
+
+## Stress results (virtual-clock sim, `experiments/stress_match.py` scenarios, 5 seeds each)
+
+37 scenarios × 5 seeds = 185 matches per config. Each scenario is a table the harness has to cope with: refusals,
+frozen or empty cameras, API outages, false "done" verdicts, an 8-ball near the bin, a knocky tower, and so on.
+"fast" means a judge call takes 4–6 s; otherwise 6.5–13 s (Haiku as measured). "final" is this merge with
+`--live-guard` and gripper sensors.
+
+| config | mean pts | lost to DQ | lost to 8-ball | mean balls |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| main, v4 plan | 60.5 | 4 | 0 | 0.00 |
+| hybrid, hybrid plan | 61.2 | 4 | 1 | 0.09 |
+| hybrid, hybrid plan, fast | 70.5 | 4 | **28** | 3.01 |
+| hybrid, long plan, fast | 63.2 | 5 | **34** | 3.38 |
+| **final, v4 plan, fast** | 66.6 | 6 | **0** | 2.37 |
+| **final, hybrid plan, fast** | **69.8** | 4 | **0** | 2.90 |
+
+- **The live guard is what makes a fast judge safe.** Without it, a fast judge reaches the balls and loses 28 of
+  185 matches to the 8-ball. With it, none are lost, at a cost of 0.7 points on average.
+- At Haiku's real latency, the live guard and the per-poll guard score the same (`guard_v4` and `guard_v4_live`
+  match row for row). Neither reaches the ball stage, so there is no 8-ball to stop.
+- The 4 DQs every config shares are all `reach_across` (see below).
+- **Open risk, v4 plan with a fast judge:** in 2 matches (`false_done_unplug` and `false_done_all`, same seed),
+  two false "cable out" verdicts landed seconds apart and passed `confirm: 2`. The robot then touched a block.
+  With two calls in flight, the confirming verdicts are close in time and can share the same error. A minimum gap
+  between confirming verdicts would close this. Not done yet.
+
+**This branch as committed**, at Haiku's real latency (6.5–13 s a call), with `--live-guard` and gripper sensors,
+on 8 of the scenarios (40 matches each):
+
+| scenario | main, v4 | this branch, v4 | hybrid, hybrid plan | this branch, hybrid plan |
+|---|---|---|---|---|
+| happy | 74.2 | 75.4 | 74.4 | 75.6 |
+| realistic | 47.6 | 57.6 | 60.0 | 59.2 |
+| realistic_faults | 44.6 | 46.4 | 45.6 | 44.0 |
+| calibrated_base | 35.4 | 32.6 | 49.4 | 54.0 |
+| calibrated_base_faults | 28.4 | 36.2 | 31.8 | 31.8 |
+| knocky_tower | 58.0 | 61.6 | 59.8 | 63.2 |
+| black_heavy_lagged | 89.2 | 90.4 | 90.0 | 87.8 |
+| unplug_never | 0 | 0 | 0 | 0 |
+
+No matches were lost in either config. The stitched, two-in-flight judge in the v4 plan is worth about +10 on
+`realistic`. On `calibrated_base` (base Dream-1's real unplug rate), the hybrid plan beats the v4 plan by about 20
+points.
+
+`hybrid_v4.yaml` needs `--skip cable_extra` in a match. Without it, the cable 3 and 4 stages take about 90 s, and
+the same 8 scenarios score 33.5 instead of 52.0.
+
+## Not solved by the harness
+
+- **Stuck cable / never unplugs:** 0–10 points in every config. Base Dream-1 unplugs about 1 try in 6
+  (TELEOP_PLAN). The sim's default of 0.8 per try is too optimistic, so treat the `calibrated_*` rows as the
+  realistic ones. A fine-tune for the unplug is worth more than any harness change.
+- **`reach_across`:** the robot touches a later-stage object early and gets a DQ in every config (4 of 5 seeds).
+  The guard sees it only after it happens.
+- **Judge latency:** with Haiku at 6.5–13 s a call, even two calls in flight rarely reach the ball stage. Balls
+  showed up only at 4–6 s a call.

+# Goal: hill-climb the harness to the highest consistent score
+
+Set by Darsh, 8 Nov 2026. Every change and every cloud/cluster run is judged against these targets.
+
+## Targets
+
+| # | Target | Measured as | Done when |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| 1 | **Dream calls at 2 Hz** (normal action frequency) | `get_action` requests per second of match, from the sim's trip log | median >= 2.0 Hz in valid matches, arms keep moving during inference (sim time / wall time >= 0.95) |
+| 2 | **Every task succeeds** | share of valid matches where each pass-bar stage passes (unplug, stack_2, stack_3, tower_pass) | 100% over the latest 100 valid matches |
+| 3 | **Zero losses** | DQs and 8-ball (illegal ball) losses | 0 in the latest 100 valid matches |
+| 4 | **Points as high as possible** | mean points per valid match, 95% bootstrap CI | each accepted change raises the mean with the CI lower bound above the previous mean |
+| 5 | **Infrastructure works** | valid-match rate (`match.json` `valid`), paid retries | 100% valid, 0 paid re-runs |
+
+## Rules for the hill climb
+
+- One change per arm. Same seeds across arms (paired comparison). At least 20 valid matches per arm before a
+  decision, more when the difference is small.
+- Keep a change only if it improves target 4 without hurting 2 or 3. Record every arm in `HILL_CLIMB_LOG.md`
+  (arm, change, n, mean points with CI, pass-bar rate, losses, Dream Hz, cost).
+- Darsh's operator decisions (`docs/decisions.pdf`, `plans/operator_notes.yaml`) are the starting policy:
+  technique per stage, error detection, 2 tries (3 for high points), push the white ball off first.
+- Fine-tuned Dream-1 gets its recorded texts byte for byte. Free-text prompting only on base Dream-1.
+- Review running jobs with their live snapshots (`live/scene.jpg`, `live/status.json`) and the console,
+  with screenshots, at least every 20 minutes while jobs run. Cancel a job the moment its matches look broken.
+- Never print or commit keys. Never let Batch re-run a paid match.
+
+## Levers, in order of expected value
+
+1. Correct sim setup: Dream's async pacing (`execution_horizon` 10 at 20 Hz = 2 Hz calls, physics keeps
+   running during inference, seam smoothing via `commanded`), as the Dream SDK documents (`dream/pacing.py`).
+2. Judge per decision: fastest accurate model for "is the stage done" vs "is a gripper holding an illegal
+   ball" (benchmarks in `hedged-calls/runs/bench_fair`), reasoning level per decision, hedged calls.
+3. Prompting: operator technique on/off, prompt levels 0-5, per stage.
+4. Plan: retry counts, stage order, white-ball push, stopping rules.
+5. Scale: cloud Batch (12 VMs, quota-limited) + home cluster (Inspiron, 4 containers).

@@ -249,6 +249,10 @@ guard:
 - It only sees what the monitor sees, at the monitor's pace: one answer per `check_every_s`, none during `settle_s` after a prompt change. It is a backstop, not a reflex. A team member still watches, ready to call "stop".
 - `max_blind_s: 20` also stops the run when a guarded stage has had no yes/no answer that long (Claude API down, bad frames).
 - **Local detector** (`--detect plans/table_colors.yaml`): `harness/detect.py` checks the wrist frames on every poll, with no API call, and counts an illegal ball (white, or black) between the fingers as a guard yes. It also draws labelled boxes (ball / cube / lying stick, by colour) on the frames the monitor sees. Dream's inputs and the prompts are unchanged. Re-tune the colours on the station's own cameras: `python -m harness.detect plans/table_colors.yaml frame.jpg --out boxed.jpg`.
+- **Live guard** (`--detect plans/table_colors.yaml --live-guard`): runs the same illegal-ball check in its own thread on live wrist frames from the station add-on (`GET /frame/wrist_l.jpg`, `/frame/wrist_r.jpg`, 30 fps, works idle or running) at 10 looks a second (`--live-guard-hz`). Three yes answers in a row on distinct frames (`--live-guard-frames`) POST `/stop` at once, without waiting for the next poll, and the match ends as for any guard trip. Without it the guard sees debug-view trips (one every ~3.4 s on the station) once per poll (3 s in v4), so a held ball could be judged up to ~6 s late. Both wrist views take ~76 ms to check on the laptop. At startup it probes `/frame` and warns if no wrist frame comes back (no add-on, other camera names); it keeps trying, so a camera that comes up later is used. The per-poll guard keeps running either way as a fallback. Yes answers count per camera and only on new frames, so a frozen camera reads as blind (`max_blind_s` stops the run) rather than as all clear. The guard never waits on the runner: it sends `/stop` the moment it trips. The runner checks for a trip (and an operator stop and the deadline) before every /start, /prompt and /home, and again after; a trip it finds afterwards may have been overtaken by its own command, so it sends `/stop` again. That leaves a window of milliseconds in which a /start or /prompt can follow the guard's /stop; the second /stop lands before Dream-1's first action chunk (~3 s for a fresh start on the station). `/home` can't be stopped once begun, so it is refused outright while the live guard is armed (ball stages only; homing only happens before unplug). Both wrist cameras are fetched in parallel with a 0.25 s timeout, so a dead camera doesn't slow the live one. The per-poll guard also counts only new frames now, so a cached debug-view trip can't confirm a yes or keep `max_blind_s` satisfied.
+- **Start pose** (`start_pose:` in the plan): before each stage and each retry the runner logs how far the arms' joints are from the starts recorded in the teleop data for that exact prompt (`start_pose` events in events.jsonl; reference `plans/start_poses.json`, rebuilt with `python tools/start_poses.py plans/start_poses.json <dataset dirs>`). Why: an unfamiliar start pose costs a VLA more than anything else measured in LIBERO-Plus (-60 points vs -16 for reworded instructions). On `home_before` stages (v4: `unplug_cable`) a start farther than `tolerance_rad` (0.35 rad on any joint) stops the run, homes the arms and starts fresh, once per stage, and only if the home pose is itself a recorded start for that prompt. `/home` can't be cancelled once started (the station refuses `/stop` while homing), so it is skipped with less than 30 s of match left, and an operator stop or guard trip during the stop holds the arms where they are instead of homing. Joint angles come from `GET /arms` `positions_rad` (new in `station_addons/sitecustomize.py`; the station must be restarted with it) or, while a run is going, from the newest trip. With neither, the check logs `no_state` and does nothing. Only unplug and put-down have recorded starts so far; stacking and balls log `no_reference`.
+- **Cable ends** (`--detect plans/table_colors.yaml --cable-ends models/cable_end.onnx`): `harness/cable_end.py` finds compact orange blobs (the anodised plug ferrules) and labels each crop USB-C end / USB-A end / neither with a small CNN (MobileNetV3-small, 6 MB ONNX, run by OpenCV; no torch at runtime). The monitor's frames get MAGENTA boxes on USB-C ends and AMBER boxes on USB-A ends (the end that goes into the power strip). Dream's inputs and the prompts are unchanged. Trained on 891 hand-labelled crops from the teleop videos and the table photos (`tools/cable_end/train.py`). Held out by video (7 Nov): 94% accuracy; at the 0.7 confidence it reports at, USB-C precision 0.70 / recall 0.82 and USB-A 0.96 / 0.84 (17 USB-C and 51 USB-A test crops, so treat these as rough). Blender renders of the cable (`tools/cable_end/render_cable.py`, random pose, lighting, lens, power strip, distractors) did not help: synthetic + real scored 92% and lower on the phone photos, so the shipped model is real-only. Try it on images: `python -m harness.cable_end models/cable_end.onnx frame.jpg --out-dir boxed/`.
+- **Decision equations, shadow mode** (`harness/decide.py`, optional `decide:` in the plan): the runner's done / fail rules written as weighted sums with thresholds, plus a planner that gives each stage the timeout worth the most points for the time left. On every look the runner logs a `decide` event: the done score, the fail score and its terms, and what the equations would decide. They never act; with no `decide:` section they decide exactly as the runner does (tests check this on 5,000 random look sequences), so any difference in the log comes from weights you set, e.g. `decide: {done: {theta: 1.0}}` or `decide: {fail: {combine: sum}}`. Stage success curves for the planner: `python tools/fit_stage_curves.py runs/ --out plans/stage_curves.json` (censored maximum likelihood over each stage's attempts). `plans/stage_curves_sim.json` is from Dream's YAM sim only (8 Nov): unplug p_max 0.64, no success before ~23 s, median 56 s; put-down and stacking never succeeded there, so fit station runs before trusting any timeout. Background: `docs/DECISION_MODEL.md`.
 
 ### Other stage fields added 8 Nov
 - `confirm: 1` on a stage overrides the plan's `confirm` (for stages where a false "done" costs nothing).

 **Rules:** an agent choosing stages live is not a human input. Still, check with the organizers before letting any model other than Dream-1 decide prompts in a match.
 
+## 6g. Zero-shot base Dream-1: `plans/hybrid_v4.yaml`
+
+Same match structure as `hill_climb_v4.yaml`, written for base Dream-1 instead of a fine-tune:
+
+- **Specific prompts** naming objects by colour and position ("pick up the pink cube ... onto the blue cube"),
+  and `tower_prompts` with `<piece>` and `<top>` so each stacking prompt names the next piece and the one below it.
+- **`rewrite_prompts: true`**: the monitor may answer `next_prompt`, and the runner sends it as the robot's next
+  instruction (at most `REWRITE_MAX` = 3 an attempt, `REWRITE_COOLDOWN_S` = 15 s apart). `prompt_prefix` is put back
+  on a rewrite when the stage's own prompt opens with it (Dream refuses cable work without the "no power" sentence).
+  `system_prompt` holds the station lessons the monitor follows. Logged as `prompt_rewrite`.
+- **`reset_first: true`**: `/reset` and wait for idle before the first stage, so both grippers start open.
+- After Dream's IK error (`SVD did not converge`) the restart is preceded by `/reset` + idle, at most
+  `AUTO_RESET_MAX` = 2 a run (`auto_reset` events).
+- Gripper sensors (`GET /arms`) overrule the monitor's `holding`: a gripper closed fully on nothing is reported as
+  holding nothing, whatever the images seem to show.
+- Frames come from the station's `/frame/<cam>.jpg?w=640` by default (~35 KB a camera, not the debug view's ~2 MB
+  round trips that slowed Dream to 2-6 s); `HARNESS_FRAMES=view` forces the debug view, which is also the fallback.
+- `--skip cable_` drops the cable section for stack + balls practice.
+- **Fine-tune labels must match the prompts we settle on** (stage prompts, tower prompts, logged rewrites).
+
+### `plans/hybrid_v4_long.yaml`: Dream-1's own prompt style
+
+Dream Labs' examples for Dream-1 are short imperatives that each carry a whole task ("Stack the cubes into a single
+tower", "Pick up all markers one by one and put into black pencil holder using both arms"; dreamlabs.ai/research/dream-1).
+This plan has 6 stages instead of 20: one short prompt each for a cable, the whole tower, the stick on top and all the
+balls, with long timeouts and the monitor stepping in only when the robot is on the wrong thing. Run both plans on the
+same table and keep what scores.
+
+### Talking to it while it runs (testing only)
+
+Type plain words and Enter at any time: "skip the next step", "why did it grab the pink one?", "lower the blocks
+more slowly next time". Claude reads each line with the run so far and the newest frames, answers in the terminal,
+and may end the current stage, skip later ones, send the robot a new instruction now, or keep a note that the monitor
+follows for the rest of the run. Notes are appended to `--lessons` (default `lessons/operator.md`) and read into the
+monitor's system prompt on later runs. Everything is in `events.jsonl` (`operator_input`, `operator_reply`,
+`operator_action`) and on the trace page (`python tools/trace_report.py`). `s` + Enter still stops. Any human input
+during a match is a DQ: run matches with `--match` (it turns the operator off; see [FINAL_HARNESS.md](FINAL_HARNESS.md)).
+
+## 6h. Observe a stage through LiveKit (`python -m harness.livekit_observer`)
+
+The rubric gives +10 for showing LiveKit use to the referee before the match. The observer subscribes to the station's
+camera tracks, never publishes, and isn't part of the runner: the runner still verifies its own stages from the
+station's frames.
+
+Install `livekit==1.2.1` (not in the base requirements). Set `LIVEKIT_URL` and `LIVEKIT_TOKEN` for the station's room,
+with a token that has `canSubscribe` on and `canPublish` and `canPublishData` off.
+
+```bash
+python -m harness.livekit_observer --publisher metal-station \
+  --track overhead=table_top --track wrist_l=left_wrist \
+  --plan plans/hill_climb_v4.yaml --stage tower_pass
+```
+
+Use the publisher identity and exact video track names from the room. With `--plan` and `--stage`, each sample also
+gets an advisory verdict from the Claude monitor (needs `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY`). Leave both out to capture camera evidence
+only. JPEGs and verdicts go to `runs_livekit/`. `receive_age_ms` is the time since this subscriber got the frame; the
+RTC timestamp is logged separately. A sample is taken only when every named track has a frame under `--max-age` (1 s)
+that wasn't in the previous sample.
+
 ## 7. Test in simulation (before the robot)
 
 `sim_station.py` serves the same API (8091) and debug view (8093), driven by Dream's MuJoCo YAM simulator (`dream_examples/yam_mujoco`). The harness can't tell it apart from a real station, and nothing physical moves, so `--live` is safe here. Note that the Hill Climb uses Metal arms, while the sim is YAM only. That makes it a test of the harness logic and prompts, not of the real task.

+# Hill climb log

+| arm | where | change | n valid | points (95% CI) | pass-bar | losses | Dream Hz | sim rate | notes |

+| C B + real judge | cloud | B, `--judge openrouter` Haiku 5.5 reasoning low | | | | | | | |
+| D B at 1 Hz | cluster | B with `--dream-pipeline 1` | | | | | | | |
+
+Decision after round 1: keep B over A only if the points CI says so without more losses; C vs B is the judge gap.

+# State-space form of the harness decisions (`harness/statespace.py`)
+
+Every quantity below is what the code computes. `K` = number of weight sets evaluated at once (1 in a match,
+thousands when hill-climbing over a logged run). Rows of `X` are weight sets; columns are state variables.
+
+## 1. Decision state, inputs, sizes
+
+State `x` (n = 9), one row per weight set, `X ∈ R^(K×9)`:
+
+| i | name | meaning |
+|---|---|---|
+| 0 | `done` | done evidence (counted "done" verdicts) |
+| 1 | `fail` | failing evidence (failing verdicts in a row) |
+| 2 | `stalled` | "stalled" verdicts in a row |
+| 3 | `guard_m` | guard "yes" from the monitor, in a row |
+| 4 | `guard_l` | guard "yes" from the local detector on new live frames, in a row |
+| 5 | `pmax` | best progress this attempt |
+| 6 | `since_rise` | seconds since progress last rose by 0.05 |
+| 7 | `t` | seconds into the attempt |
+| 8 | `since_done` | seconds since the last counted "done" (10^6 = none yet) |
+
+Input `u` (m = 12), one row vector per tick, `u ∈ R^(1×12)`:
+
+| j | name | value |
+|---|---|---|
+| 0 | `dt` | seconds since the last tick (0.05 at 20 Hz) |
+| 1 | `v_new` | 1 on the tick a real monitor verdict lands |
+| 2 | `v_done` | verdict says done |
+| 3 | `v_same` | ...on the same frames as the last done |
+| 4 | `v_failing` | verdict says no, or status failing, or progress fell 0.3 below its best |
+| 5 | `v_stalled` | status stalled |
+| 6 | `v_rise` | progress rose ≥ 0.05 above its best |
+| 7 | `v_guard_yes` | monitor guard yes |
+| 8 | `v_guard_no` | monitor guard no |
+| 9 | `l_guard_yes` | local detector: illegal ball between the fingers, new frame |
+| 10 | `l_guard_no` | local detector: clear, new frame |
+| 11 | `obs_release` | observer: gripper released at the goal (weight 0 by default) |
+
+## 2. The linear part (every tick)
+
+```
+X_{k+1} = (1_K ⊗ (1 − clip(u_k R, 0, 1))) ⊙ (X_k Aᵀ) + 1_K (u_k B)
+```
+
+`⊙` is element-wise, `1_K` a K×1 column of ones (the same input reaches every weight set).
+
+`A = I_9`: every state holds its value unless something resets it.
+
+`B ∈ R^(12×9)`: what each input adds (all other entries 0):
+
+```
+             done fail stalled guard_m guard_l pmax since_rise  t  since_done
+dt             0    0     0       0       0     0       1       1      1
+v_guard_yes    0    0     0       1       0     0       0       0      0
+l_guard_yes    0    0     0       0       1     0       0       0      0
+```
+
+`R ∈ R^(12×9)`: which states an input resets to 0 (all other entries 0):
+
+```
+             done fail stalled guard_m guard_l pmax since_rise  t  since_done
+v_rise         0    0     0       0       0     0       1       0      0
+v_guard_no     0    0     0       1       0     0       0       0      0
+l_guard_no     0    0     0       0       1     0       0       0      0
+```
+
+So the clocks integrate `dt`, and each guard counter counts "yes" answers in a row and resets on a "no".
+
+## 3. The verdict jump map (only on ticks with `v_new = 1`)
+
+The counters for "done" and "failing" switch on which kind of verdict landed, so this part is a switched
+(hybrid) system: one masked update per weight set. Per-weight-set vectors: confirm `c ∈ R^K`, done window
+`w ∈ R^K` (15 s), minimum gap `g ∈ R^K` (3 s). Let `s = X[:, since_done]` before the update.
+
+```
+stalled ← (stalled_prev + 1) · v_stalled            (a non-stalled verdict resets it)
+
+if v_same:                   nothing                (the same frames judged twice are one look)
+elif v_done:
+    ok     = s ≥ g                                   (closer than g: the same look, not a second one)
+    rows ok:  fail ← 0,  done ← done + 1,  since_done ← 0
+    pass   = ok ∧ ( s ≤ w  ∨  done ≥ c )             (a second done inside the window, or c in a row)
+elif v_failing:
+    out    = s > w                                   (one flicker right after a done does not undo it)
+    rows out: done ← 0,  fail ← fail + 1
+else:                                                (a verdict with no answer)
+    done ← 0,  fail ← 0
+```
+
+`pmax` and `v_rise` come from the feature step: `v_rise = 1[p ≥ pmax + 0.05]`, `pmax ← max(pmax, p)`.
+
+## 4. Observer coupling (off by default)
+
+```
+done ← done + w_goal · 1[ẑ_at_goal ≥ γ] · dt + w_release · obs_release
+pass ← pass ∨ (done ≥ c)
+```
+
+Defaults `w_goal = w_release = 0`, so the local perception cannot pass a stage until the hill-climb shows that it
+should.
+
+## 5. Decisions (read out every tick, per weight set)
+
+```
+pass    = from §3 / §4
+fail    = (fail ≥ c_f) ∧ (t ≥ φ·T  ∨  ¬monitor)               c_f = 2, φ = 0.5, T = stage timeout
+stall   = no_done ∧ ( stalled ≥ c_s  ∨  (since_rise ≥ T_s ∧ 0 < pmax < 0.95) )      c_s = 2, only if the stage has stall_s
+guard   = (guard_m ≥ c_gm) ∨ (guard_l ≥ c_gl)                c_gm = plan guard confirm (1), c_gl = 3 live frames
+timeout = t ≥ T
+```
+
+`no_done` = no counted done this attempt (`since_done = 10^6`).
+
+## 6. Observer (state estimate)
+
+Estimated task state `ẑ ∈ [0,1]^5` = (progress, holding, at_goal, illegal_grip, cable_out).
+
+```
+predict (every tick):   ẑ ← ½·1 + ρ (ẑ − ½·1)          (progress is held, not relaxed)          ρ = 0.98
+fast update (local):    ẑ ← ẑ + L_f · H_f (y_f − ẑ)                                              L_f = 0.25
+slow update (Claude):   ẑ ← ẑ + L_s · H_s (y_s − ẑ)                                              L_s = 0.80
+```
+
+`H` is a 0/1 diagonal mask of the components that were measured on that tick. Written the usual way:
+`ẑ⁺ = (I − L H) ẑ⁻ + L H y`, a fixed-gain (steady-state Kalman / Luenberger) observer. The fast path pulls the
+estimate a quarter of the way to each local measurement; a Claude verdict pulls it 80% of the way, so the slower
+but more reliable source wins when they disagree. All values are clipped to [0, 1].
+
+## 7. As a neural network
+
+Sections 2–3 are one gated recurrent cell:
+
+```
+h_{k+1} = (1 − σ_hard(u_k R)) ⊙ (h_k Aᵀ) + u_k B + J(h_k, u_k)
+```
+
+with `σ_hard = clip(·, 0, 1)` and `J` the jump map of §3 (piecewise-linear, so it is exactly expressible with ReLU
+and step units). `StateSpace.export(path)` saves `A`, `B`, `R` and the per-weight-set thresholds; with logged sim
+runs, the thresholds and gains can be fitted instead of hand-set.
+
+## 8. Defaults (= today's runner)
+
+| weight | value | runner constant |
+|---|---|---|
+| `confirm` c | 2 | plan `confirm` |
+| `window_s` w | 15 s | `DONE_WINDOW_S` |
+| `min_gap_s` g | 3 s | plan `confirm_gap_s` (new) |
+| `fail_confirm` c_f | 2 | plan `confirm` |
+| `fail_min_fraction` φ | 0.5 | `FAIL_MIN_FRACTION` |
+| `stall_confirm` c_s | 2 | plan `confirm` |
+| `guard_confirm` c_gm | 1 | plan guard `confirm` |
+| `live_guard_frames` c_gl | 3 | `--live-guard-frames` |
+| `l_fast`, `l_slow`, `decay` | 0.25, 0.8, 0.98 | new (observer) |
+| `w_goal`, `w_release`, `goal_conf` γ | 0, 0, 0.9 | new (off) |
+
+With these defaults the model makes the same pass decision as `harness/decide.py`'s reference rule on 3,000
+random verdict sequences (`tests/test_statespace.py`), and one tick over 1,000 weight sets costs well under 5 ms.

+# Cost and sizing
+
+Measured on 2026-11-08 with the existing Windows sibling venv. No Dream key,
+Claude key, cloud job or cloud build was used. The command was:
+
+```powershell
+& ..\hill_climb_harness\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m cloud.run --policy scripted --out cloud/evidence/smoke
+```
+
+The full result is in `evidence/smoke-match.json`. The match used the production
+Runner, v4 plan, live image guard, measured grippers and delayed oracle.
+
+| Measurement | Value |
+|---|---:|
+| Total process wall time, including setup and shutdown | 301.156 s |
+| Match clock | 300.000 s |
+| Process CPU time | 136.531 s |
+| Consumed vCPU-minutes per match | 2.276 |
+| Mean CPU use, one core = 100% | 45.3% |
+| Peak process RSS, sampled every 200 ms | 689.34 MiB |
+| Physics time at scoring | 194.05 s |
+| Oracle calls | 43 |
+| Result | 0 points, no DQ, no black ball in bin |
+
+The sinusoidal scripted policy never unplugs a cable, so the first stage used
+the whole 295-second harness budget, then the match held through 300 seconds.
+This is a performance and integration check, not a policy-quality result.
+CPU time includes all process threads and the guard. Windows offscreen OpenGL
+can use the local graphics driver; this does not measure Linux OSMesa CPU use.
+The camera render/step overhead also prevented 1:1 physics time. Compare both
+wall and physics time in the pilot before interpreting scores.
+
+## Selected shape
+
+Use `e2-highcpu-2` Spot, two vCPUs and 2 GiB, with **N = 1 concurrent match per
+VM**. The task requests 2,000 milliCPU and 1,280 MiB. Its measured RSS leaves
+about 590 MiB inside that task allocation, and the VM leaves 768 MiB for the
+host. Two matches on this shape would consume the measured RAM plus the OS
+with little room for software rendering, and share the two renderer threads.
+
+This is the smallest full-CPU E2 candidate that fits the local measurement.
+It is the expected cheapest suitable predefined E2 shape, subject to the Linux
+pilot. Shared-core E2 shapes have sustained CPU limits and are unsuitable for
+assuming constant 20 Hz control. Larger shapes have no demonstrated packing
+benefit. `--matches-per-task K` runs K matches sequentially to amortize VM and
+image startup; it does not pack K concurrent matches on a VM.
+
+The official [E2 shape table](https://docs.cloud.google.com/compute/docs/general-purpose-machines)
+lists the shape and [Spot pricing](https://cloud.google.com/spot-vms/pricing)
+explains variable regional prices. Use **$0.030 per VM-hour in us-central1 as a
+planning assumption**, not a verified live quote. The official pricing page's
+retrieved E2 standard-2 comparator was $0.040212/hour; a direct current price for
+highcpu-2 could not be established from the retrieved table. Confirm the actual
+highcpu-2 quote in the calculator before running. The scripts do not query billing.
+
+Allocated CPU is 10 vCPU-minutes per 300-second match, regardless of the 2.276
+CPU-minutes actually consumed. Formula: matches × VM seconds / 3,600 × $0.030.
+
+| Matches | Match-only VM hours | VM cost at 300 s | VM cost with 60 s startup per task | Dream realtime usage |
+|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
+| 100 | 8.333 | $0.25 | $0.30 | 500 min / 8.333 h |
+| 1,000 | 83.333 | $2.50 | $3.00 | 5,000 min / 83.333 h |
+| 5,000 | 416.667 | $12.50 | $15.00 | 25,000 min / 416.667 h |
+
+The ten-match pilot is $0.025 match-only, or $0.030 with that startup allowance.
+At parallelism eight, 1,000 matches take at least 10.42 hours of match time,
+plus startup and scheduling. Eight concurrent tasks means up to eight Dream
+sessions and, in API judge mode, up to sixteen monitor calls in flight.
+
+Dream usage above is the requested five-minute-per-match envelope. Actual billed
+inference depends on Dream's session billing, starts/stops and retries. It is not
+priced here because the account's rate is unknown. A match can create multiple
+Dream sessions during recovery. Judge API mode additionally bills Claude image
+and text tokens; oracle mode has no Claude API charge. The measured oracle count
+was 43 per match, not a guaranteed count or API token estimate.
+
+Build time, image storage, 30 GB boot disks, source staging, GCS results, logs,
+network traffic, tax and interrupted/retried matches are excluded. Batch allows
+two task retries, so budget for repeated Dream usage as well as VM time. A monthly
+budget alert is not a hard spending cap. For other rates or startup times, scale
+the table using the formula above. Do not scale to thousands until the pilot's
+Linux RSS, camera freshness, physics/wall ratio and Dream concurrency are checked.

+# Hill Climb on Google Cloud Batch
+
+This directory runs real Dream-1 sessions against the local MuJoCo relay scene.
+The default matrix has 1,000 matches, 500 seeds each for `hill_climb_v4` and
+`hybrid_v4 --skip cable_extra`. Both use `--match`, the sim colour detector and
+`--live-guard`. No cloud resources were created or changed during development.
+
+Files:
+
+- `run.py` wires the existing Runner, station, light frames, detector, live guard,
+  start-pose gate and read-only lessons together without an operator console.
+- `judge.py` uses the existing monitor's two-call asynchronous scheduler with
+  referee answers captured at request time and uniform 6.5–13 s delays. Unknown
+  stage questions stay unknown. Oracle mode does not invent prompt rewrites.
+  `--judge api` uses the existing Claude monitor and API transport, never the
+  subscription CLI. Oracle and API therefore differ in perception and rewrites,
+  not just latency. Do not treat their scores as interchangeable.
+- `entrypoint.py` starts a fresh process and sim for each match, writes JSON and
+  logs beneath `/out`, uploads them, then writes a completion marker. Each Spot
+  attempt uses a unique prefix. Only complete attempts enter collection.
+- `setup.sh`, `build.sh`, `submit.sh` call `control.py`; all cloud operations use
+  the user's `gcloud`. They default to offline dry-run and require `--execute`
+  for changes. `collect.py` likewise defaults to a printed plan.
+- `Dockerfile` extends the cluster container with CPU OSMesa, the baked checkout,
+  pinned Dream 1.0.37 / MuJoCo 4.0.0 and gcloud. Cloud Build runs the original
+  example and relay build/step/render/API/detector checks before publishing.
+- `COST.md`, `RESEARCH.md` and `evidence/` record measurements and source checks.
+
+The two real-table YAML plans contain incorrect ball instructions for this sim.
+`run.py` adapts those texts in memory, including monitor questions and lessons.
+White golf balls are targets; black 8-balls are forbidden. It records the entire
+effective plan, its hash, original plan hash, versions, selected skips and colour
+adaptation in the manifest. Original match plans remain available for the real
+table. The sim layout does not currently support scene randomisation. Seeds
+control oracle delay samples and label independent Dream repetitions; the Dream
+API does not expose a deterministic policy seed through this example.
+
+## Run commands in order
+
+Run from this checkout in Git Bash. Use the supplied venv without changing it:
+
+```bash
+export PYTHON='../hill_climb_harness/.venv/Scripts/python.exe'
+bash -n cloud/setup.sh cloud/build.sh cloud/submit.sh
+bash cloud/setup.sh --dry-run
+bash cloud/build.sh --tag cloud-v1 --dry-run
+bash cloud/submit.sh --tag cloud-v1 --job hill-climb-pilot-v1 --pilot --parallelism 8 --dry-run
+```
+
+The same scripts work in Cloud Shell with Python 3 and PyYAML installed. They do
+not need local Docker. The Windows machine currently has Git Bash but no gcloud
+on PATH. Use a newly built, unique tag after any code or matrix change.
+
+After the user actions below are complete, these commands create resources and
+spend money. They are supplied for the user's execution, not run in this session:
+
+```bash
+gcloud auth login
+bash cloud/setup.sh --execute
+bash cloud/build.sh --tag cloud-v1 --execute
+bash cloud/submit.sh --tag cloud-v1 --job hill-climb-pilot-v1 --pilot --parallelism 8 --execute
+gcloud batch jobs describe hill-climb-pilot-v1 --location us-central1 --project hill-climb-sims
+"$PYTHON" -m cloud.collect --prefix gs://hill-climb-sims-results/runs/hill-climb-pilot-v1 --execute
+```
+
+Inspect the pilot's task errors, manifests, metrics and referee outcomes. A normal
+295-second harness timeout is a valid completed match. An interruption, init
+failure or failed upload is not. Check Linux peak RSS fits the 1,280 MiB task
+request, cameras stay under the runner's 2 s freshness limit, and measure physics
+time relative to 300 s wall time. Verify Dream accepts eight concurrent sessions
+and that recoveries are not dominated by rate limits. If it does not, submit a
+new pilot job with lower `--parallelism`. No script automatically raises quotas
+or switches away from Spot. `--machine-type e2-standard-2` supplies a larger-memory
+fallback for a new pilot if OSMesa exceeds the selected shape's RAM.
+
+Then submit and collect the full matrix:
+
+```bash
+bash cloud/submit.sh --tag cloud-v1 --job hill-climb-full-v1 --parallelism 8 --execute
+"$PYTHON" -m cloud.collect --prefix gs://hill-climb-sims-results/runs/hill-climb-full-v1 --local cloud/generated/full --out cloud/generated/full.jsonl --execute
+```
+
+For a real Claude judge, upload the Anthropic secret first, then use a new job
+name and `--judge api`. Optional `--matches-per-task 3` groups sequential matches.
+Pilot mode always selects ten matches, interleaved across configurations.
+For a standalone container's sequential seed range, use entrypoint arguments
+`--config hybrid_v4 --seed-start 100 --matches 5 --judge oracle`, with
+`OUTPUT_PREFIX` and runtime credentials supplied by the execution environment.
+
+Setup enables APIs and ensures existing named resources. IAM additions are
+idempotent. It never rotates an existing secret, edits existing budget amounts,
+deletes resources or changes the global gcloud project setting. Build skips an
+existing image tag. Submit skips an existing job with the same manifest and
+rejects a reused name with different inputs. The matrix hash is checked inside
+the image to reject a stale baked matrix. Collection downloads using gcloud,
+ignores unmarked partial attempts, deduplicates completed Spot attempts by
+job/config/seed, and rewrites JSONL deterministically. Retried tasks may still
+incur repeated inference charges; deduplication only prevents duplicate rows.
+
+Worker identity has project-level Batch agent reporting and log writing,
+repository-scoped image reading, bucket-scoped object creation, and access only
+to the two secret containers. It cannot delete or overwrite results. The separate
+builder identity can write the repository, read build-source objects and write
+logs; it cannot read either secret. No key file is staged or copied into the
+container. The source upload uses `gcloudignore` as an explicit allowlist.
+Collection uses the user's identity, which needs output object read permission.
+
+## USER ACTION NEEDED steps
+
+Every cloud command failure stops with `USER ACTION NEEDED:`. Resolve the named
+step in your own terminal or console and rerun the same command.
+
+1. Install Google Cloud CLI, make it available to Git Bash, and run `gcloud auth
+   login` using the personal Gmail that owns billing account
+   `<BILLING_ACCOUNT_ID>`, "My Billing Account".
+2. Create project `hill-climb-sims` in the console if it is absent. Link that
+   billing account. Setup intentionally refuses to create the project or link
+   billing automatically.
+3. Accept any required API terms. Ensure your user can enable APIs, administer
+   project/resource IAM, create service accounts and secrets, submit Cloud Build
+   and Batch jobs, and create project-filtered budgets on that billing account.
+4. Save Dream's setup block locally in `~/.dreamlabs/credentials`. Do not paste
+   it into chat. Setup reads only its `DREAM_API_KEY` entry, passes the value to
+   your gcloud's stdin, and never prints it or stores a second local copy. If an
+   enabled secret version already exists, setup does not read the local file.
+5. For API judge mode only, upload the Anthropic key to `anthropic-api-key` in
+   Secret Manager using your own console. Setup creates the secret container but
+   leaves it empty. Oracle jobs never request its value.
+6. If the global bucket name is already owned elsewhere, choose an available
+   `--bucket NAME` and pass it consistently to setup, build, submit and collection.
+7. Confirm the actual regional Spot price. If submission reports quota or
+   capacity problems, request sufficient regional Spot/preemptible CPU quota
+   and Batch quota. Default parallelism eight needs up to sixteen vCPUs. Check
+   Artifact Registry and Cloud Build quotas too. The default VPC must provide
+   outbound HTTPS; restore a permitted network configuration if your project has
+   no default network or forbids public egress.
+8. Confirm Dream account concurrency and usage limits using the ten-match pilot.
+   Check Cloud Build's Linux render gate and pilot resource/timing metrics before
+   submitting hundreds of matches. Verify budget-alert recipients on the billing
+   account. The $50 budget produces alerts only; it does not stop jobs.
+
+## Local validation
+
+```powershell
+& ..\hill_climb_harness\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m pytest -q
+& ..\hill_climb_harness\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m cloud.validate
+& ..\hill_climb_harness\.venv\Scripts\python.exe -m cloud.run --policy scripted --out cloud/generated/smoke
+```
+
+Local rendering is offscreen with no native viewer. The committed three PNGs
+come from the real relay scene after forty physics steps. The overhead detector
+finds four visible legal white balls and both black balls; other golf balls are
+occluded or merged into adjacent white geometry in that view. The wrist guard
+uses only forbidden balls inside the grip ROI. This colour detector remains a
+heuristic, not proof that an arbitrary future pose cannot hide an 8-ball.
+
+Collector fields match stress output: `scenario, seed, points, lost, dq,
+black_in_bin, out, plugged, tower, balls, t_end, reason, stages, label`. `tower`
+is metres, `dq` is a list, and `stages` contains name/ok/attempt tuples serialized
+as arrays. Milestone fields add `stage1_at`, `stage2_at` and `first_touch`, in
+referee physics seconds. `t_end` remains harness elapsed seconds so existing
+early-finish plots retain their meaning; `match_wall_s` records the full horizon.

+# Research and validation record
+
+Research date: 2026-11-08. No cloud resources were queried or changed.
+
+Local baseline: `cluster/Dockerfile`, `cluster/run_matches.sh`, `FINAL_HARNESS.md`,
+`HARNESS_GUIDE.md`, `experiments/sim_match.py`, the referee, station add-on,
+frame client, runner and monitor were inspected. No capsule configuration exists.
+The sibling venv has dreamlabs 1.0.37, MuJoCo 4.0.0, NumPy 2.2.6 and Pillow 13.0.0.
+Its installed `dream_examples/yam_mujoco/yam_env.py` is the reference implementation.
+`YamEnv.state()` returns seven measured values per arm, with normalized opening at
+index 6. Finger actuator controls use meters, 0.0475 per finger at full opening.
+`images()` returns top/left/right RGB arrays. Only the simulation thread may read
+MuJoCo state or render; HTTP handlers must use snapshots.
+
+Supported workflows and primary sources:
+
+- [Dream package 1.0.37](https://pypi.org/project/dreamlabs/1.0.37/).
+- [MuJoCo changelog](https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/changelog.html),
+  4.0.0 released November 6. Pin only after build, step and offscreen-render checks.
+- [Batch jobs](https://docs.cloud.google.com/batch/docs/create-run-basic-job):
+  taskCount, parallelism, container runnable and allocationPolicy.
+- [Batch secrets](https://docs.cloud.google.com/batch/docs/create-run-job-secret-manager):
+  environment.secretVariables maps names to secret version resource paths.
+- [Custom job account](https://docs.cloud.google.com/batch/docs/create-run-job-custom-service-account):
+  dedicated VM identity, resource-scoped secret, image and output permissions.
+- [Budget CLI](https://docs.cloud.google.com/sdk/gcloud/reference/billing/budgets/create):
+  project-filtered monthly amount and threshold rules, default billing recipients.
+- [Spot pricing](https://cloud.google.com/products/compute/pricing): variable pricing,
+  interruption possible; prices are assumptions until verified for the chosen region.
+
+Plausible failure causes: missing GL libraries, incompatible model/compiler API,
+wrong camera aliases, stale render snapshots, real-table colour roles applied to
+the white sim balls, synchronous oracle blocking health checks, real-table prompts
+contradicting sim colours, missing ADC/secret permissions, Spot quota or capacity.
+
+First diagnostic: build the installed example with the relay-table patch, step
+without policy/network, save three offscreen frames and inspect state and pixel
+statistics. Success requires finite state, nonuniform images and six white/two
+black ball geoms. Then test HTTP snapshots, asynchronous judge scheduling and job
+JSON locally. Only then run one 300-second scripted match and measure process CPU,
+wall time and peak RSS. Linux OSMesa is separately checked during Cloud Build.
+No Dream calls, cloud build, job submission or downstream training in this session.
+
+Results: the installed original example and the patched relay scene both build,
+step and render with MuJoCo 4.0.0. All three offscreen camera images have nonzero
+contrast. `/frame` and `/arms` integration checks passed. The scripted full match
+ran for 300 wall seconds, with 301.156 s total wall, 136.531 s CPU and 689.34 MiB
+sampled peak RSS. The referee advanced 194.05 physics seconds. See COST.md.
+
+A supplemental Linux/Python 3.12 wheel-only dependency dry-run did not finish:
+the sibling venv has no pip, so the system pip was used without installation;
+its first target-tag list omitted JAX's manylinux_2_27 wheel. Official PyPI's
+[JAX 0.7.1 files](https://pypi.org/project/jaxlib/0.7.1/#files) established the tag.
+With all supported glibc tags, the next check reached Dream's source-only
+`portal==3.7.3`. Its [official release](https://pypi.org/project/portal/3.7.3/#files)
+contains only a source archive. The known-good installed Portal contains Python
+files and no native extensions, depending on cloudpickle/msgpack/numpy/psutil.
+The container uses ordinary pip, which permits this source package; no pins were
+changed to force a wheel-only check through. Linux installation and OSMesa remain
+Cloud Build gates, unexecuted under the brief's cloud-change prohibition.

+"""Run the cloud matches on the home cluster too (the Inspiron head node), with the same image and entrypoint.
+
+    python -m cloud.cluster push                    # ship HEAD's sources, build hill-climb-sim:<rev> on the node
+    python -m cloud.cluster keys                    # Secret Manager -> ~/.hill-climb/keys.env (mode 600), never printed
+    python -m cloud.cluster run --job J --config hybrid_v4 --seed-start 0 --matches 8 --containers 4 -- <entrypoint args>
+    python -m cloud.cluster status --job J
+    python -m cloud.cluster pull --job J            # results -> cloud/generated/cluster/<J>/ for cloud.collect
+    python -m cloud.cluster stop --job J
+
+Each container runs `cloud.entrypoint --config C --seed-start S --matches M`, sequential matches on its own seed
+range, 2 CPUs and 2 GB like a Batch task, writing to the cluster's shared folder (OUTPUT_PREFIX is a local path, so
+no cloud credentials are needed on the node). Live snapshots land there too, under each match's live/.
+"""
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import subprocess
+import sys
+from pathlib import Path
+
+ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
+HOST = "inspiron"
+BASE = "/cluster/shared/dev/darsh/projects/hill_climb_sims"
+SOURCES = ["cloud", "harness", "sim_scenes", "plans", "lessons", "experiments/sim_match.py", "sim_station.py"]
+
+
+def ssh(command: str, *, stdin: bytes | None = None, check: bool = True) -> str:
+    result = subprocess.run(["ssh", HOST, command], input=stdin, capture_output=True)
+    if check and result.returncode:
+        raise SystemExit(f"ssh {HOST} failed ({result.returncode}): {result.stderr.decode(errors='replace')[-600:]}")
+    return result.stdout.decode(errors="replace")
+
+
+def revision() -> str:
+    return subprocess.run(["git", "rev-parse", "--short", "HEAD"], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True,
+                          text=True, check=True).stdout.strip()
+
+
+def push(_args) -> None:
+    rev = revision()
+    src = f"{BASE}/src-{rev}"
+    archive = subprocess.run(["git", "archive", "--format=tar", "HEAD", *SOURCES], cwd=ROOT, capture_output=True,
+                             check=True).stdout
+    # Readable by everyone: the containers run as the node's user, and COPY keeps the files' modes.
+    ssh(f"mkdir -p {src} && tar -x -C {src} && chmod -R a+rX {src}", stdin=archive)
+    print(f"sources at {HOST}:{src}; building hill-climb-sim:{rev} (first build pulls the base images)", flush=True)
+    out = ssh(f"cd {src} && docker build -q -f cloud/Dockerfile --build-arg SOURCE_REVISION={rev} "
+              f"-t hill-climb-sim:{rev} -t hill-climb-sim:latest .")
+    print(out.strip()[-200:])
+
+
+def keys(args) -> None:
+    import shutil
+
+    gcloud = shutil.which(args.gcloud) or args.gcloud      # gcloud.cmd on Windows: run directly, no shell quoting
+    lines = []
+    for env, secret in (("DREAM_API_KEY", "dream-api-key"), ("OPENROUTER_API_KEY", "openrouter-api-key")):
+        value = subprocess.run([gcloud, "secrets", "versions", "access", "latest", f"--secret={secret}",
+                                f"--project={args.project}"], capture_output=True, text=True)
+        if value.returncode or not value.stdout.strip():
+            raise SystemExit(f"could not read secret {secret} (is gcloud logged in?)")
+        lines.append(f"{env}={value.stdout.strip()}")
+    ssh("umask 077 && mkdir -p ~/.hill-climb && cat > ~/.hill-climb/keys.env", stdin=("\n".join(lines) + "\n").encode())
+    print(f"keys written to {HOST}:~/.hill-climb/keys.env (mode 600); not printed")
+
+
+def run(args) -> None:
+    out = f"{BASE}/results/{args.job}"
+    per = -(-args.matches // args.containers)
+    starts = [(args.seed_start + i * per, min(per, args.matches - i * per)) for i in range(args.containers)
+              if args.matches - i * per > 0]
+    extra = " ".join(args.entrypoint_args)
+    lines = [f"mkdir -p {out}"]
+    for i, (start, n) in enumerate(starts):
+        name = f"hc-{args.job}-{i}"
+        lines.append(f"docker run -d --name {name} --cpus 2 --memory 2g --env-file ~/.hill-climb/keys.env "
+                     f"--user $(id -u):$(id -g) -e HOME=/tmp "
+                     # The node's Intel GPU through EGL: ~20 ms per capture instead of ~320 ms in software.
+                     f"--device /dev/dri --group-add $(getent group render | cut -d: -f3) "
+                     f"--group-add $(getent group video | cut -d: -f3) -e MUJOCO_GL=egl -e PYOPENGL_PLATFORM=egl "
+                     f"-e OUTPUT_PREFIX=/results/{args.job} -e RUN_ID={args.job} -v {BASE}/results:/results "
+                     f"--label hill-climb-job={args.job} hill-climb-sim:{args.tag} "
+                     f"--config {args.config} --seed-start {start} --matches {n} --out /tmp/out {extra}")
+    print(ssh(" && ".join(lines)).strip())
+    print(f"{len(starts)} containers on {HOST}; results in {out}")
+
+
+def status(args) -> None:
+    print(ssh(f"docker ps -a --filter label=hill-climb-job={args.job} --format '{{{{.Names}}}} {{{{.Status}}}}'; "
+              f"echo matches: $(find {BASE}/results/{args.job} -name match.json 2>/dev/null | wc -l) "
+              f"complete: $(find {BASE}/results/{args.job} -name complete.json 2>/dev/null | wc -l)", check=False))
+
+
+def pull(args) -> None:
+    target = ROOT / "cloud/generated/cluster" / args.job
+    target.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+    data = subprocess.run(["ssh", HOST, f"tar -c -C {BASE}/results {args.job}"], capture_output=True, check=True).stdout
+    subprocess.run(["tar", "-x", "-C", str(target.parent)], input=data, check=True)
+    print(f"results in {target}")
+
+
+def stop(args) -> None:
+    print(ssh(f"docker rm -f $(docker ps -aq --filter label=hill-climb-job={args.job}) 2>/dev/null; true",
+              check=False).strip() or "nothing to stop")
+
+
+def main() -> None:
+    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
+    p.add_argument("op", choices=["push", "keys", "run", "status", "pull", "stop"])
+    p.add_argument("--job")
+    p.add_argument("--config", default="hybrid_v4")
+    p.add_argument("--seed-start", type=int, default=0)
+    p.add_argument("--matches", type=int, default=4)
+    p.add_argument("--containers", type=int, default=4)
+    p.add_argument("--tag", default="latest")
+    p.add_argument("--project", default="hill-climb-sims")
+    p.add_argument("--gcloud", default="gcloud")
+    argv = sys.argv[1:]
+    passthrough = argv[argv.index("--") + 1:] if "--" in argv else []     # after --: for cloud.entrypoint
+    args = p.parse_args(argv[:argv.index("--")] if "--" in argv else argv)
+    args.entrypoint_args = passthrough
+    if args.op in ("run", "status", "pull", "stop") and not args.job:
+        p.error("--job is required")
+    globals()[args.op](args)
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    main()
diff --git a/cloud/collect.py b/cloud/collect.py
new file mode 100644

+++ b/cloud/collect.py

+"""Download completed results using gcloud and emit stress-compatible JSONL."""
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import json
+import shutil
+import subprocess
+from pathlib import Path
+
+
+def normalize(match):
+    ref, harness = match["referee"], match["harness"]
+    return {"scenario": match["scenario"], "seed": match["seed"], "label": match["label"],
+            "points": ref["points"], "lost": ref["loses"], "dq": [ref["dq"]] if ref["dq"] else [],
+            "black_in_bin": ref["eight_in_bin"], "out": ref["unplugged"], "plugged": ref["seated"],
+            "tower": ref["tower_in"] * 0.0254, "balls": ref["golf_in_bin"],
+            "t_end": round(harness["seconds"], 1), "reason": harness["reason"],
+            "stages": [(s["name"], s["ok"], s["attempts"]) for s in match["stages"]],
+            "harness_ok": harness["ok"], "stage1_at": ref["stage1_at"], "stage2_at": ref["stage2_at"],
+            "first_touch": ref["first_touch"], "match_wall_s": match["match_wall_s"],
+            "valid": match.get("valid", True), "invalid_because": match.get("invalid_because", []),
+            "health": match.get("health", {}), "sim_t": match.get("sim_t", ref.get("t")),
+            "sim_rate": match.get("sim_rate"),
+            **{k: match.get(k) for k in ("judge_models", "judge_mode", "judge_reasoning", "operator_notes",
+                                         "prompt_technique", "prompt_level", "dream_hedge", "dream")},
+            "policy": match["policy"], "judge": match["judge"], "metrics": match["metrics"],
+            "monitor_calls": match["monitor_calls"], "versions": match["versions"],
+            "source_revision": match.get("source_revision", "unknown"),
+            "effective_plan_sha256": match["effective_plan_sha256"]}
+
+
+def collect(source):
+    rows = {}
+    for marker in sorted(Path(source).rglob("complete.json")):
+        match_path = marker.with_name("match.json")
+        if not match_path.exists():
+            raise ValueError(f"completion marker without match.json: {marker}")
+        row = normalize(json.loads(match_path.read_text(encoding="utf-8")))
+        meta = json.loads(marker.read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+        identity = (meta["run_id"], row["label"], row["seed"])
+        # Spot retries are separate attempts. Keep one complete result per job/config/seed.
+        if identity in rows:
+            if rows[identity]["effective_plan_sha256"] != row["effective_plan_sha256"]:
+                raise ValueError(f"conflicting plans for {identity}")
+            print(f"Duplicate completed attempt for {identity}; keeping first sorted attempt.")
+            continue
+        row["run_id"], row["attempt"] = meta["run_id"], meta["attempt"]
+        rows[identity] = row
+    return list(rows.values())
+
+
+def main():
+    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
+    p.add_argument("--prefix", help="gs://bucket/runs/job")
+    p.add_argument("--local", type=Path, default=Path("cloud/generated/downloads"))
+    p.add_argument("--out", type=Path, default=Path("cloud/generated/matches.jsonl"))
+    p.add_argument("--execute", action="store_true", help="download results and write JSONL")
+    p.add_argument("--dry-run", action="store_true")
+    args = p.parse_args()
+    print(f"Will download {args.prefix or '(local only)'} into {args.local} and write {args.out}.")
+    if not args.execute or args.dry_run:
+        return
+    if args.prefix:
+        if not args.prefix.startswith("gs://"):
+            p.error("prefix must start with gs://")
+        exe = shutil.which("gcloud") or shutil.which("gcloud.cmd")
+        if not exe:
+            raise SystemExit("USER ACTION NEEDED: install gcloud and run gcloud auth login.")
+        args.local.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+        result = subprocess.run([exe, "storage", "rsync", "--recursive", args.prefix, str(args.local), "--quiet"],
+                                capture_output=True)
+        if result.returncode:
+            raise SystemExit("USER ACTION NEEDED: download failed; check gcloud auth login, prefix and objectViewer permissions.")
+    rows = collect(args.local)
+    if not rows:
+        raise SystemExit("USER ACTION NEEDED: no completed matches found; check Batch task status and logs.")
+    args.out.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+    args.out.write_text("".join(json.dumps(r) + "\n" for r in rows), encoding="utf-8")
+    print(f"Wrote {len(rows)} matches to {args.out}.")
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    main()
