+# Learned joint fault detector
+
+`harness/joint_fault_net.py` · trained by `tools/train_joint_fault.py` · data from `tools/joint_fault_data.py` ·
+weights `harness/models/joint_fault_net.json` · full search results `harness/models/joint_fault_search.json`.
+
+## What it does
+
+For each joint, at the harness telemetry rate (20 Hz), the last 16 readings (0.8 s) of 8 features go in:
+
+| # | feature | meaning |
+|---|---|---|
+| 1 | pos_frac | where the joint is in its range, -1 to 1 |
+| 2 | margin | distance to the nearer limit / 30 deg |
+| 3 | v_toward | velocity toward that limit / 200 deg/s |
+| 4 | track_err | command minus position / 10 deg |
+| 5 | cmd_v_toward | command velocity toward the limit / 200 deg/s |
+| 6 | tau | measured torque / torque limit |
+| 7 | tau_cmd | commanded torque / torque limit |
+| 8 | tau_diff | measured minus commanded / torque limit |
+
+Two flags come out: **now** (the joint is in a fault state) and **soon** (now, or within 0.25 s). An alert is raised
+when a probability stays over its threshold for 2 readings in a row. It runs in the harness on the MacBook next to the
+rule observer (`harness/joint_observer.py`); the NUC keeps the rules (stdlib only). `JointTelemetry` loads the model
+only when its file says it beat the rules on held-out traces (`beats_baseline`).
+
+## Data
+
+Real motion: the 6 sim matches of round 1 (Dream driving both YAM arms), 72 joint series, 413k samples. Each 6 s clip
+is one joint, placed inside the YAM's or the Metal arm's limits, with noise at the Damiao CAN reply's resolution and an
+independent torque-reading error (the sim's measured torque equals the command). One of these is injected; labels come
+from the resulting signals, not from any rule:
+
+normal · near-miss (fast move that stops 1-12 deg short: no fault) · limit run · blocked/crawling joint · oscillation
+(1.5-6 Hz) · overload (torque to the limit) · overspeed (over half the motor's top speed).
+
+Split by match file: 4 files train (16,000 clips), 1 validation (4,000), 1 test (4,000).
+
+## Architecture search (8 Nov 2026)
+
+Same windows, 3,000 steps each, AdamW + one-cycle, BCE with balanced positive weight. Threshold chosen on validation
+for at most 2 false alarms per joint-hour; ranked by "timely recall" (alarm no later than 0.25 s after the fault
+starts), mean over the 5 fault kinds.
+
+| model | weights | val timely | AUROC soon |
+|---|---:|---:|---:|
+| logistic | 258 | 0.00 | 0.600 |
+| MLP 32 | 4,194 | 0.86 | 0.975 |
+| MLP 64-32 | 10,402 | 0.91 | 0.978 |
+| MLP 128-64 | 24,898 | 0.96 | 0.979 |
+| TCN 8 ch, 4 layers | 834 | 0.73 | 0.977 |
+| TCN 16 ch, 3 layers | 2,178 | 0.86 | 0.979 |
+| TCN 16 ch, 4 layers | 2,962 | 0.95 | 0.980 |
+| **TCN 32 ch, 4 layers** | **10,530** | **0.98** | **0.980** |
+| GRU 8 | 450 | 0.50 | 0.976 |
+| GRU 16 | 1,282 | 0.68 | 0.977 |
+| GRU 32 | 4,098 | 0.91 | 0.978 |
+| GRU 32 x2 | 10,434 | 0.95 | 0.979 |
+| Transformer d16, 1 layer | 2,690 | 0.81 | 0.979 |
+| Transformer d32, 1 layer | 9,474 | 0.96 | 0.980 |
+| Transformer d32, 2 layers | 18,018 | 0.98 | 0.980 |
+
+Top 3 retrained 3 seeds x 6,000 steps: TCN 32x4 0.987 ± 0.001, transformer d32x2 0.985 ± 0.004, transformer d32x1
+0.953 ± 0.013. **Winner: dilated causal TCN, 32 channels, 4 layers (dilations 1-2-4-8, kernel 3, residual), last-step +
+mean pooling, 10.5k weights.** It ties the transformer at 58% of its weights and is the most stable across seeds.
+Numpy inference: 0.83 ms for all 12 joints per reading on this laptop, about 2% of one core at 20 Hz.
+
+## Operating point and comparison with the rules (TEST file, never seen in training or selection)
+
+Thresholds picked on validation for at most 1 false alarm per joint-hour: soon 0.998, now 0.95, 2 readings in a row.
+
+| | false alarms / joint-hour | timely recall | limit run (early) | blocked | oscillation | overload | overspeed |
+|---|---:|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
+| learned (soon) | 0.2 | 0.91 | 1.00 (90% before the breach) | 0.94 | 0.92 | 0.99 | 0.68 |
+| rules (any alert) | 197 | 0.50 | 1.00 (99% before) | 0.01 | 0.22 | 0.34 | 0.93 |
+
+The rules' false alarms are almost all `limit_approach` (198/joint-hour): its constant-acceleration projection fires on
+every fast move toward a limit, including ones that stop short. A looser learned operating point (soon 0.97) reaches
+0.99 timely recall at 3.8 false alarms/joint-hour.
+
+## Limits
+
+- All motion is simulated YAM motion and the faults are injected models of faults. Retrain on the NUC's CAN traces
+  (`joints.jsonl` from `can_live` + `JointTelemetry(record_to=...)`) before trusting the numbers on the Metal arms.
+- Overspeed bursts last 1-3 readings at 20 Hz, so the 2-in-a-row rule misses a third of them; the rules catch those.
+  Keep both running.
+- Temperature and motor status are not inputs; the rules cover overheat and motor faults.
+
+Retrain: `python tools/train_joint_fault.py --traces <folder with joints.jsonl>` (needs torch; ~45 min on 20 threads).

+# OpenRouter benchmark implementation notes
+
+Research and bounded diagnostic completed on 2026-11-08 before implementation.
+
+The local reference is `tools/check_monitor.py`, its `CASES`, `SIM_CASES` and
+`sim_frames`, plus `MonitorVerifier.check_now`, `to_openai_messages` and
+`harness.hedge.first_of`. The branch is `hedged-calls`. There is no capsule
+configuration. The only pre-existing untracked file is the user-supplied brief.
+The cable close-ups in `tests/data` label connector types, not stage completion
+or illegal-ball possession, so they cannot supply extra benchmark answers.
+
+Supported workflow:
+
+- Build the real monitor request locally, translate text and base64 JPEG blocks
+  into Chat Completions messages, then validate that translation without HTTP.
+  [OpenRouter vision input formats](https://openrouter.ai/collections/vision-models/)
+  support `image_url` content blocks and base64 data URLs on this endpoint.
+- Authenticated inference uses a Bearer key. Keep credentials out of reports.
+  [Authentication](https://openrouter.ai/docs/api_reference/authentication).
+- Read actual `usage.cost` from every completed HTTP attempt, including losing
+  race and hedge calls. Usage is automatic; no extra accounting request or
+  deprecated include-usage switch is needed.
+  [Usage accounting](https://openrouter.ai/docs/cookbook/administration/usage-accounting).
+- Keep the existing race semantics, first nonempty model response, followed by
+  the monitor parser. A hedge selects the first reply accepted by that parser.
+  Wait for losing calls for accounting after measuring the winning latency.
+- Stop scheduling at the spend threshold or when accounting is incomplete.
+  The last concurrent trial can overshoot the threshold; use the server-side
+  key credit limit for a separate cap.
+  [Key credit limits](https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/best-practices/capping-shared-agent-keys).
+- Use the existing local MuJoCo renderer without starting a policy, station,
+  solver or training job. MuJoCo 4.0.0 and the local `yam_mujoco` example are
+  available. [MuJoCo Python rendering](https://mujoco.readthedocs.io/en/stable/python.html).
+
+Plausible failures and distinguishing evidence:
+
+| Failure | Smallest diagnostic | Success evidence |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| Broken image translation or lost system prompt | Offline build, compare every block and decode JPEG data URLs | Same text, ordering, image bytes and system text |
+| Missing scene dependencies or assets | Render the two existing labelled scenes offline | Three valid JPEG camera frames per scene |
+| Invalid verdict or truncated reasoning | Feed fake response through the real monitor | Parse failures separate from transport failures and wrong answers |
+| Understated race cost | Mock fast winner and slow loser with distinct costs | Both costs counted, latency measured before loser finishes |
+| Spend stop bypassed by missing usage | Fake response with absent cost | Unknown cost remains null and scheduling stops |
+| Bad key, exhausted credit, provider filtering | User-run single-model, single-repeat benchmark only after dry-run | Report has usage and parsed verdict; no credentials in output |
+
+The first diagnostic built the real `release_cable` photo request, translated it
+to one message containing one image, and retained the plan's 4,000-token limit.
+It used no network. The public models catalog was read without authentication;
+the selected IDs and advertised prices are recorded in `OPENROUTER_SETUP.md`.
+No inference, account operation, secret operation or cloud mutation is part of
+implementation verification.
+
+The first full suite also exposed an existing scheduling race in
+`test_monitor_async_never_blocks_and_one_in_flight`. Its fake transport leaves
+the release event set, so the second call can finish before the next poll and
+return a new valid answer. Loading the unchanged `HEAD` monitor in memory and
+letting that second call complete reproduced the same assertion failure.
+[Python Event documentation](https://docs.python.org/3/library/threading.html#event-objects)
+confirms that waits do not block while the flag stays set. Reset the fake's
+event after each release and explicitly release the second call during cleanup.
+This makes the consume-once assertion deterministic without changing monitor
+scheduling. Verification is the isolated test, its monitor test group, then the
+full suite; the two documented Windows fake-CLI failures remain excluded from
+the success criterion.
+
+Final offline verification on this machine:
+
+- `python -m pytest -q`: 354 passed, 1 skipped, 2 known Windows failures in
+  `test_claude_cli.py`, both caused by trying to execute the shell-script fake
+  CLI as a Win32 program. Log: `runs/judges-dry/pytest-final.txt`.
+- New benchmark tests: 32 passed. The combined benchmark, guard, fast-addition
+  and corrected async-monitor checks: 49 passed.
+- Actual CLI dry-run with `--hedge 1 --hedge 2`: 108 validated trials,
+  18 configurations, 180 translated requests, including both real MuJoCo scenes.
+  Reports: `runs/judges-dry/summary.csv` and `results.json`.
+- Native API and subscription dry-run with hedge 2: 8 photo trials validated
+  without constructing authenticated transports or starting Claude.
+- `git diff --cached --check` passed. No inference, account creation, credential
+  entry, secret/cloud mutation or push was performed.

+# OpenRouter judge setup
+
+Account, credit and secret changes belong to you. Never paste a key into chat,
+source files, YAML, benchmark arguments or a Git commit.
+
+## Account and credentials
+
+1. USER ACTION NEEDED: Create an account or sign in at <https://openrouter.ai>.
+2. USER ACTION NEEDED: Open [Credits](https://openrouter.ai/settings/credits),
+   buy a small starting balance, for example $5 if offered, and leave automatic
+   top-ups off for the initial experiment.
+3. USER ACTION NEEDED: Open [API keys](https://openrouter.ai/settings/keys),
+   choose **Create API Key**, name it `hill-climb-judges`, and set **Key limit →
+   Credit limit** to $1 for the first check. Leave the reset interval unset for
+   a lifetime limit. Existing keys have the same fields in their edit dialog.
+   OpenRouter documents these controls in [key credit limits](https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/best-practices/capping-shared-agent-keys).
+4. USER ACTION NEEDED: Store the key as a Windows *user* environment variable
+   in PowerShell. This masked prompt keeps the value out of shell history:
+
+   ```powershell
+   $routerSecret = Read-Host 'Paste <OPENROUTER_API_KEY> here, never into chat' -AsSecureString
+   $routerCredential = [System.Net.NetworkCredential]::new('', $routerSecret)
+   [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('OPENROUTER_API_KEY', $routerCredential.Password, 'User')
+   $env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY = $routerCredential.Password
+   Remove-Variable routerSecret, routerCredential
+   ```
+
+   Exact literal equivalent, with a placeholder only:
+
+   ```powershell
+   [Environment]::SetEnvironmentVariable('OPENROUTER_API_KEY', '<OPENROUTER_API_KEY>', 'User')
+   ```
+
+   Prefer the masked version so the real value never enters PowerShell history.
+   New shells inherit the user variable. Existing shells can load it with
+   `$env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY = [Environment]::GetEnvironmentVariable('OPENROUTER_API_KEY', 'User')`.
+   The variable lives in your Windows profile. Do not print it.
+5. USER ACTION NEEDED: For cloud runs, sign in to your Google account using
+   `gcloud auth login`, then enable Secret Manager if needed:
+
+   ```powershell
+   gcloud services enable secretmanager.googleapis.com --project=hill-climb-sims
+   ```
+
+   Create `openrouter-api-key` from stdin, using the environment variable above:
+
+   ```powershell
+   $env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY | gcloud secrets create openrouter-api-key --project=hill-climb-sims --replication-policy=automatic --data-file=-
+   ```
+
+   The stdin source is `<OPENROUTER_API_KEY>`, not a repository file. PowerShell
+   adds a newline; the retrieval commands below trim it. If the secret already
+   exists, rotate it with
+   `$env:OPENROUTER_API_KEY | gcloud secrets versions add openrouter-api-key --project=hill-climb-sims --data-file=-`.
+   The [gcloud create reference](https://docs.cloud.google.com/sdk/gcloud/reference/secrets/create)
+   documents `--data-file=-` and PowerShell's newline behavior.
+6. USER ACTION NEEDED: Grant only the cloud runner's service account access to
+   this secret. Replace the service-account placeholder with the identity used
+   by the job, then run:
+
+   ```powershell
+   gcloud secrets add-iam-policy-binding openrouter-api-key --project=hill-climb-sims --member='serviceAccount:<RUNNER_SERVICE_ACCOUNT_EMAIL>' --role=roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor
+   ```
+
+   In the Linux cloud job, retrieve the value into the process environment before
+   running the benchmark or harness. Keep shell tracing off:
+
+   ```sh
+   set +x
+   export OPENROUTER_API_KEY=[REDACTED]\r\n')"
+   export HARNESS_CLAUDE_BACKEND=openrouter
+   export HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS=google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite
+   ```
+
+   If the job runs in Docker, pass the existing variable by name with
+   `docker run --rm -e OPENROUTER_API_KEY -e HARNESS_CLAUDE_BACKEND -e HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS ...`.
+   The current cluster launcher does not inject this secret automatically. Put
+   retrieval in the cloud runner or job wrapper.
+
+   The labelled station photo is tracked at `tests/data/table_c_topdown.jpg`;
+   include it when packaging the checkout for a cloud job.
+7. USER ACTION NEEDED: Optional, review [Privacy](https://openrouter.ai/settings/privacy)
+   and [Observability](https://openrouter.ai/workspaces/default/observability).
+   Choose whether providers may train on inputs, whether zero data retention is
+   required, and whether prompts and images should appear in logs. Stricter
+   choices can remove providers. See [data collection](https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/privacy/data-collection)
+   and [provider routing](https://openrouter.ai/docs/guides/routing/provider-selection).
+   These benchmarks use the harness's default provider routing.
+
+## Verify and measure
+
+8. USER ACTION NEEDED: From this worktree in PowerShell, run the offline check.
+   It needs no account, key or network:
+
+   ```powershell
+   $judgePython = '..\hill_climb_harness\.venv\Scripts\python.exe'
+   & $judgePython tools/bench_judges.py --dry-run --hedge 1 --hedge 2 --out runs/judges-dry
+   ```
+
+   This checks all eight singles and the Haiku/Gemini race on four photo
+   questions and two MuJoCo scenes. `--hedge 2` sets `claude.hedge` to two copies.
+   Every request passes through the real monitor and shared wire-body builder;
+   image bytes, text, ordering, system prompt and token limit are checked.
+   Dry summaries leave accuracy, latency and cost null. Add `--no-sim` for a
+   photo-only check if the local `yam_mujoco` example, MuJoCo or Pillow is missing.
+   Full runs require the same renderer as `tools/check_monitor.py --sim`.
+9. USER ACTION NEEDED: When ready to spend credits, run one single-model check:
+
+   ```powershell
+   & $judgePython tools/bench_judges.py --judge openrouter:google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite -n 1 --max-cost-usd 0.10 --out runs/judges-first
+   ```
+
+   Look for numeric cost and parsed answers. Exit 1 means wrong answers;
+   exit 2 means setup, validation, accounting, budget or interruption stopped
+   the run. Partial reports are preserved. Reports omit reply text and exception
+   messages. HTTP and parse errors count as incorrect stage answers.
+10. USER ACTION NEEDED: Compare singles, a model race, and a hedge after the
+    first check works:
+
+    ```powershell
+    & $judgePython tools/bench_judges.py -n 3 --hedge 1 --hedge 2 --max-cost-usd 1 --out runs/judges-all
+    & $judgePython tools/bench_judges.py --judge openrouter:anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5,google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite -n 3 --max-cost-usd 0.50 --out runs/judges-race
+    ```
+
+    Linux cloud jobs use `python tools/bench_judges.py` with the same arguments.
+    For a harness run after model selection, set `HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS` to
+    one ID or a comma-separated race, and pass `--claude-backend openrouter` to
+    the existing harness command. A race uses the first nonempty response, even
+    if it later fails parsing. A hedge uses the first parseable monitor answer.
+    Neither checks correctness before choosing a winner.
+
+`summary.csv` and `results.json` report stage and guard accuracy, parse and
+transport failure rates, and latency p50, p90 and max. Guard accuracy uses only
+guard-labelled cases. `accepted_done_accuracy` also applies the confidence gate.
+Percentiles use linear interpolation. JSON includes each trial, plan hash,
+frame digest and numeric usage. Connector close-ups have no stage/guard labels.
+
+Winning latency ends when `check_now` returns. Accounting waits separately for
+every losing HTTP call before starting the next trial. `cost_per_call_usd` is
+total reported cost divided by HTTP attempts; `cost_per_trial_usd` includes
+every race and hedge copy for one verdict. [Usage accounting](https://openrouter.ai/docs/cookbook/administration/usage-accounting)
+returns `usage.cost` automatically. Missing costs stay null and stop scheduling.
+`--max-cost-usd` defaults to $1 and stops new trials at reported spend. It can
+overshoot by the full concurrent trial already started. Zero starts no calls.
+The key's server-side credit limit is the separate account control.
+
+`--judge api:claude-haiku-5-5` and `--judge subscription:claude-haiku-5-5` also
+work with their existing credentials, but these transports do not expose dollar
+cost in usage. A live run stops after the first trial with unknown cost. Use
+OpenRouter for the budgeted comparison. No live benchmark was run during
+implementation.
+
+## Default candidates
+
+The unauthenticated [live model catalog](https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/models)
+was checked on 2026-11-08. All eight advertise image input and text output.
+Prices below are advertised USD per million input/output tokens, not measured
+costs. Image tokens, provider choice, caching and reasoning change actual cost.
+Speed and guard reliability are hypotheses to measure.
+
+| OpenRouter ID | Input / output | Reason to include |
+| --- | --- | --- |
+| `anthropic/claude-haiku-5.5` | $0.10 / $0.50 | Existing judge family, comparison baseline |
+| `google/gemini-3.1-flash-lite` | $0.25 / $1.50 | Flash-Lite alternative and default race partner |
+| `qwen/qwen3.8-flash` | $0.15 / $0.47 | Current Qwen Flash with vision, inexpensive output |
+| `z-ai/glm-5.3-flash` | $0.0335 / $0.083 | Cheapest advertised paid Flash candidate here |
+| `meta-llama/llama-4-scout` | $0.10 / $0.30 | Independent vision model family at low cost |
+| `openai/gpt-5-nano` | $0.05 / $0.40 | Low-cost small vision model; check reasoning delay |
+| `mistralai/mistral-small-2603` | $0.15 / $0.60 | Current Mistral Small vision alternative |
+| `google/gemma-4-26b-a4b-it` | $0.0675 / $0.225 | Low-cost sparse Gemma vision alternative |
+
+Text-only GLM 4.7 Flash and Qwen Coder Flash are excluded. Batch endpoints,
+image-generation variants and free variants are excluded from the defaults.
+The six cases are a smoke test, not evidence to deploy a guard. Compare guard
+false negatives and false positives separately, collect more labelled match
+frames, and increase repeats before choosing a judge for a match.

+# At the station: grip strength and live joint telemetry
+
+Both need the NUC (`pah@10.103.0.143`, you type the password). Neither changes how Dream's agent drives the arms
+except step 1, which is Dream's own setting.
+
+## 1. Grip at max strength (Darsh's notes: "grip at max strength", "peak strength")
+
+The Metal gripper's grip force is `gripper_hold_torque_nm` in `~/.dreamlabs/station.yaml` (Dream's
+`robots.makers_hold` law: the target is regulated so the measured torque sits at this value). Dream's default is
+0.5 N.m. The station already runs **1.5** on both arms (changed 7 Nov, backup `station.yaml.bak-before-holdtorque`).
+Dream's config accepts at most **2.0** (`MAX_HOLD_TORQUE_NM`).
+
+```bash
+ssh pah@10.103.0.143
+cp ~/.dreamlabs/station.yaml ~/.dreamlabs/station.yaml.bak-before-hold2
+sed -i 's/gripper_hold_torque_nm: 1.5.*/gripper_hold_torque_nm: 2.0  # max Dream accepts (was 1.5)/' ~/.dreamlabs/station.yaml
+grep -n gripper_hold_torque_nm ~/.dreamlabs/station.yaml      # both arms should say 2.0
+dreamcli robot up                                              # restart the station so it reads the file
+```
+
+Then watch the gripper temperature with step 2 while it holds a plug: the gripper latches off at 55 C
+(`robots.metal_gripper.STOP_C`) and refuses to start above 45 C. Going past 2.0 means patching Dream's package
+constant; only consider it if 2.0 still lets plugs slip and the temperature stays well under 45 C.
+
+## 2. Live joint torque, position and commanded torque (harness/can_live.py)
+
+The arms are on SocketCAN (`can_follower_l`, `can_follower_r`, station.log 7 Nov), so a receive-only listener
+sees Dream's MIT commands and the motors' replies. It never sends a frame.
+
+```bash
+# from this laptop (Git Bash), copy the two files:
+scp harness/can_live.py harness/can_torque.py pah@10.103.0.143:~/
+# on the NUC:
+python3 ~/can_live.py --serve 0.0.0.0:8097 --arm left=can_follower_l --arm right=can_follower_r
+# it prints reply rates every 5 s (about 100+ Hz per arm while Dream drives it)
+```
+
+Then run the harness with `--joints http://10.103.0.143:8097`. The monitor's gripper note gains measured grip
+("right gripper is squeezing an object (1.9 of 20 N.m)"), and the run log gets `joint_alert` events (strain,
+blocked, outside force). Check it once by hand: `curl http://10.103.0.143:8097/joints`.

+# USB port finder for the plug-in stage: plan (not built)
+
+Status: plan only, 8 Nov. Nothing trained, nothing committed.
+
+## 0. What the photos and frames show (read this first)
+
+- **The strip has no USB-C port.** It is a black 12-outlet surge strip (red rocker switch, two LEDs "Protected / Grounded", brand logo). The outlets are two columns of six. At the **end nearest the robots** there is a recessed bank of **four silver USB-A ports in one row** (printed "IO 5V/0-3A"). IMG_2703/2704 show three or four silver slots and no USB-C opening.
+- The orange cables are **USB-A at one end, USB-C at the other** (IMG_2702). The USB-A end is what goes into the strip. `harness/cable_end.py` already says the same. So "USB-C port, first from the left" in DECISIONS.md almost certainly means "the first USB port in the bank, counted from the left, that takes the cable's plug". **Confirm with the operator (5 min) before labelling anything.** The rest of this plan calls them "bank ports" and detects USB-A openings; if a USB-C port ever appears, it is a new class, not a rewrite.
+- Viewpoints. Overhead scene cam (640x360): strip is vertical, bank at the bottom edge, each port only ~5-6 px wide, enough for strip pose, not for ports. Wrist cams (640x360, 30 fps, forward-looking, fingers at the bottom of the frame): the strip is a diagonal or vertical dark slab; in the right wrist the bank is ~15-25 px per port and partly hidden by the fingers. Phone photos are 4032x3024 and not the same optics.
+- Appearance: the ports are bright silver slots in a dark recess, so they stand out. The matt black plastic is the problem under glare. Orange ferrules and a golf ball sit near the strip and can be mistaken for it.
+- Data on disk (`nuc_data/`): `hackathon_data/plug-unplug-v1` (42 episodes, 115k frames, left_wrist/right_wrist/scene), `plugin-split-v1-frozen` (12 plug-in episodes, task "plug the loose USB cable on the table into a free port on the hub"), the unplug sets, 1176 mp4 files, 4098 jpg (rollouts, `.dreamlabs/last-run_inputs`). All 640x360. Many frames show people's faces: **keep full frames local or on Drive; anything committed must be strip crops only.**
+- Reality check on value: `plans/hill_climb_v4.yaml` has no plug-in stage (decided 7 Nov: base Dream-1 never seated a plug). So the first payoff is judge help and a seated check, not pushing a plug in. Build in that order.
+
+## 1. What to detect and output
+
+Per frame, per camera, one `PortReading`:
+
+```
+strip: {found, quad[4 pts], conf}            # dark slab, switch end vs USB end known
+ports: [ {index 0..3, box, centre, state, conf, visible_frac} x4 ]
+state:  empty | occupied | occluded | unknown
+index 0 = port on the LEFT of the bank when you stand at the table edge by the robots, facing the strip (= image-left in the overhead photo).
+free_leftmost: index of first state==empty port in 0..3, or null
+```
+
+- Classes: bank USB-A opening (the target) and, only as negatives, AC outlets, the switch, cable ferrules. AC outlets are never output.
+- "First from the left" is decided from the **strip template, not from image x**: the strip is found at any rotation, the USB end is identified (bank of four silver blobs vs the switch/LED end), and index 0 is the end of the bank that is on the left when viewed from the USB end. This matters because in the wrist views the strip is diagonal and in IMG_2703 it is rotated about 60 degrees. The same logic runs on the scene cam (index agrees across cameras) and is the cross-check.
+- Occupied = the opening is filled by the orange or metal plug shell (silver slot interior gone, orange or black blob covering it). A cable merely lying across the strip, as in IMG_2703/2704, must read as `occluded` or `empty`, not `occupied`.
+
+## 2. Approaches compared
+
+| Option | Pros | Cons | Verdict |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| Template match / contour + aspect filters on the dark strip | Cheap, no data | Scale/rotation/glare fragile on its own | Use only as the strip finder |
+| **Strip pose + fixed port offsets from a template** (homography or similarity from strip quad + 4 silver anchors + red switch) | 4 ports are rigid, so one fit gives all four and their indices; survives a gripper hiding 1-2 ports; tiny runtime | Needs strip measurements (a ruler, 5 min); strip quad poor when the strip leaves the frame | **Core** |
+| Tiny learned detector (YOLOv8n or NanoDet, 320 px, ONNX via `cv2.dnn`) | Handles glare and odd poses without hand tuning | ~400 labelled frames; no index logic; 6-15 ms on M-series | Fallback if section 5 acceptance fails after step 5 |
+| Small patch CNN on the 4 projected port patches (48x48, empty/occupied/occluded) | Needs only ~600 patches; mirrors `cable_end.py` | Needs the pose first | **Use for occupancy** |
+| VLM only | Already there | ~1 s and cents per call, cannot say "leftmost of four" reliably at 15 px ports | Stays as the judge; detector feeds it |
+
+**Recommendation:** classical strip finder (dark large quad, `minAreaRect`, aspect ~1:2.9, plus a row of 4 bright silver blobs at one short end to fix orientation) then template fit, then a patch CNN for occupancy. It mirrors the shipped cable-end design (numpy + OpenCV, ONNX through `cv2.dnn`, no torch), the geometry is the thing that does not change between runs, and labelling is mostly 5 clicks a frame. Do not use sim frames for training: the sim hub is a silver 4-port USB-A box, and the Blender-render result in HARNESS_GUIDE (synthetic did not help `cable_end`) says to skip it. If sim testing of the plumbing is wanted, add a black strip mesh to `sim_scenes/hill_climb_table.py` later, for pipeline tests only.
+
+## 3. Data
+
+- **Bootstrap (no station time):** extract every 10th frame from `plugin-split-v1-frozen`, `plug-unplug-v1`, `unplug-*` for wrist_l, wrist_r, scene; keep the ~600 that show the bank (auto-filter by silver-blob row). Add the 4 phone photos only for the strip-finder unit test, not for training (different optics, people in IMG_2698).
+- **Collect at the station:** about 40 minutes. Wrist and scene frames from `GET /frame/<cam>.jpg` while an operator teleoperates or holds arms at 8 poses over the bank (approach from left, centre, right; heights from 30 cm down to contact), strip moved to 5 positions and 3 rotations, with 0, 1, 2, 4 cables seated in every combination, with and without the golf ball and loose cables on the strip, both overhead-light and window-light, and glare from a lamp. Target 1500 frames total, 300 of them with a cable seated in each port position.
+- **Labels:** one small OpenCV click tool (no new service): 4 strip corners + 4 port centres + per-port state, saved as one JSON per frame (`{image, strip_quad, ports:[{xy,state,visible}]}`); COCO export script if the YOLO fallback is needed. ~8 s per frame, ~400 frames labelled by hand, the rest pre-labelled by the template fit and corrected.
+- **Augmentation:** brightness/contrast/gamma, specular blobs on the black plastic, motion blur, JPEG quality, small rotations and scale, random finger-shaped occluders at the bottom edge. No flips (flipping swaps "left").
+- **Splits:** by session/day and by camera, never by frame. Train = bootstrap episodes plus the first station session. Val = one held-out set of episodes. **Test = the last station session recorded at match-day lighting, plus the 4 phone photos.** Nothing from the test session is looked at while tuning.
+
+## 4. Integration
+
+New `harness/usb_port.py` (same shape as `cable_end.py`): `UsbPortFinder(template_yaml, model_path)` with `.read(bgr) -> PortReading`, `.annotate(jpeg) -> bytes`, `.legend()`. Template (measured strip size, port pitch) in `plans/usb_strip.yaml`; patch model in `models/usb_port_state.onnx`; training in `tools/usb_port/`.
+
+- `harness/cli.py` near line 206: add `--usb-ports` flag, set `det.usb_ports`.
+- `harness/detect.py` `annotate_frames` / `legend` (lines ~274-287): chain `usb_ports.annotate`, draw ports as numbered boxes (green = empty, red = occupied, grey = occluded, port 1 outlined) and add the legend line.
+- `harness/verifiers.py` (monitor lines, ~626): optional one-line text hint from `PortReading`, e.g. "detector: port 1 empty, gripper aligned". Ship boxes first and A/B the text hint, since the judge could over-trust it.
+- **Seated check (c):** a `plug_cable` stage's `success` is the VLM question AND detector `ports[target].state == occupied` for 3 consecutive frames, both required. This is the first thing to wire up. A false confirm is worse than a late one, so tune for low false-occupied.
+- **Alignment gate (a):** the wrist camera is rigid on the wrist, so the plug tip is at a fixed pixel `(u*, v*)` and expected scale. Learn both from the teleop insertion moments in `plugin-split-v1-frozen` (port centre and port width at the frame just before seating). Gate: target port centre within 0.5 port-widths of `(u*, v*)` and port width within +-25% of expected, stable for 5 frames. The harness cannot command poses, so the gate does only two things: hold the "bring it vertically down" prompt until aligned, and otherwise re-prompt or retry with a nudge prompt ("move slightly left"). Dream-1 may not honour a nudge; log how often, do not assume it.
+- **Port choice (d):** `free_leftmost`; the operator says port 1, so use it, and fall to the next free port only if port 1 is occupied. Respect the rule that an unplugged cable is not plugged back in.
+- Live guard (`harness/live_guard.py`): leave alone, it is a stop-on-illegal-ball thread. A seated check can reuse `LiveFrames` later if polling is too slow.
+- `harness/decide.py`: log the occupancy and alignment as shadow features before they drive decisions.
+
+## 5. Evaluation and acceptance (held-out test)
+
+| Metric | Threshold |
+|---|---|
+| Strip found when >=80% visible | recall >= 0.97 on wrist, >= 0.99 on scene |
+| Port centre error (when state is not occluded) | median <= 0.25 port-width, 95th pct <= 0.5 |
+| **Index correct** (port 1 is the true leftmost) | >= 0.98 of frames where all 4 ports are visible; a wrong index counts as a critical failure |
+| Occupied vs empty | F1 >= 0.90; false-occupied rate <= 3% |
+| Alignment gate | precision >= 0.90 against labels from teleop insertions (recall >= 0.6 is fine) |
+| Latency (M-series MacBook Air, 640x360, one core) | <= 20 ms per frame all in; target ~8 ms (strip 3 ms, fit 1 ms, patch CNN 4 patches 3 ms). Measure on the Mac, not this PC. Run at <= 10 Hz on wrist cams so the fanless Air does not throttle. |
+
+Also check on every frame set: percentage `unknown` (it must fall back to `unknown`, not guess), and the same numbers per lighting condition. If strip-pose acceptance fails on glare frames after step 5, switch to the YOLO-nano fallback and re-run the same test.
+
+## 6. Risks and mitigations
+
+- **Glare on black plastic:** the strip finder keys on large dark area plus the silver USB blobs, not on edges; add glare augmentation; use CLAHE on V before thresholding; fall back to `unknown`.
+- **Gripper occludes ports:** the template infers hidden ports from visible ones (need >= 2 blobs or the quad); states are `occluded`, never `empty`, when under the fingers.
+- **Strip moves** (arm bumps it, 2703 shows it rotated): re-fit every frame; no stored absolute pose; scene cam gives an independent pose check.
+- **Camera calibration:** no intrinsics needed; the learned `(u*, v*)` absorbs wrist mounting error, but remeasure after any camera re-mount (10 min at the station).
+- **Distractors:** orange ferrules, golf ball, a cable lying across the bank; negatives in the patch set; require silver slot geometry.
+- **Mismatch with the real strip at the match** (different strip model): measure the real strip first; the template is one yaml.
+- **Label ambiguity (USB-C vs USB-A):** settled in step 0.
+- **Privacy:** people appear in scene and wrist frames and IMG_2698; train on crops, commit only crops and the ONNX.
+
+## 7. Build order and time
+
+| Step | Work | Time | Needs user |
+|---|---|---|---|
+| 0 | Confirm USB-A port is the target, measure strip and port pitch | 15 min | Yes (ruler, 5 min at the strip) |
+| 1 | Frame mining + auto-filter from existing videos | 2 h | No |
+| 2 | Click labeller, ~400 labels | 2 h | Light (label review) |
+| 3 | Strip finder + template fit + indexing, unit-tested on the 4 photos and mined frames | 4 h | No |
+| 4 | Patch CNN for occupied/empty (reuse `tools/cable_end/train.py`) | 2 h | No |
+| 5 | `usb_port.py`, `--usb-ports`, annotate + legend; run on recorded rollouts | 3 h | No |
+| 6 | **Station session** (collection above) | 40 min | **Yes: moves the strip, seats cables, teleops arms** |
+| 7 | Retrain on station data, run the held-out test, measure on the Mac | 3 h | Mac access |
+| 8 | Seated-check wiring into the stage + shadow logging; alignment gate calibration | 3 h | No |
+| 9 | Match-day re-calibration at the venue and a smoke run | 20 min | Yes |
+
+About 2 days of engineering plus about 1 hour of the user at the station. Steps 0-5 and 8 (seated check) can happen before any station time; the gate is the last and least certain piece because the policy cannot be steered finely.

diff --git a/harness/cable_end.py b/harness/cable_end.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..848f658
--- /dev/null
+++ b/harness/cable_end.py
@@ -0,0 +1,163 @@
+"""Which end of the orange cable is where: USB-C end, USB-A end, or neither, from any camera frame.
+
+Two steps, numpy + OpenCV only (no torch at runtime):
+1. Candidates: compact orange blobs, i.e. the anodised ferrules (the cable is a long thin run, the strain-relief
+   ribs are slivers). Skin, faces and the orange golf ball also pass; step 2 rejects them.
+2. A small CNN (MobileNetV3-small, 96 x 96 crop, ~6 MB ONNX run by cv2.dnn) labels each crop usb_c / usb_a / none.
+   Trained on Blender renders of this cable (tools/cable_end/render_cable.py: random pose, lighting, lens,
+   backgrounds from station frames) plus hand-labelled station crops (tools/cable_end/train.py).
+
+The station's cables are USB-A to USB-C; the USB-A end is the one that goes into the power strip's USB ports.
+Used to draw "USB-C end" / "USB-A end" boxes on the frames the monitor sees, like harness.detect's boxes. It never
+touches Dream's inputs or the prompts.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import cv2
+import numpy as np
+
+LABELS = ("usb_c", "usb_a", "none")
+NAMES = {"usb_c": "USB-C end", "usb_a": "USB-A end"}
+WORK_W = 640
+SIZE = 96
+BATCH = 8                 # fixed DNN input batch (see CableEndClassifier._forward)
+CROP_SCALE = 2.6
+MEAN = np.array([0.485, 0.456, 0.406], np.float32)
+STD = np.array([0.229, 0.224, 0.225], np.float32)
+
+
+@dataclass
+class End:
+    label: str            # usb_c | usb_a
+    prob: float
+    x0: float
+    y0: float
+    x1: float
+    y1: float             # fractions of the image
+
+
+def ferrules(bgr: np.ndarray, work_w: int = WORK_W) -> list[dict]:
+    """Candidate ferrules: [{cx, cy, side}] in the input image's pixels (side = crop side)."""
+    h0, w0 = bgr.shape[:2]
+    s = work_w / w0
+    im = cv2.resize(bgr, (work_w, max(1, round(h0 * s))), interpolation=cv2.INTER_AREA)
+    hsv = cv2.cvtColor(im, cv2.COLOR_BGR2HSV)
+    H, S, V = hsv[..., 0], hsv[..., 1], hsv[..., 2]
+    mask = ((H >= 3) & (H <= 22) & (S >= 110) & (V >= 60)).astype(np.uint8)
+    mask = cv2.morphologyEx(mask, cv2.MORPH_CLOSE, np.ones((3, 3), np.uint8))
+    n, lab, stats, _ = cv2.connectedComponentsWithStats(mask)
+    total = im.shape[0] * im.shape[1]
+    out = []
+    for i in range(1, n):
+        area = int(stats[i, cv2.CC_STAT_AREA])
+        if area < 30 or area > 0.08 * total:
+            continue
+        ys, xs = np.nonzero(lab == i)
+        (cx, cy), (w, h), _ = cv2.minAreaRect(np.stack([xs, ys], 1).astype(np.float32))
+        L, W = max(w, h), max(1.0, min(w, h))
+        if L / W > 4.0 or area / max(L * W, 1.0) < 0.5:
+            continue
+        out.append({"cx": cx / s, "cy": cy / s, "side": max(L * CROP_SCALE, 24) / s})
+    return out
+
+
+def crop(bgr: np.ndarray, cand: dict, size: int = SIZE) -> np.ndarray:
+    side = cand["side"]
+    x0, y0 = cand["cx"] - side / 2, cand["cy"] - side / 2
+    M = np.float32([[size / side, 0, -x0 * size / side], [0, size / side, -y0 * size / side]])
+    return cv2.warpAffine(bgr, M, (size, size), flags=cv2.INTER_AREA, borderMode=cv2.BORDER_REPLICATE)
+
+
+def _prep(crops: list[np.ndarray]) -> np.ndarray:
+    x = np.stack([c[:, :, ::-1].astype(np.float32) / 255.0 for c in crops])
+    return ((x - MEAN) / STD).transpose(0, 3, 1, 2).copy()
+
+
+class CableEndClassifier:
+    def __init__(self, model_path: str | Path, min_prob: float = 0.7, max_candidates: int = 24):
+        self.net = cv2.dnn.readNetFromONNX(str(model_path))
+        self.min_prob = min_prob
+        self.max_candidates = max_candidates
+
+    def _forward(self, x: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+        """Always the same input shape, padded: OpenCV 5.0's DNN kills the process when one net is reused with a
+        batch larger than an earlier one (2, 5, 4, 4, then 16 crashed it, 7 Nov). Chunks of BATCH cost ~2.5 ms."""
+        out = []
+        for k in range(0, len(x), BATCH):
+            chunk = x[k:k + BATCH]
+            pad = np.zeros((BATCH,) + x.shape[1:], np.float32)
+            pad[:len(chunk)] = chunk
+            self.net.setInput(pad)
+            out.append(self.net.forward()[:len(chunk)].copy())
+        return np.concatenate(out)
+
+    def ends(self, bgr: np.ndarray) -> list[End]:
+        cands = ferrules(bgr)[: self.max_candidates]
+        if not cands:
+            return []
+        logits = self._forward(_prep([crop(bgr, c) for c in cands]))
+        e = np.exp(logits - logits.max(1, keepdims=True))
+        probs = e / e.sum(1, keepdims=True)
+        h, w = bgr.shape[:2]
+        out = []
+        for c, p in zip(cands, probs):
+            k = int(p.argmax())
+            if LABELS[k] == "none" or p[k] < self.min_prob:
+                continue
+            half = c["side"] / 2.6 * 0.75     # box around the ferrule and tip, not the whole crop
+            out.append(End(LABELS[k], float(p[k]), max(0, c["cx"] - half) / w, max(0, c["cy"] - half) / h,
+                           min(w, c["cx"] + half) / w, min(h, c["cy"] + half) / h))
+        return out
+
+    def ends_jpeg(self, jpeg: bytes) -> list[End]:
+        bgr = cv2.imdecode(np.frombuffer(jpeg, np.uint8), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
+        return [] if bgr is None else self.ends(bgr)
+
+    def annotate(self, jpeg: bytes) -> bytes:
+        bgr = cv2.imdecode(np.frombuffer(jpeg, np.uint8), cv2.IMREAD_COLOR)
+        if bgr is None:
+            return jpeg
+        found = self.ends(bgr)
+        if not found:
+            return jpeg
+        h, w = bgr.shape[:2]
+        th = max(2, w // 300)
+        for e in found:
+            col = (255, 0, 255) if e.label == "usb_c" else (0, 200, 255)   # magenta = USB-C, amber = USB-A
+            p0, p1 = (int(e.x0 * w), int(e.y0 * h)), (int(e.x1 * w), int(e.y1 * h))
+            cv2.rectangle(bgr, p0, p1, col, th)
+            cv2.putText(bgr, f"{NAMES[e.label]} {e.prob:.2f}", (p0[0], max(12, p0[1] - 4)), 0,
+                        max(0.4, w / 1600), col, max(1, th // 2))
+        ok, buf = cv2.imencode(".jpg", bgr, [cv2.IMWRITE_JPEG_QUALITY, 88])
+        return buf.tobytes() if ok else jpeg
+
+    @staticmethod
+    def legend() -> str:
+        return ("MAGENTA boxes = USB-C end of a cable, AMBER boxes = USB-A end (the end that plugs into the power "
+                "strip's USB ports); from a local classifier, can miss or mislabel.")
+
+
+def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
+    import argparse
+    ap = argparse.ArgumentParser(description="label cable ends in images")
+    ap.add_argument("model")
+    ap.add_argument("images", nargs="+")
+    ap.add_argument("--out-dir", default=None)
+    a = ap.parse_args(argv)
+    clf = CableEndClassifier(a.model)
+    for p in a.images:
+        data = Path(p).read_bytes()
+        for e in clf.ends_jpeg(data):
+            print(f"{p}: {NAMES[e.label]} p={e.prob:.2f} box=({e.x0:.2f},{e.y0:.2f})-({e.x1:.2f},{e.y1:.2f})")
+        if a.out_dir:
+            Path(a.out_dir).mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+            (Path(a.out_dir) / (Path(p).stem + "_ends.jpg")).write_bytes(clf.annotate(data))
+    return 0
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

new file mode 100644
index 0000000..1654053

+++ b/harness/can_live.py
@@ -0,0 +1,201 @@
+"""Live joint telemetry from the arms' CAN buses on the station NUC: what the Dream API never returns.
+
+Receive-only (it never sends a frame): Dream's agent drives each Metal arm over SocketCAN (`can_follower_l`,
+`can_follower_r`, station.log), and Linux delivers every frame on a link to every raw socket bound to it, so this
+sees both directions:
+
+- Dream's MIT command to motor i (arbitration id i): target position, target velocity, kp, kd, feed-forward torque
+  (robots/damiao.py encode_mit, dreamlabs 1.0.37);
+- the motor's reply (id 0x10 + i): measured position, velocity, torque (from phase current), temperatures, status.
+
+From the pair it computes the torque the controller commands, `kp (q_cmd - q) + kd (dq_cmd - dq) + tau_ff`, next to
+the torque the motor reports. Served as JSON, latest values plus a short history:
+
+    python3 harness/can_live.py --serve 0.0.0.0:8097 --arm left=can_follower_l --arm right=can_follower_r
+    GET /joints                 {"t", "rate_hz": {arm}, "arms": {arm: {joint: {...}}}}
+    GET /joints/history?s=2     the last s seconds, one row per arm per reply cycle (decimated to <= 100 Hz)
+
+Standard library only (Python 3.8+ on Linux), so it runs on the NUC with nothing installed: copy this file and
+harness/can_torque.py next to it. The gripper runs in force-position mode (commands at 0x300 + id), whose frames
+are kept raw; its reply is decoded like any joint's. A CANable in slcan (serial) mode is exclusive to Dream's
+agent: there is nothing to listen to then, and this says so.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import json
+import math
+import struct
+import sys
+import threading
+import time
+from collections import deque
+from http.server import BaseHTTPRequestHandler, ThreadingHTTPServer
+from urllib.parse import parse_qs, urlsplit
+
+try:
+    from harness.can_torque import LIMITS, MOTORS, REPLY_OFFSET, decode as decode_reply
+except ImportError:                                  # run as a lone script next to can_torque.py on the NUC
+    from can_torque import LIMITS, MOTORS, REPLY_OFFSET, decode as decode_reply  # type: ignore
+
+KP_RANGE, KD_RANGE = (0.0, 500.0), (0.0, 5.0)        # robots/damiao.py
+FORCE_POSITION_OFFSET = 0x300                        # robots/damiao.py MODE_OFFSETS[MODE_FORCE_POSITION]
+
+
+def _u2f(x: int, lo: float, hi: float, bits: int) -> float:
+    return x * (hi - lo) / ((1 << bits) - 1) + lo
+
+
+def decode_mit(data: bytes, model: str) -> dict[str, float]:
+    """Inverse of robots.damiao.encode_mit: [q16, dq12, kp12, kd12, tau12]."""
+    pmax, vmax, tmax = LIMITS[model]
+    q = (data[0] << 8) | data[1]
+    dq = (data[2] << 4) | (data[3] >> 4)
+    kp = ((data[3] & 0x0F) << 8) | data[4]
+    kd = (data[5] << 4) | (data[6] >> 4)
+    tau = ((data[6] & 0x0F) << 8) | data[7]
+    return {"q_cmd_deg": math.degrees(_u2f(q, -pmax, pmax, 16)), "dq_cmd_deg_s": math.degrees(_u2f(dq, -vmax, vmax, 12)),
+            "kp": _u2f(kp, *KP_RANGE, 12), "kd": _u2f(kd, *KD_RANGE, 12), "tau_ff_nm": _u2f(tau, -tmax, tmax, 12)}
+
+
+class ArmState:
+    """Latest command and reply per joint of one arm, plus a short history."""
+
+    def __init__(self, arm: str, history_s: float = 10.0):
+        self.arm = arm
+        self.joints: dict[str, dict] = {name: {} for name, _ in MOTORS.values()}
+        self.lock = threading.Lock()
+        self.history: deque = deque(maxlen=int(history_s * 100))
+        self._replies: deque = deque(maxlen=400)
+        self._last_row_t = 0.0
+        try:
+            from harness.joint_observer import JointObserver
+        except ImportError:                          # lone script on the NUC: joint_observer.py next to it
+            try:
+                from joint_observer import JointObserver  # type: ignore
+            except ImportError:
+                JointObserver = None
+        self.observer = JointObserver() if JointObserver else None
+        self.alerts: dict[tuple, dict] = {}         # active look-ahead alerts, updated at the reply rate
+
+    def feed(self, can_id: int, data: bytes, t: float) -> None:
+        if len(data) != 8:
+            return
+        if can_id in MOTORS:                                         # Dream's MIT command
+            name, model = MOTORS[can_id]
+            if data == b"\xff" * 7 + data[7:]:                       # enable / disable / zero, not a command
+                return
+            with self.lock:
+                self.joints[name].update(decode_mit(data, model), t_cmd=t)
+        elif can_id - FORCE_POSITION_OFFSET in MOTORS:               # gripper force-position command: raw
+            name, _ = MOTORS[can_id - FORCE_POSITION_OFFSET]
+            with self.lock:
+                self.joints[name].update(fp_cmd_hex=data.hex(), t_cmd=t)
+        elif can_id - REPLY_OFFSET in MOTORS and (data[0] & 0x0F) == can_id - REPLY_OFFSET:
+            name, model = MOTORS[can_id - REPLY_OFFSET]
+            r = decode_reply(data, model)
+            with self.lock:
+                j = self.joints[name]
+                j.update(q_deg=r["pos_deg"], dq_deg_s=r["vel_deg_s"], tau_nm=r["torque_nm"], status=r["status"],
+                         temp_mos_c=r["temp_mos_c"], temp_rotor_c=r["temp_rotor_c"], t_reply=t,
+                         tau_max_nm=LIMITS[model][2])
+                if "kp" in j:
+                    j["tau_cmd_nm"] = (j["kp"] * math.radians(j["q_cmd_deg"] - j["q_deg"])
+                                       + j["kd"] * math.radians(j["dq_cmd_deg_s"] - j["dq_deg_s"]) + j["tau_ff_nm"])
+                self._replies.append(t)
+                if self.observer is not None:     # at the CAN reply rate, per joint
+                    for key in [k for k in self.alerts if k[1] == name]:
+                        del self.alerts[key]
+                    for a in self.observer.observe(t, self.arm, {name: j}):
+                        self.alerts[(a["kind"], a["joint"])] = a
+                if t - self._last_row_t >= 0.01:                     # history at <= 100 Hz
+                    self._last_row_t = t
+                    self.history.append({"t": round(t, 4), **{
+                        n: [round(v.get(k, float("nan")), 3) for k in ("q_deg", "q_cmd_deg", "tau_nm", "tau_cmd_nm")]
+                        for n, v in self.joints.items() if "q_deg" in v}})
+
+    def snapshot(self) -> dict:
+        with self.lock:
+            return {n: {k: (round(v, 4) if isinstance(v, float) else v) for k, v in j.items()}
+                    for n, j in self.joints.items()}
+
+    def rate_hz(self, now: float) -> float:
+        with self.lock:
+            recent = [t for t in self._replies if now - t <= 1.0]
+        return round(len(recent) / len(MOTORS), 1)          # reply cycles per second (one reply per motor each)
+
+
+def listen(channel: str, arm: ArmState, stop: threading.Event) -> None:
+    """Receive-only raw SocketCAN loop. Never sends."""
+    import socket
+
+    if not hasattr(socket, "AF_CAN"):
+        raise SystemExit("SocketCAN needs Linux: run this on the station NUC.")
+    sock = socket.socket(socket.AF_CAN, socket.SOCK_RAW, socket.CAN_RAW)
+    sock.bind((channel,))
+    sock.settimeout(0.5)
+    while not stop.is_set():
+        try:
+            frame = sock.recv(16)
+        except socket.timeout:
+            continue
+        can_id, dlc = struct.unpack("=IB3x", frame[:8])
+        arm.feed(can_id & 0x7FF, frame[8:8 + dlc], time.time())
+
+
+def serve(arms: dict[str, ArmState], host: str, port: int) -> ThreadingHTTPServer:
+    class Handler(BaseHTTPRequestHandler):
+        def log_message(self, *a):  # quiet
+            pass
+
+        def do_GET(self):
+            url = urlsplit(self.path)
+            now = time.time()
+            if url.path == "/joints":
+                body = {"t": now, "rate_hz": {a: s.rate_hz(now) for a, s in arms.items()},
+                        "arms": {a: s.snapshot() for a, s in arms.items()},
+                        "observer_alerts": [x for s in arms.values() for x in list(s.alerts.values())]}
+            elif url.path == "/joints/history":
+                secs = float(parse_qs(url.query).get("s", ["2"])[0])
+                body = {"t": now, "arms": {a: [r for r in list(s.history) if now - r["t"] <= secs]
+                                           for a, s in arms.items()}}
+            else:
+                self.send_error(404)
+                return
+            data = json.dumps(body).encode()
+            self.send_response(200)
+            self.send_header("Content-Type", "application/json")
+            self.send_header("Content-Length", str(len(data)))
+            self.end_headers()
+            self.wfile.write(data)
+
+    server = ThreadingHTTPServer((host, port), Handler)
+    threading.Thread(target=server.serve_forever, daemon=True).start()
+    return server
+
+
+def main() -> None:
+    p = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__, formatter_class=argparse.RawDescriptionHelpFormatter)
+    p.add_argument("--serve", default="0.0.0.0:8097", help="host:port for the JSON routes")
+    p.add_argument("--arm", action="append", default=[], help="name=channel, e.g. right=can_follower_r")
+    args = p.parse_args()
+    pairs = dict(a.split("=", 1) for a in (args.arm or ["left=can_follower_l", "right=can_follower_r"]))
+    arms = {name: ArmState(name) for name in pairs}
+    stop = threading.Event()
+    for name, channel in pairs.items():
+        threading.Thread(target=listen, args=(channel, arms[name], stop), daemon=True).start()
+    host, port = args.serve.rsplit(":", 1)
+    serve(arms, host, int(port))
+    print(f"can_live: listening (receive only) on {pairs}; GET http://{args.serve}/joints", flush=True)
+    try:
+        while True:
+            time.sleep(5)
+            now = time.time()
+            print("rates Hz:", {a: s.rate_hz(now) for a, s in arms.items()}, flush=True)
+    except KeyboardInterrupt:
+        stop.set()
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    main()

+def match_problems(args: argparse.Namespace, plan) -> list[str]:
+    """Why these args and this plan can't run as a match (empty list = fine). Any human input after go is a DQ."""
+    problems = []
+    if args.budget:
+        problems.append("--budget: a match runs on the plan's own time_budget_s")
+    if args.verifier in MANUAL_VERIFIERS:
+        problems.append(f"--verifier {args.verifier}: a typed y/n is human input")
+    manual = sorted({st.name for st in plan.stages if st.verifier in MANUAL_VERIFIERS})
+    if manual and args.verifier not in ("monitor", "claude", "time"):
+        problems.append(f"stages answered by a person: {', '.join(manual)} (use --verifier monitor)")
+    return problems

+            runner.fast = FastLoop(weights=fw, rate_hz=float(plan.fast.get("rate_hz", 20.0)),
+                                   mode=str(plan.fast.get("mode", "shadow")), frames=frames_fn,
+                                   guard_judge=det.guard if det is not None else None,

+            runner.fast.start()
+            log.event("fast_loop", rate_hz=plan.fast.get("rate_hz", 20.0), mode=plan.fast.get("mode", "shadow"),

new file mode 100644
index 0000000..e366c45

@@ -0,0 +1,337 @@
+"""The runner's decision tree as weighted equations, so its constants can be tuned (HARNESS_DECISION_MODEL section 5).
+
+Three pieces, all pure functions of numbers (no I/O, no clock):
+
+1. Done evidence, a running score over looks:  l_t = keep * l_{t-1} + sum_i w_i * phi_i(t),  done when l_t >= theta.
+   With DEFAULT weights it is exactly the runner's rule: `confirm` monitor "done" looks in a row pass the stage, a
+   "not done" look resets the count, a repeated look at the same frames counts for nothing, and a second "done"
+   within done_window_s passes even with a "failing" flicker in between.
+2. Fail score:  F = combine_k(w_k * g_k), each g_k a ratio that reaches 1 when today's hard rule fires
+   (consecutive failing looks / confirm, consecutive "stalled" looks / confirm, time since progress rose / stall_s,
+   elapsed / timeout_s). A look is failing on a no, a "failing" status, or progress falling REGRESSION_DROP below
+   the attempt's best (look_failing). The attempt fails when F >= theta_fail. combine="max" is today's tree (any one rule);
+   combine="sum" lets weak signals add up, which is what tuning can explore.
+3. Value of the time left:  W_i(L) = max_T  F_i(T) (V_i + W_{i+1}(L - t_succ)) + (1 - F_i(T)) * [skip ? W_{i+1}(L - T) : 0]
+   over a stage list, by dynamic programming on a 1 s grid. It gives each stage the timeout that maximises expected
+   points for the time left (`plan_timeouts`) and the value of an action (`action_value`):
+   Q(a) = P_a * V_s + W(L - c_a) - risk_weight * R_a.
+
+Nothing here moves a robot or changes the runner yet: the runner keeps its own rules. The tests check that the
+default weights reproduce them; tuning and shadow logging come next.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import math
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field, replace
+from typing import Iterable, Sequence
+
+# Today's runner constants (harness/runner.py), the starting point for tuning.
+CONFIRM = 2
+DONE_WINDOW_S = 15.0
+REGRESSION_DROP = 0.3
+FAIL_MIN_FRACTION = 0.5
+
+
+# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 1. done evidence
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class DoneWeights:
+    w_monitor: float = 1.0          # a monitor "done" look
+    w_sensor_release: float = 0.0   # gripper went from closed on an object to open near the target (/arms); new, off
+    w_goal_box: float = 0.0         # detector sees the object at its goal (in the bin, on the tower); new, off
+    w_plug_count: float = 0.0       # cable-end boxes: one fewer plug in the strip; new, off
+    keep: float = 1.0               # share of the score carried to the next look when nothing argues against it
+    reset_on_no: bool = True        # a "not done" / "failing" look sets the score to 0 (outside the done window)
+    theta: float = float(CONFIRM)   # done when the score reaches this
+    window_s: float = DONE_WINDOW_S  # a second "done" within this passes, flickers in between notwithstanding
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Look:
+    """One check. done: the verifier's answer (True / False / None = no answer). failing: monitor status failing."""
+    t: float
+    done: bool | None
+    failing: bool = False
+    same_frames: bool = False       # the same camera images as the previous done look
+    monitor: bool = True            # from the monitor: a look with no done answer still resets the count
+    sensor_release: float = 0.0     # 0..1 features from the fast sensors
+    goal_box: float = 0.0
+    plug_count: float = 0.0
+
+
+@dataclass
+class DoneState:
+    score: float = 0.0
+    last_done_t: float | None = None
+
+
+def done_step(state: DoneState, look: Look, w: DoneWeights = DoneWeights()) -> tuple[DoneState, bool]:
+    """Folds one look into the done score. Returns (new state, stage passes now)."""
+    fast = w.w_sensor_release * look.sensor_release + w.w_goal_box * look.goal_box + w.w_plug_count * look.plug_count
+    if look.done is True and look.same_frames:
+        return DoneState(state.score * w.keep + fast, state.last_done_t), state.score * w.keep + fast >= w.theta
+    if look.done is True:
+        recent = state.last_done_t is not None and look.t - state.last_done_t <= w.window_s
+        score = state.score * w.keep + w.w_monitor + fast
+        return DoneState(score, look.t), score >= w.theta or recent
+    # The runner: a no / failing look right after a done (within the window) changes nothing; otherwise it resets the
+    # count. A monitor look with no done answer resets it even inside the window (the window still passes the next done).
+    flicker = look.done is False or look.failing
+    blank = look.done is None and look.monitor
+    in_window = state.last_done_t is not None and look.t - state.last_done_t <= w.window_s
+    if flicker and in_window:
+        score = state.score
+    elif (flicker or blank) and w.reset_on_no:
+        score = fast
+    elif flicker or blank:
+        score = state.score * w.keep + fast
+    else:
+        score = state.score * w.keep + fast
+    return DoneState(score, state.last_done_t), score >= w.theta
+
+
+def run_looks(looks: Iterable[Look], w: DoneWeights = DoneWeights()) -> float | None:
+    """Time of the look that passes the stage, or None."""
+    st = DoneState()
+    for lk in looks:
+        st, ok = done_step(st, lk, w)
+        if ok:
+            return lk.t
+    return None
+
+
+# ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 2. fail score
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class FailWeights:
+    w_fail: float = 1.0          # consecutive failing looks / confirm (monitor: only after min_fraction of the timeout)
+    w_stalled: float = 1.0       # consecutive "stalled" monitor looks / confirm (stages with a stall window)
+    w_stall: float = 1.0         # seconds since progress last rose / stall_s
+    w_time: float = 1.0          # elapsed / timeout_s
+    w_on_target: float = 0.0     # subtracted while a gripper holds the target (today: a one-off 20 s extension)
+    w_regress: float = 1.0       # in look_failing: (max progress - progress) / regress_drop >= 1 makes a failing look
+    confirm: int = CONFIRM
+    regress_drop: float = REGRESSION_DROP
+    min_fraction: float = FAIL_MIN_FRACTION
+    combine: str = "max"         # "max" = today's tree (any rule fires); "sum" = weak signals add up
+    theta: float = 1.0
+
+
+def look_failing(done: bool | None, status_failing: bool, progress: float | None, max_progress: float | None,
+                 done_seen: bool, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> bool:
+    """Is one look a failing look? The verifier said no, the monitor said failing, or progress fell regress_drop
+    below the attempt's best (not counted once a done has been seen, as in the runner)."""
+    regress = 0.0
+    if progress is not None and max_progress is not None and not done_seen:
+        regress = w.w_regress * max(0.0, max_progress - progress) / w.regress_drop
+    return done is False or status_failing or regress >= 1.0 - 1e-9
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class AttemptState:
+    elapsed: float
+    timeout_s: float
+    consec_fail: int = 0                 # failing looks in a row (look_failing)
+    consec_stalled: int = 0              # "stalled" monitor looks in a row
+    since_rise_s: float | None = None    # seconds since progress last rose by STALL_RISE (None: no progress yet)
+    stall_s: float | None = None         # the stage's stall window (None: no stall rules)
+    monitor: bool = True                 # failing looks come from the monitor (then min_fraction applies)
+    on_target: bool = False
+
+
+def fail_terms(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> dict[str, float]:
+    """Each term reaches its weight when its hard rule would fire on its own."""
+    gate_ok = not a.monitor or a.elapsed >= w.min_fraction * a.timeout_s
+    terms = {"fail": w.w_fail * (a.consec_fail / w.confirm) if gate_ok else 0.0,
+             "time": w.w_time * a.elapsed / a.timeout_s}
+    if a.stall_s is not None:
+        terms["stalled"] = w.w_stalled * a.consec_stalled / w.confirm
+        if a.since_rise_s is not None:
+            terms["stall"] = w.w_stall * a.since_rise_s / a.stall_s
+    return terms
+
+
+def fail_score(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> float:
+    terms = list(fail_terms(a, w).values())
+    s = max(terms) if w.combine == "max" else sum(terms)
+    return s - (w.w_on_target if a.on_target else 0.0)
+
+
+def attempt_fails(a: AttemptState, w: FailWeights = FailWeights()) -> bool:
+    return fail_score(a, w) >= w.theta - 1e-9
+
+
+# -------------------------------------------------------------------------------- 3. value of the time left
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class StageModel:
+    """One stage for planning. Success by time tau: F(tau) = p_max * (1 - exp(-max(0, tau - t_min) / tau_c)).
+    `gate`: a pass bar; if it fails, nothing after it scores (it is retried until the clock instead of skipped).
+    Fit p_max, t_min, tau_c per stage from run logs or the stress sim."""
+    name: str
+    value: float
+    p_max: float
+    tau_c: float
+    t_min: float = 0.0
+    gate: bool = False
+
+    def F(self, tau: float) -> float:
+        if tau <= self.t_min:
+            return 0.0
+        return self.p_max * (1.0 - math.exp(-(tau - self.t_min) / self.tau_c))
+
+    def mean_success_time(self, T: float, step: float = 1.0) -> float:
+        """Expected time spent given success by T (integral of tau dF over [0, T] / F(T))."""
+        FT = self.F(T)
+        if FT <= 0:
+            return T
+        n = max(1, int(T / step))
+        acc, prev = 0.0, 0.0
+        for k in range(1, n + 1):
+            tau = k * T / n
+            f = self.F(tau)
+            acc += (tau - T / n / 2) * (f - prev)
+            prev = f
+        return acc / FT
+
+
+@dataclass
+class Plan:
+    """W[i][L]: expected points from stage i on with L whole seconds left; best_T[i][L]: that stage's timeout."""
+    stages: Sequence[StageModel]
+    horizon_s: int
+    W: list[list[float]] = field(default_factory=list)
+    best_T: list[list[int]] = field(default_factory=list)
+
+    def value(self, i: int, left_s: float) -> float:
+        if i >= len(self.stages):
+            return 0.0
+        return self.W[i][max(0, min(self.horizon_s, int(left_s)))]
+
+    def timeout(self, i: int, left_s: float) -> int:
+        return self.best_T[i][max(0, min(self.horizon_s, int(left_s)))]
+
+
+def plan_timeouts(stages: Sequence[StageModel], horizon_s: int, step_s: int = 1) -> Plan:
+    """Backward DP over the stage list. For a gate stage a failure leaves 0 (nothing later scores)."""
+    n = len(stages)
+    W = [[0.0] * (horizon_s + 1) for _ in range(n + 1)]
+    best = [[0] * (horizon_s + 1) for _ in range(n)]
+    for i in range(n - 1, -1, -1):
+        st = stages[i]
+        succ_t = [st.mean_success_time(T) for T in range(horizon_s + 1)]
+        for L in range(horizon_s + 1):
+            top, top_T = W[i + 1][L], 0           # T = 0: skip the stage
+            for T in range(step_s, L + 1, step_s):
+                p = st.F(T)
+                after_ok = W[i + 1][max(0, L - int(round(succ_t[T])))]
+                after_fail = 0.0 if st.gate else W[i + 1][L - T]
+                v = p * (st.value + after_ok) + (1 - p) * after_fail
+                if v > top + 1e-12:
+                    top, top_T = v, T
+            W[i][L], best[i][L] = top, top_T
+    return Plan(stages, horizon_s, W, best)
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class Action:
+    """A choice at a decision point: p = chance it succeeds the stage, cost_s = seconds it uses, risk = 0..1 chance of
+    something costly (a DQ, a knocked tower, an illegal ball)."""
+    name: str
+    p: float
+    cost_s: float
+    risk: float = 0.0
+
+
+def action_value(plan: Plan, i: int, left_s: float, a: Action, risk_weight: float = 50.0) -> float:
+    """Q(a) = p (V_i + W_{i+1}(L - c)) + (1 - p) W_i'(L - c) - risk_weight * risk, where after a failed action the
+    same stage can still be tried with the time left (W_i), or nothing later scores for a gate that ran out."""
+    st = plan.stages[i]
+    rest = left_s - a.cost_s
+    if rest < 0:
+        return -risk_weight * a.risk
+    ok = a.p * (st.value + plan.value(i + 1, rest))
+    again = (1 - a.p) * plan.value(i, rest)
+    return ok + again - risk_weight * a.risk
+
+
+def choose(plan: Plan, i: int, left_s: float, actions: Sequence[Action], risk_weight: float = 50.0) -> Action:
+    return max(actions, key=lambda a: action_value(plan, i, left_s, a, risk_weight))
+
+
+def with_weights(obj, **kw):
+    """Copy of a weights dataclass with some fields changed (for hill climbing)."""
+    return replace(obj, **kw)
+
+
+# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ 4. shadow mode
+def weights_from(raw: dict | None) -> tuple[DoneWeights, FailWeights]:
+    """plan `decide: {done: {...}, fail: {...}}` -> weights. Unknown keys are an error (a typo must not pass)."""
+    raw = raw or {}
+    extra = set(raw) - {"done", "fail"}
+    if extra:
+        raise ValueError(f"plan.decide: unknown key(s): {', '.join(sorted(extra))}")
+    out = []
+    for key, cls in (("done", DoneWeights), ("fail", FailWeights)):
+        part = raw.get(key) or {}
+        if not isinstance(part, dict):
+            raise ValueError(f"plan.decide.{key}: must be a mapping")
+        names = set(cls.__dataclass_fields__)
+        bad = set(part) - names
+        if bad:
+            raise ValueError(f"plan.decide.{key}: unknown weight(s): {', '.join(sorted(bad))}")
+        if key == "fail" and part.get("combine", "max") not in ("max", "sum"):
+            raise ValueError("plan.decide.fail.combine: max or sum")
+        out.append(cls(**part))
+    return out[0], out[1]
+
+
+class Shadow:
+    """Runs the equations next to the runner on every look and reports what they would decide. It never acts.
+
+    The runner calls start() at each attempt and look() once per verdict, with the inputs it already computed
+    (`failing` includes the regression rule). Counts follow the runner's: a failing look right after a done (within
+    the window) is not counted, a done resets the failing count."""
+
+    def __init__(self, done: DoneWeights = DoneWeights(), fail: FailWeights = FailWeights(), *,
+                 fixed: set[str] = frozenset()):
+        self.base_done, self.base_fail = done, fail
+        self.done_w, self.fail_w = done, fail
+        self._fixed = set(fixed)        # "done.theta", "fail.confirm": set by the plan, not taken from the stage
+        self._st = DoneState()
+        self._consec_fail = 0
+        self._timeout = 1.0
+        self._stall: float | None = None
+
+    @classmethod
+    def from_plan(cls, raw: dict | None) -> "Shadow":
+        done, fail = weights_from(raw)
+        raw = raw or {}
+        fixed = {f"{k}.{n}" for k in ("done", "fail") for n in (raw.get(k) or {})}
+        return cls(done, fail, fixed=fixed)
+
+    def start(self, timeout_s: float, stall_s: float | None, confirm: int = CONFIRM) -> None:
+        """Per attempt. The stage's confirm sets the done threshold and the failing-look count unless the plan fixed them."""
+        self.done_w = self.base_done if "done.theta" in self._fixed else replace(self.base_done, theta=float(confirm))
+        self.fail_w = self.base_fail if "fail.confirm" in self._fixed else replace(self.base_fail, confirm=int(confirm))
+        self._st, self._consec_fail = DoneState(), 0
+        self._timeout, self._stall = timeout_s, stall_s
+
+    def look(self, elapsed: float, done: bool | None, failing: bool, *, same_frames: bool, monitor: bool,
+             consec_stalled: int, since_rise_s: float | None, sensor_release: float = 0.0, goal_box: float = 0.0,
+             plug_count: float = 0.0) -> dict:
+        lk = Look(elapsed, done, failing=failing and done is not False, same_frames=same_frames, monitor=monitor,
+                  sensor_release=sensor_release, goal_box=goal_box, plug_count=plug_count)
+        in_window = self._st.last_done_t is not None and elapsed - self._st.last_done_t <= self.done_w.window_s
+        self._st, passes = done_step(self._st, lk, self.done_w)
+        if done is True and not same_frames:
+            self._consec_fail = 0
+        elif (failing or done is False) and not in_window:
+            self._consec_fail += 1
+        elif not (failing or done is False) and (done is not None or monitor):
+            self._consec_fail = 0
+        a = AttemptState(elapsed=elapsed, timeout_s=self._timeout, consec_fail=self._consec_fail,
+                         consec_stalled=consec_stalled, since_rise_s=since_rise_s, stall_s=self._stall, monitor=monitor)
+        terms = fail_terms(a, self.fail_w)
+        score = fail_score(a, self.fail_w)
+        return {"done_score": round(self._st.score, 3), "would_pass": bool(passes),
+                "fail_score": round(score, 3), "would_fail": bool(not passes and score >= self.fail_w.theta - 1e-9),
+                "terms": {k: round(v, 3) for k, v in terms.items()}}

+HOLE_SURE = 0.6     # at or above this a hole was really seen on the overhead view; only then is it shown or used
+_HOLE_DARK = 0.62   # a hole is at least this much darker than the cube's median brightness
+
+
+def _hole_axis(raw: np.ndarray, blob: np.ndarray, value: np.ndarray) -> tuple[str, float]:
+    """Which way a cube's round hole runs, from one view that sees the cube's top: (axis, confidence).
+
+    The hole is a roughly round patch inside the blob that is not the cube's colour, or is much darker than it.
+    vertical = hole on the top face (near the blob's centre or upper half). horizontal-y = on the face toward the
+    camera (lower half). horizontal-x = on a side face (off to one side and narrow). horizontal = no hole seen, so it
+    runs sideways through a face this view can't see. Foam shadow and small cubes make this a guess: use the
+    confidence, and don't gate a stage on it without checking it on the station's cameras."""
+    if _ndi is None:
+        return "unknown", 0.0
+    ys, xs = np.nonzero(blob)
+    y0, y1, x0, x1 = ys.min(), ys.max() + 1, xs.min(), xs.max() + 1
+    b, r, v = blob[y0:y1, x0:x1], raw[y0:y1, x0:x1], value[y0:y1, x0:x1]
+    body = b & r
+    if body.sum() < 40:
+        return "unknown", 0.0
+    H, W = b.shape
+    cand = _ndi.binary_erosion(b, iterations=1) & (~r | (v < _HOLE_DARK * float(np.median(v[body]))))
+    lab, n = _ndi.label(cand)
+    best, area = None, 0
+    for i in range(1, n + 1):
+        comp = lab == i
+        size = int(comp.sum())
+        if size > area and not (_ndi.binary_dilation(comp, iterations=1) & ~b).any():
+            best, area = comp, size
+    frac = area / float(b.sum())
+    if best is None or frac < 0.02:
+        return "horizontal", 0.5
+    hy, hx = np.nonzero(best)
+    w, h = int(hx.max() - hx.min() + 1), int(hy.max() - hy.min() + 1)
+    fill = area / (np.pi / 4 * w * h)
+    if frac > 0.40 or min(w, h) < 3 or not 0.55 <= fill <= 1.25:
+        return "unknown", 0.3
+    dx, dy = (hx.mean() - (W - 1) / 2) / W, (hy.mean() - (H - 1) / 2) / H
+    conf = 0.85 if frac >= 0.05 and 0.7 <= fill <= 1.1 else 0.6
+    if abs(dx) > 0.15 and w < 0.85 * h:
+        return "horizontal-x", conf
+    return ("horizontal-y", conf) if dy > 0.1 else ("vertical", conf)
+
+
+def _mark_upright(boxes: list[Box], ratio: float) -> None:
+    """A tall thin stick standing on a cube in the image is upright. A stick lying toward the camera can look the
+    same, so this only applies on top of a cube. `ratio` is the frame's height / width."""
+    cubes = [b for b in boxes if b.cls.endswith(" cube")]
+    for s in boxes:
+        if not (s.cls.endswith(" stick") and s.orientation == "lying"):
+            continue
+        tall = (s.y1 - s.y0) * ratio > 1.6 * (s.x1 - s.x0)
+        if tall and any(abs(c.y0 - s.y1) < 0.03 and c.x0 <= s.cx <= c.x1 for c in cubes):
+            s.orientation = "upright"
+
+
+def _top_cube(boxes: list[Box]) -> Box | None:
+    """The highest cube in the image that sits on another cube; else the highest cube."""
+    cubes = sorted((b for b in boxes if b.cls.endswith(" cube")), key=lambda b: b.y0)
+    stacked = [c for c in cubes if any(o is not c and abs(o.y0 - c.y1) < 0.04 and o.x0 <= c.cx <= o.x1 for o in cubes)]
+    return (stacked or cubes or [None])[0]
+
+
+def top_cube_hole(boxes: list[Box]) -> tuple[str | None, float]:
+    """(hole axis, confidence) of the top cube, for the note 'last cube hole vertical before inserting the cylinder'.
+    Trust "vertical" only at confidence >= HOLE_SURE from the overhead camera. It informs; it does not gate anything."""
+    c = _top_cube(boxes)
+    return (None, 0.0) if c is None else (c.hole_axis, c.confidence)
+
