+"""The fast loop: perception + observer + state-space decisions at `rate_hz` (default 20), in its own thread.
+
+Each tick:
+  1. grab the newest live frames (GET /frame/<cam>.jpg, ~4 ms on the station) at a small width,
+  2. run the local classifiers on frames it has not judged before (colour detector guard on the wrists),
+  3. read the gripper sensors (GET /arms) every few ticks,
+  4. update the observer (fast measurements), and fold in any Claude verdict the runner handed over since the last
+     tick (slow measurements),
+  5. step the state-space model and publish the decision.
+
+mode "shadow" (default): logs what it would decide; the runner keeps its own rules.
+mode "live": a guard decision sends /stop at once (like the live guard), and pass / fail / stall are picked up by
+the runner on its next look (check_every_s can be 0.25 s), so a stage ends within one runner look of the evidence.
+The runner's own rules still run: whichever fires first ends the attempt.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import hashlib
+import threading
+import time
+from typing import Any, Callable
+
+from .statespace import Observer, StateSpace, Weights
+
+
+class FastLoop:
+    def __init__(self, *, weights: Weights | None = None, rate_hz: float = 20.0, mode: str = "shadow",
+                 frames: Callable[[], dict[str, bytes]] | None = None,
+                 guard_judge: Callable[[dict[str, bytes]], tuple[bool | None, str]] | None = None,
+                 grippers: Callable[[], dict[str, Any]] | None = None,
+                 stop: Callable[[], Any] | None = None, log: Callable[..., None] | None = None,
+                 clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic):
+        if mode not in ("shadow", "live"):
+            raise ValueError("fast.mode: shadow or live")
+        self.w = weights or Weights()
+        self.ss = StateSpace(self.w)
+        self.obs = Observer(self.w)
+        self.period = 1.0 / rate_hz
+        self.mode = mode
+        self._frames, self._judge, self._grippers, self._stop = frames, guard_judge, grippers, stop
+        self._log = log or (lambda *a, **k: None)
+        self._clock = clock
+        self._lock = threading.Lock()
+        self._pending: list[dict[str, float]] = []      # verdict inputs handed over by the runner
+        self._sig: dict[str, str] = {}
+        self._stage: str | None = None
+        self._guarded = False
+        self._last_t: float | None = None
+        self.decision: dict[str, bool] = {}
+        self.tripped: str | None = None
+        self.ticks = 0
+        self.tick_ms: list[float] = []
+        self._closed = threading.Event()
+        self._thread: threading.Thread | None = None
+
+    # -- runner hooks -------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def attempt_start(self, stage: str, timeout_s: float, stall_s: float | None, *, guarded: bool,
+                      monitor: bool = True) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            self._stage, self._guarded = stage, guarded
+            self.ss.start(timeout_s, stall_s, monitor=monitor)
+            self.obs.reset()
+            self._pending.clear()
+            self._sig.clear()
+            self._last_t = self._clock()
+            self.decision, self.tripped = {}, None
+
+    def attempt_end(self) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            self._stage = None
+
+    def verdict(self, *, done: bool | None, failing: bool, same_frames: bool, stalled: bool,
+                progress: float | None, guard: bool | None, holding: bool | None = None) -> None:
+        """A real monitor verdict landed (slow measurement). Applied on the next tick."""
+        with self._lock:
+            self._pending.append(dict(done=done, failing=failing, same=same_frames, stalled=stalled,
+                                      progress=progress, guard=guard, holding=holding))
+
+    # -- one tick -----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def step(self) -> dict[str, bool]:
+        t0 = self._clock()
+        with self._lock:
+            if self._stage is None:
+                return {}
+            pending, self._pending = self._pending, []
+            stage, guarded = self._stage, self._guarded
+            dt = t0 - (self._last_t or t0)
+            self._last_t = t0
+        l_yes = l_no = 0.0
+        if guarded and self._frames is not None and self._judge is not None:
+            for role, jpeg in sorted(self._frames().items()):
+                sig = hashlib.sha1(jpeg).hexdigest()
+                if self._sig.get(role) == sig:
+                    continue                     # a frame judged before is not a new look
+                self._sig[role] = sig
+                ans, _why = self._judge({role: jpeg})
+                if ans is True:
+                    l_yes = 1.0
+                elif ans is False and not l_yes:
+                    l_no = 1.0
+        fast_meas: dict[str, float | None] = {"illegal_grip": 1.0 if l_yes else (0.0 if l_no else None)}
+        if self._grippers is not None and self.ticks % 4 == 0:
+            g = self._grippers() or {}
+            closed = [a.get("opening") for a in g.values() if isinstance(a, dict) and a.get("opening") is not None]
+            if closed:
+                fast_meas["holding"] = 1.0 if min(closed) < 0.15 else 0.0
+        with self._lock:
+            self.obs.fast(fast_meas)
+            u = self.ss.u(dt=dt, l_guard_yes=l_yes, l_guard_no=l_no)
+            d = self.ss.step(u, self.obs.z)
+            for v in pending:
+                rise = self.ss.note_progress(v["progress"]) if v["progress"] is not None else False
+                self.obs.slow({"progress": v["progress"], "illegal_grip": None if v["guard"] is None else
+                               float(v["guard"]), "holding": None if v["holding"] is None else float(v["holding"])})
+                u = self.ss.u(dt=0.0, v_new=1, v_done=v["done"] is True, v_same=v["same"], v_failing=v["failing"],
+                              v_stalled=v["stalled"], v_rise=rise, v_guard_yes=v["guard"] is True,
+                              v_guard_no=v["guard"] is False)
+                d2 = self.ss.step(u, self.obs.z)
+                d = {k: d[k] | d2[k] for k in d}
+            dec = {k: bool(v[0]) for k, v in d.items()}
+            if any(dec.values()) and dec != self.decision:
+                self._log("fast_decision", stage=stage, mode=self.mode, **dec,
+                          z={k: round(float(x), 2) for k, x in zip(("progress", "holding", "at_goal", "illegal",
+                                                                     "cable_out"), self.obs.z)})
+            self.decision = {k: self.decision.get(k, False) or v for k, v in dec.items()}
+            trip = self.mode == "live" and dec["guard"] and self.tripped is None
+            if trip:
+                self.tripped = "fast loop guard"
+        if trip and self._stop is not None:
+            try:
+                self._stop()
+                self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, source="fast", stop="sent")
+            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+                self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, source="fast", stop=f"failed: {exc}")
+        self.ticks += 1
+        self.tick_ms.append(1000 * (self._clock() - t0))
+        if len(self.tick_ms) > 2000:
+            del self.tick_ms[:1000]
+        return dec
+
+    def start(self) -> None:
+        if self._thread is None:
+            self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._loop, name="fast-loop", daemon=True)
+            self._thread.start()
+
+    def close(self) -> None:
+        self._closed.set()
+        if self._thread is not None:
+            self._thread.join(timeout=2.0)
+
+    def _loop(self) -> None:
+        while not self._closed.is_set():
+            t = self._clock()
+            try:
+                self.step()
+            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a bad tick must not kill the loop
+                self._log("fast_loop_error", error=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
+            self._closed.wait(max(0.0, self.period - (self._clock() - t)))

+"""Hedged calls: send the same request N times at once and use the first good answer.
+
+Model APIs (Claude, Dream-1) have long latency tails: a Haiku 5.5 monitor call took 6.5-13 s for the same request
+(8 Nov). With N copies in flight, the answer arrives at the minimum of N draws instead of one draw, so the tail
+shrinks; the floor (the fastest a call can be) does not move. Cost and rate-limit use grow N-fold.
+
+`first_of` is generic: any function of no arguments that returns a result or raises. The losing calls keep running
+in their daemon threads and their results are dropped (HTTP calls cannot be cancelled mid-flight from here).
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import threading
+import time
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field
+from typing import Any, Callable, Generic, TypeVar
+
+T = TypeVar("T")
+
+
+@dataclass
+class HedgeResult(Generic[T]):
+    value: T | None
+    winner: int | None              # which copy answered first (0-based), None if none was good
+    latency_s: float
+    errors: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+    answered: int = 0               # copies that had finished when the winner was taken
+
+
+def first_of(fn: Callable[[], T], n: int, *, timeout_s: float = 60.0,
+             good: Callable[[T], bool] | None = None, stagger_s: float = 0.0,
+             clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic) -> HedgeResult[T]:
+    """Run `fn` `n` times in parallel; return the first result that `good` accepts (default: any result that did
+    not raise). If every copy fails or is rejected, returns the last good-or-not result with winner None.
+    `stagger_s` delays copy k by k*stagger_s (a smaller extra load when the first copy is usually fast)."""
+    n = max(1, int(n))
+    t0 = clock()
+    if n == 1:
+        try:
+            v = fn()
+            return HedgeResult(v, 0 if good is None or good(v) else None, clock() - t0, [], 1)
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+            return HedgeResult(None, None, clock() - t0, [f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"], 1)
+    cond = threading.Condition()
+    state: dict[str, Any] = {"done": 0, "winner": None, "value": None, "fallback": None, "errors": []}
+
+    def run(k: int) -> None:
+        if stagger_s > 0 and k:
+            time.sleep(k * stagger_s)
+            with cond:
+                if state["winner"] is not None:
+                    return               # someone already answered: don't spend the call
+        try:
+            v = fn()
+            ok = good is None or good(v)
+            err = None
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001
+            v, ok, err = None, False, f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}"
+        with cond:
+            state["done"] += 1
+            if err:
+                state["errors"].append(err)
+            elif not ok and state["fallback"] is None:
+                state["fallback"] = v
+            if ok and state["winner"] is None:
+                state["winner"], state["value"] = k, v
+            cond.notify_all()
+
+    for k in range(n):
+        threading.Thread(target=run, args=(k,), name=f"hedge-{k}", daemon=True).start()
+    deadline = t0 + timeout_s
+    with cond:
+        while state["winner"] is None and state["done"] < n:
+            left = deadline - clock()
+            if left <= 0:
+                break
+            cond.wait(min(left, 0.05))
+        w = state["winner"]
+        value = state["value"] if w is not None else state["fallback"]
+        return HedgeResult(value, w, clock() - t0, list(state["errors"]), state["done"])

+"""Learned joint fault detector: 8 numbers per joint reading in, 2 fault flags out.
+
+Per joint, at the harness's telemetry rate (20 Hz, harness.joint_telemetry), the last WINDOW readings become 8
+features each (features()): where the joint is in its range, how close it is to the nearer limit, how fast it moves
+toward that limit, how far it lags its command, how fast the command moves toward the limit, measured torque, commanded
+torque and their difference (both as a share of the joint's torque limit). A small network trained on Dream's sim
+matches with injected faults (tools/train_joint_fault.py) turns that window into two probabilities:
+
+  now    the joint is in a fault state: past a limit, blocked, oscillating, at its torque limit or over speed
+  soon   it is now, or will be within HORIZON_S (0.25 s): the look-ahead flag
+
+Inference is numpy only (the harness on the MacBook); the NUC keeps the rule observer (harness.joint_observer), which
+is stdlib. The weights file names the architecture the search picked (mlp, tcn, gru or transformer) and the
+thresholds chosen on validation traces; JointFaultNet runs it on live readings.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import json
+import math
+from collections import deque
+from pathlib import Path
+
+import numpy as np
+
+FEATURES = ("pos_frac", "margin", "v_toward", "track_err", "cmd_v_toward", "tau", "tau_cmd", "tau_diff")
+N_FEATURES = len(FEATURES)
+RATE_HZ = 20.0
+DT = 1.0 / RATE_HZ
+WINDOW = 16                      # 0.8 s of history
+HORIZON_S = 0.25
+HORIZON = int(round(HORIZON_S * RATE_HZ))
+MARGIN_SCALE_DEG = 30.0
+SPEED_SCALE_DEG_S = 200.0
+TRACK_SCALE_DEG = 10.0
+DEFAULT_MODEL = Path(__file__).with_name("models") / "joint_fault_net.json"
+
+
+def features(q, q_cmd, dq, tau, tau_cmd, lo, hi, tau_max, dt=DT, q_cmd_prev=None):
+    """(T, 8) float32 features from per-sample arrays (degrees, deg/s, N.m). `q_cmd_prev` is the command one sample
+    before the first (None: the command's first speed is 0)."""
+    q, q_cmd, dq, tau, tau_cmd = (np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64) for a in (q, q_cmd, dq, tau, tau_cmd))
+    span = max(hi - lo, 1e-6)
+    to_hi = hi - q
+    to_lo = q - lo
+    toward = np.where(to_hi < to_lo, 1.0, -1.0)            # +1: the nearer limit is the upper one
+    margin = np.minimum(to_hi, to_lo)
+    prev = np.concatenate([[q_cmd[0] if q_cmd_prev is None else q_cmd_prev], q_cmd[:-1]])
+    dt = np.broadcast_to(np.asarray(dt, dtype=np.float64), q.shape)
+    cmd_v = (q_cmd - prev) / np.clip(dt, 0.02, 0.2)
+    tmax = max(float(tau_max or 0.0), 1e-6)
+    out = np.stack([
+        np.clip(2.0 * (q - lo) / span - 1.0, -2.0, 2.0),
+        np.clip(margin / MARGIN_SCALE_DEG, -1.0, 3.0),
+        np.clip(dq * toward / SPEED_SCALE_DEG_S, -5.0, 5.0),
+        np.clip((q_cmd - q) / TRACK_SCALE_DEG, -5.0, 5.0),
+        np.clip(cmd_v * toward / SPEED_SCALE_DEG_S, -5.0, 5.0),
+        np.clip(tau / tmax, -2.0, 2.0),
+        np.clip(tau_cmd / tmax, -2.0, 2.0),
+        np.clip((tau - tau_cmd) / tmax, -2.0, 2.0),
+    ], axis=-1)
+    return out.astype(np.float32)
+
+
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------- numpy forward passes (match tools/train_joint_fault.py)
+
+def _relu(x):
+    return np.maximum(x, 0.0)
+
+
+def _sigmoid(x):
+    return 1.0 / (1.0 + np.exp(-x))
+
+
+def _layer_norm(x, w, b, eps=1e-5):
+    m = x.mean(-1, keepdims=True)
+    v = ((x - m) ** 2).mean(-1, keepdims=True)
+    return (x - m) / np.sqrt(v + eps) * w + b
+
+
+def _forward_mlp(p, cfg, x):                         # x (B, W, F)
+    h = x.reshape(x.shape[0], -1)
+    for i in range(len(cfg["hidden"])):
+        h = _relu(h @ p[f"l{i}.weight"].T + p[f"l{i}.bias"])
+    return h @ p["head.weight"].T + p["head.bias"]
+
+
+def _forward_tcn(p, cfg, x):
+    h = x.transpose(0, 2, 1)                         # (B, C, W)
+    k = cfg["kernel"]
+    for i, d in enumerate(cfg["dilations"]):
+        w, b = p[f"c{i}.weight"], p[f"c{i}.bias"]  # (Cout, Cin, k)
+        pad = (k - 1) * d
+        hp = np.pad(h, ((0, 0), (0, 0), (pad, 0)))
+        T = h.shape[2]
+        y = np.zeros((h.shape[0], w.shape[0], T), dtype=np.float64) + b[None, :, None]
+        for j in range(k):
+            seg = hp[:, :, j * d: j * d + T]           # (B, Cin, T)
+            y += np.einsum("oc,bct->bot", w[:, :, j], seg)
+        y = _relu(y)
+        if f"r{i}.weight" in p:                       # 1x1 projection on the residual when channels change
+            res = np.einsum("oc,bct->bot", p[f"r{i}.weight"][:, :, 0], h) + p[f"r{i}.bias"][None, :, None]
+        else:
+            res = h
+        h = y + res
+    last = h[:, :, -1]
+    mean = h.mean(axis=2)
+    return np.concatenate([last, mean], axis=1) @ p["head.weight"].T + p["head.bias"]
+
+
+def _forward_gru(p, cfg, x):
+    B, T, _ = x.shape
+    inp = x
+    for layer in range(cfg["layers"]):
+        wi, wh = p[f"gru.weight_ih_l{layer}"], p[f"gru.weight_hh_l{layer}"]
+        bi, bh = p[f"gru.bias_ih_l{layer}"], p[f"gru.bias_hh_l{layer}"]
+        H = wh.shape[1]
+        h = np.zeros((B, H))
+        outs = []
+        for t in range(T):
+            gi = inp[:, t] @ wi.T + bi
+            gh = h @ wh.T + bh
+            r = _sigmoid(gi[:, :H] + gh[:, :H])
+            z = _sigmoid(gi[:, H:2 * H] + gh[:, H:2 * H])
+            n = np.tanh(gi[:, 2 * H:] + r * gh[:, 2 * H:])
+            h = (1 - z) * n + z * h
+            outs.append(h)
+        inp = np.stack(outs, axis=1)
+    return inp[:, -1] @ p["head.weight"].T + p["head.bias"]
+
+
+def _forward_transformer(p, cfg, x):
+    d, heads = cfg["d_model"], cfg["heads"]
+    h = x @ p["inp.weight"].T + p["inp.bias"] + p["pos"][None, : x.shape[1]]
+    B, T, _ = h.shape
+    hd = d // heads
+    for i in range(cfg["layers"]):
+        pre = f"enc.layers.{i}."
+        a = _layer_norm(h, p[pre + "norm1.weight"], p[pre + "norm1.bias"])
+        qkv = a @ p[pre + "self_attn.in_proj_weight"].T + p[pre + "self_attn.in_proj_bias"]
+        q, k, v = np.split(qkv, 3, axis=-1)
+        q = q.reshape(B, T, heads, hd).transpose(0, 2, 1, 3)
+        k = k.reshape(B, T, heads, hd).transpose(0, 2, 1, 3)
+        v = v.reshape(B, T, heads, hd).transpose(0, 2, 1, 3)
+        s = q @ k.transpose(0, 1, 3, 2) / math.sqrt(hd)
+        s = np.exp(s - s.max(-1, keepdims=True))
+        s = s / s.sum(-1, keepdims=True)
+        o = (s @ v).transpose(0, 2, 1, 3).reshape(B, T, d)
+        h = h + o @ p[pre + "self_attn.out_proj.weight"].T + p[pre + "self_attn.out_proj.bias"]
+        a = _layer_norm(h, p[pre + "norm2.weight"], p[pre + "norm2.bias"])
+        f = _relu(a @ p[pre + "linear1.weight"].T + p[pre + "linear1.bias"])
+        h = h + f @ p[pre + "linear2.weight"].T + p[pre + "linear2.bias"]
+    h = _layer_norm(h, p["norm.weight"], p["norm.bias"])
+    return h[:, -1] @ p["head.weight"].T + p["head.bias"]
+
+
+FORWARD = {"mlp": _forward_mlp, "tcn": _forward_tcn, "gru": _forward_gru, "transformer": _forward_transformer}
+
+
+class FaultModel:
+    """A trained detector from its JSON file: probs(windows) -> (B, 2) probabilities [now, soon]."""
+
+    def __init__(self, spec: dict):
+        self.spec = spec
+        self.family = spec["family"]
+        self.cfg = spec["config"]
+        self.params = {k: np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64) for k, v in spec["weights"].items()}
+        self.mean = np.asarray(spec["feature_mean"], dtype=np.float64)
+        self.std = np.asarray(spec["feature_std"], dtype=np.float64)
+        self.threshold = dict(spec.get("threshold") or {"now": 0.5, "soon": 0.5})
+        self.persist = int(spec.get("persist", 2))
+        if spec.get("window", WINDOW) != WINDOW or tuple(spec.get("features", FEATURES)) != FEATURES:
+            raise ValueError("model was trained on another window or feature set")
+
+    @classmethod
+    def load(cls, path: str | Path = DEFAULT_MODEL) -> "FaultModel":
+        return cls(json.loads(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8")))
+
+    def logits(self, windows: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+        x = (np.asarray(windows, dtype=np.float64) - self.mean) / self.std
+        return FORWARD[self.family](self.params, self.cfg, x)
+
+    def probs(self, windows: np.ndarray) -> np.ndarray:
+        return _sigmoid(self.logits(windows))
+
+
+class JointFaultNet:
+    """Runs a FaultModel on live readings, one window per (arm, joint).
+
+    step(t, arm, joints) takes the same per-joint dicts as JointObserver.observe (q_deg, q_cmd_deg, dq_deg_s, tau_nm,
+    tau_cmd_nm, tau_max_nm) and returns alerts `learned_fault` (now) and `learned_fault_soon` for joints whose
+    probability stayed over its threshold for `persist` readings in a row. A repeated timestamp is not a new reading.
+    """
+
+    def __init__(self, model: FaultModel, limits_deg: dict, skip: tuple[str, ...] = ("gripper",)):
+        self.model = model
+        self.limits = limits_deg
+        self.skip = set(skip)
+        self._feat: dict[tuple, deque] = {}
+        self._last: dict[tuple, tuple] = {}
+        self._streak: dict[tuple, int] = {}
+        self.last_probs: dict[tuple, tuple[float, float]] = {}
+
+    def step(self, t: float, arm: str, joints: dict) -> list[dict]:
+        ready, keys = [], []
+        for name, j in joints.items():
+            if name in self.skip or name not in self.limits or j.get("q_deg") is None:
+                continue
+            key = (arm, name)
+            prev = self._last.get(key)
+            if prev is not None and t <= prev[0]:
+                continue
+            lo, hi = self.limits[name]
+            dt = DT if prev is None else t - prev[0]
+            row = features([j["q_deg"]], [j.get("q_cmd_deg", j["q_deg"])], [j.get("dq_deg_s") or 0.0],
+                           [j.get("tau_nm") or 0.0], [j.get("tau_cmd_nm") or 0.0], lo, hi,
+                           j.get("tau_max_nm") or 10.0, dt=dt, q_cmd_prev=None if prev is None else prev[1])[0]
+            self._last[key] = (t, j.get("q_cmd_deg", j["q_deg"]))
+            buf = self._feat.setdefault(key, deque(maxlen=WINDOW))
+            if not buf:
+                buf.extend([row] * (WINDOW - 1))     # pad a fresh joint with its first reading
+            buf.append(row)
+            ready.append(np.stack(buf))
+            keys.append(key)
+        alerts = []
+        if not ready:
+            return alerts
+        p = self.model.probs(np.stack(ready))
+        for key, (p_now, p_soon) in zip(keys, p):
+            self.last_probs[key] = (float(p_now), float(p_soon))
+            for kind, prob, head in (("learned_fault", p_now, "now"), ("learned_fault_soon", p_soon, "soon")):
+                sk = (kind,) + key
+                self._streak[sk] = self._streak.get(sk, 0) + 1 if prob >= self.model.threshold[head] else 0
+                if self._streak[sk] >= self.model.persist:
+                    alerts.append({"kind": kind, "arm": key[0], "joint": key[1], "p": round(float(prob), 3)})
+        return alerts
diff --git a/harness/joint_observer.py b/harness/joint_observer.py

+++ b/harness/joint_observer.py
@@ -0,0 +1,151 @@
+"""Joint look-ahead observer: flags a failure mode BEFORE the arm reaches an illegal joint state.
+
+Fed every joint reading (harness.can_live on the NUC: each motor reply, ~30 Hz per joint under Dream's 30 Hz Metal
+loop; the sim: every control step), per joint it keeps an alpha-beta-gamma filter on position, velocity and
+acceleration (blending in the motor's own velocity), projects `horizon_s` ahead and checks the projection and the
+present against the limits:
+
+  limit_breach        position outside its soft limit now
+  limit_approach      projected position past the limit within the horizon, while moving toward it (eta given)
+  overspeed           |velocity| over speed_frac of the motor's max speed
+  torque_saturation   |torque| over sat_frac of the motor's max for sat_s (pressing, stalled, lifting too much)
+  tracking_divergence command and measurement drifting apart for div_s while the command moves (blocked/collision)
+  oscillation         velocity sign flips >= osc_flips within osc_window_s at speed (unstable / buzzing)
+  fault               the motor's status nibble is a fault (robots.damiao FAULT_STATUSES)
+  overheat            a temperature at or over hot_c (the Metal gripper latches off at 55 C)
+
+Pure Python (stdlib) so it runs on the NUC and the match MacBook. The learned detector (harness.joint_fault_net, a
+small TCN picked by an architecture search; docs/JOINT_FAULT_NET.md) runs next to it in the harness.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import math
+from collections import deque
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field
+
+# Soft limits, degrees, from robots/metal.py JOINTS (dreamlabs 1.0.37); the gripper in motor degrees.
+METAL_LIMITS_DEG = {"shoulder_pan": (-160.0, 160.0), "shoulder_lift": (-180.0, 0.0), "elbow_flex": (0.0, 180.0),
+                    "wrist_flex": (-123.0, 81.0), "wrist_yaw": (-85.0, 85.0), "wrist_roll": (-145.0, 145.0),
+                    "gripper": (0.0, 137.5)}
+# The sim's YAM arms (i2rt yam.xml joint ranges), in the Metal joint names sim_station reports them under.
+YAM_LIMITS_DEG = {"shoulder_pan": (-150.0, 175.0), "shoulder_lift": (0.0, 209.1), "elbow_flex": (0.0, 210.0),
+                  "wrist_flex": (-90.0, 90.0), "wrist_yaw": (-90.0, 90.0), "wrist_roll": (-120.0, 120.0)}
+# Max speed per motor model, deg/s (robots/damiao.py MOTOR_LIMITS VMAX, rad/s).
+METAL_VMAX_DEG_S = {"shoulder_pan": 573.0, "shoulder_lift": 573.0, "elbow_flex": 573.0, "wrist_flex": 573.0,
+                    "wrist_yaw": 1719.0, "wrist_roll": 1719.0, "gripper": 1719.0}
+FAULT_STATUSES = set(range(8, 15))
+
+
+@dataclass
+class ObserverConfig:
+    horizon_s: float = 0.25
+    margin_deg: float = 3.0
+    speed_frac: float = 0.5
+    sat_frac: float = 0.85
+    sat_s: float = 0.3
+    div_deg: float = 8.0
+    div_s: float = 0.3
+    osc_flips: int = 4
+    osc_window_s: float = 0.6
+    osc_min_speed: float = 20.0
+    hot_c: float = 50.0
+    alpha: float = 0.6
+    beta: float = 0.3
+    gamma: float = 0.05
+    limits_deg: dict = field(default_factory=lambda: dict(METAL_LIMITS_DEG))
+    vmax_deg_s: dict = field(default_factory=lambda: dict(METAL_VMAX_DEG_S))
+
+
+class _JointFilter:
+    def __init__(self):
+        self.t = None
+        self.q = self.v = self.a = 0.0
+        self.sat_since = None
+        self.div_since = None
+        self.flips: deque = deque(maxlen=32)
+        self.last_sign = 0
+
+    def update(self, t: float, q: float, dq: float | None, cfg: ObserverConfig) -> None:
+        if self.t is None or t <= self.t:
+            self.t, self.q, self.v, self.a = t, q, (dq or 0.0), 0.0
+            return
+        dt = t - self.t
+        qp = self.q + self.v * dt + 0.5 * self.a * dt * dt
+        vp = self.v + self.a * dt
+        r = q - qp
+        self.q = qp + cfg.alpha * r
+        self.v = vp + cfg.beta * r / dt
+        self.a = self.a + 2 * cfg.gamma * r / (dt * dt)
+        if dq is not None:                       # the motor measures its own velocity: trust it half-way
+            self.v = 0.5 * self.v + 0.5 * dq
+        self.t = t
+
+
+class JointObserver:
+    """observe(t, arm, joints) per reading; returns the failure modes active now (list of dicts)."""
+
+    def __init__(self, config: ObserverConfig | None = None):
+        self.cfg = config or ObserverConfig()
+        self.filters: dict[tuple[str, str], _JointFilter] = {}
+
+    def predict(self, f: _JointFilter, horizon: float) -> float:
+        """Kinematic baseline: constant acceleration, its share capped so a noisy estimate can't dominate."""
+        acc = max(-abs(f.v) / max(horizon, 1e-3), min(abs(f.v) / max(horizon, 1e-3), f.a))
+        return f.q + f.v * horizon + 0.5 * acc * horizon * horizon
+
+    def observe(self, t: float, arm: str, joints: dict) -> list[dict]:
+        cfg, alerts = self.cfg, []
+        for name, j in joints.items():
+            q = j.get("q_deg")
+            if q is None:
+                continue
+            f = self.filters.setdefault((arm, name), _JointFilter())
+            f.update(t, q, j.get("dq_deg_s"), cfg)
+
+            def alert(kind, **extra):
+                alerts.append({"kind": kind, "arm": arm, "joint": name, **extra})
+
+            lo, hi = cfg.limits_deg.get(name, (-math.inf, math.inf))
+            if q < lo - cfg.margin_deg or q > hi + cfg.margin_deg:
+                alert("limit_breach", q_deg=round(q, 1), limit=(lo, hi))
+            else:
+                qh = self.predict(f, cfg.horizon_s)
+                for bound, sign in ((hi, 1), (lo, -1)):
+                    toward = f.v * sign > 0
+                    if toward and (qh - bound) * sign > -cfg.margin_deg:
+                        eta = max(0.0, (bound - q) / f.v) if abs(f.v) > 1e-6 else None
+                        alert("limit_approach", q_deg=round(q, 1), bound=bound,
+                              eta_s=None if eta is None else round(eta, 2), q_pred_deg=round(qh, 1))
+            vmax = cfg.vmax_deg_s.get(name)
+            if vmax and abs(f.v) > cfg.speed_frac * vmax:
+                alert("overspeed", v_deg_s=round(f.v, 1))
+            tau, tmax = j.get("tau_nm"), j.get("tau_max_nm")
+            if tau is not None and tmax:
+                if abs(tau) >= cfg.sat_frac * tmax:
+                    f.sat_since = f.sat_since if f.sat_since is not None else t
+                    if t - f.sat_since >= cfg.sat_s:
+                        alert("torque_saturation", tau_nm=round(tau, 2), tau_max_nm=tmax)
+                else:
+                    f.sat_since = None
+            qc = j.get("q_cmd_deg")
+            if qc is not None and name != "gripper":   # the gripper holds short of its command on an object
+                if abs(qc - q) >= cfg.div_deg:
+                    f.div_since = f.div_since if f.div_since is not None else t
+                    if t - f.div_since >= cfg.div_s:
+                        alert("tracking_divergence", q_deg=round(q, 1), q_cmd_deg=round(qc, 1))
+                else:
+                    f.div_since = None
+            sign = 0 if abs(f.v) < cfg.osc_min_speed else (1 if f.v > 0 else -1)
+            if sign and f.last_sign and sign != f.last_sign:
+                f.flips.append(t)
+            if sign:
+                f.last_sign = sign
+            if sum(1 for x in f.flips if t - x <= cfg.osc_window_s) >= cfg.osc_flips:
+                alert("oscillation", flips=cfg.osc_flips)
+            if j.get("status") in FAULT_STATUSES:
+                alert("fault", status=j.get("status"))
+            hot = max(j.get("temp_rotor_c") or 0, j.get("temp_mos_c") or 0)
+            if hot >= cfg.hot_c:
+                alert("overheat", temp_c=hot)
+        return alerts
diff --git a/harness/joint_telemetry.py b/harness/joint_telemetry.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..c6b8e14
--- /dev/null
+++ b/harness/joint_telemetry.py
@@ -0,0 +1,214 @@
+"""Joint telemetry in the harness: position, commanded position, torque and commanded torque of every joint, live.
+
+The Dream API returns none of this. On the station it comes from harness.can_live on the NUC (receive-only CAN
+listener, GET /joints); in the sim from sim_station's GET /joints (MuJoCo), in the same shape. A thread polls at
+`rate_hz` and turns the numbers into signals the rest of the harness uses:
+
+- `holding[arm]`: the gripper pushes against something (|torque| >= hold_frac of its limit) and is not fully shut:
+  it is holding an object. Measured, so more reliable than an image (into the monitor's gripper note);
+- `strain`: a joint at >= strain_frac of its torque limit (lifting, pulling a seated plug, pressing on the tower);
+- `blocked`: a joint lagging its command by >= blocked_deg while barely moving (pushing into something);
+- `contact`: measured torque differs from commanded by >= contact_frac of the limit (an outside force).
+A signal counts only after `persist` polls in a row, so one noisy reading never raises it. `note()` words the
+current signals for the monitor; `alerts()` returns new ones for the run log and error detection.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import json
+import threading
+import time
+import urllib.request
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field
+from typing import Any, Callable
+
+
+@dataclass
+class Thresholds:
+    hold_frac: float = 0.25
+    strain_frac: float = 0.6
+    blocked_deg: float = 8.0
+    blocked_speed_deg_s: float = 3.0
+    contact_frac: float = 0.3
+    persist: int = 3
+
+
+@dataclass
+class JointTelemetry:
+    url: str                                   # station or sim base URL; GET {url}/joints
+    rate_hz: float = 20.0
+    thresholds: Thresholds = field(default_factory=Thresholds)
+    log: Callable[..., Any] | None = None      # RunLog.event, for joint_alert events
+    fetch: Callable[[], dict] | None = None    # tests: replace the HTTP read
+    record_to: str | None = None               # append every reading as one JSON line (q, q_cmd, dq, tau, tau_cmd)
+    # Learned fault detector (harness.joint_fault_net) next to the rules: "auto" runs the shipped model only when its
+    # file says it beat the rule observer on held-out traces; a path runs that model; None turns it off.
+    learned_model: str | None = "auto"
+
+    def __post_init__(self):
+        self.latest: dict = {}
+        self.ok = False
+        self._streak: dict[tuple, int] = {}
+        self._active: set[tuple] = set()
+        self._new: list[str] = []
+        self._lock = threading.Lock()
+        self._stop = threading.Event()
+        self._thread: threading.Thread | None = None
+        from .joint_observer import JointObserver  # noqa: PLC0415
+        self.observer = JointObserver()
+        self._observed: dict[tuple, dict] = {}
+        self.learned = None                      # (FaultModel, {source: JointFaultNet}) when a model runs
+        if self.learned_model:
+            try:
+                from .joint_fault_net import DEFAULT_MODEL, FaultModel  # noqa: PLC0415
+                path = DEFAULT_MODEL if self.learned_model == "auto" else self.learned_model
+                model = FaultModel.load(path)
+                if self.learned_model != "auto" or model.spec.get("beats_baseline"):
+                    self.learned = (model, {})
+            except (ImportError, OSError, ValueError, KeyError):
+                self.learned = None
+
+    def _learned_step(self, snap: dict) -> list[dict]:
+        if self.learned is None:
+            return []
+        from .joint_fault_net import JointFaultNet  # noqa: PLC0415
+        from .joint_observer import METAL_LIMITS_DEG, YAM_LIMITS_DEG  # noqa: PLC0415
+        model, nets = self.learned
+        source = "sim" if snap.get("source") == "sim" else "station"
+        net = nets.get(source)
+        if net is None:
+            net = nets[source] = JointFaultNet(model, YAM_LIMITS_DEG if source == "sim" else METAL_LIMITS_DEG)
+        t = snap.get("t") or time.time()
+        return [a for arm, joints in (snap.get("arms") or {}).items() for a in net.step(t, arm, joints)]
+
+    def _get(self) -> dict:
+        if self.fetch:
+            return self.fetch()
+        with urllib.request.urlopen(self.url.rstrip("/") + "/joints", timeout=1.0) as reply:
+            return json.loads(reply.read())
+
+    def poll_once(self) -> None:
+        try:
+            snap = self._get()
+        except Exception:
+            self.ok = False
+            return
+        self.ok = bool(snap.get("arms"))
+        if self.record_to and self.ok:     # joint traces: training data for a learned look-ahead observer
+            row = {"t": snap.get("t"), "arms": {
+                arm: {name: [j.get(k) for k in ("q_deg", "q_cmd_deg", "dq_deg_s", "tau_nm", "tau_cmd_nm")]
+                      for name, j in joints.items()} for arm, joints in snap["arms"].items()}}
+            try:
+                with open(self.record_to, "a", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+                    fh.write(json.dumps(row, separators=(",", ":")) + "\n")
+            except OSError:
+                pass
+        raised = self._signals(snap)
+        # Look-ahead observer (harness.joint_observer): the source's own (can_live on the NUC, at the CAN reply
+        # rate) if it sends one, else run here on each reading.
+        observed = snap.get("observer_alerts")
+        if observed is None:
+            t = snap.get("t") or time.time()
+            observed = [a for arm, joints in (snap.get("arms") or {}).items()
+                        for a in self.observer.observe(t, arm, joints)]
+        observed = list(observed) + self._learned_step(snap)
+        for a in observed:
+            raised.add((a["kind"], a["arm"], a["joint"]))
+            self._observed[(a["kind"], a["arm"], a["joint"])] = a
+        with self._lock:
+            self.latest = snap
+            for key in raised - self._active:
+                self._new.append(self._words(key))
+                if self.log:
+                    self.log("joint_alert", kind=key[0], arm=key[1], joint=key[2], text=self._words(key))
+            self._active = raised
+
+    def _signals(self, snap: dict) -> set[tuple]:
+        th, seen = self.thresholds, set()
+        for arm, joints in (snap.get("arms") or {}).items():
+            for name, j in joints.items():
+                tau, tmax = j.get("tau_nm"), j.get("tau_max_nm")
+                if tau is None or not tmax:
+                    continue
+                frac = abs(tau) / tmax
+                if name == "gripper":
+                    shut = j.get("q_cmd_deg") is not None and j.get("q_deg") is not None and \
+                        abs(j["q_deg"] - j["q_cmd_deg"]) < 1e-3
+                    if frac >= th.hold_frac and not shut:
+                        seen.add(("holding", arm, name))
+                    continue
+                if frac >= th.strain_frac:
+                    seen.add(("strain", arm, name))
+                q, qc, dq = j.get("q_deg"), j.get("q_cmd_deg"), j.get("dq_deg_s")
+                if q is not None and qc is not None and dq is not None and \
+                        abs(qc - q) >= th.blocked_deg and abs(dq) <= th.blocked_speed_deg_s:
+                    seen.add(("blocked", arm, name))
+                tc = j.get("tau_cmd_nm")
+                if tc is not None and abs(tau - tc) / tmax >= th.contact_frac:
+                    seen.add(("contact", arm, name))
+        out = set()
+        for key in seen | set(self._streak):
+            self._streak[key] = self._streak.get(key, 0) + 1 if key in seen else 0
+            if self._streak[key] >= th.persist:
+                out.add(key)
+        self._streak = {k: v for k, v in self._streak.items() if v}
+        return out
+
+    def _words(self, key: tuple) -> str:
+        kind, arm, joint = key
+        if key in self._observed:
+            a = self._observed[key]
+            return {"limit_breach": f"{arm} {joint} is past its joint limit ({a.get('q_deg')} deg)",
+                    "limit_approach": f"{arm} {joint} will reach its joint limit in "
+                                      f"{a.get('eta_s') if a.get('eta_s') is not None else '?'} s",
+                    "overspeed": f"{arm} {joint} is moving too fast ({a.get('v_deg_s')} deg/s)",
+                    "torque_saturation": f"{arm} {joint} is at its torque limit ({a.get('tau_nm')} N.m)",
+                    "tracking_divergence": f"{arm} {joint} cannot follow its command (pushing into something)",
+                    "oscillation": f"{arm} {joint} is oscillating",
+                    "fault": f"{arm} {joint} motor reports a fault (status {a.get('status')})",
+                    "overheat": f"{arm} {joint} motor is hot ({a.get('temp_c')} C)",
+                    "learned_fault": f"{arm} {joint} is in a fault state (learned detector, p={a.get('p')})",
+                    "learned_fault_soon": f"{arm} {joint} is about to fault within 0.25 s "
+                                          f"(learned detector, p={a.get('p')})"}.get(kind, f"{arm} {joint}: {kind}")
+        j = (self.latest.get("arms") or {}).get(arm, {}).get(joint, {})
+        tau, tmax = j.get("tau_nm"), j.get("tau_max_nm")
+        load = f" ({abs(tau):.1f} of {tmax:.0f} N.m)" if tau is not None and tmax else ""
+        return {"holding": f"{arm} gripper is squeezing an object{load}",
+                "strain": f"{arm} {joint} is straining{load}",
+                "blocked": f"{arm} {joint} is blocked: it lags its command and is not moving",
+                "contact": f"{arm} {joint} feels an outside force{load}"}[kind]
+
+    def holding(self) -> dict[str, bool]:
+        with self._lock:
+            arms = (self.latest.get("arms") or {}).keys()
+            return {a: ("holding", a, "gripper") in self._active for a in arms}
+
+    def note(self) -> str:
+        """The current signals in words, for the monitor; '' when the feed is down or nothing stands out."""
+        with self._lock:
+            if not self.ok:
+                return ""
+            parts = [self._words(k) for k in sorted(self._active)]
+            for arm in sorted((self.latest.get("arms") or {})):
+                if ("holding", arm, "gripper") not in self._active:
+                    parts.append(f"{arm} gripper is not squeezing anything (joint torque)")
+            return "; ".join(parts)
+
+    def alerts(self) -> list[str]:
+        with self._lock:
+            new, self._new = self._new, []
+            return new
+
+    def start(self) -> "JointTelemetry":
+        def loop():
+            period = 1.0 / self.rate_hz
+            while not self._stop.is_set():
+                t = time.monotonic()
+                self.poll_once()
+                self._stop.wait(max(0.0, period - (time.monotonic() - t)))
+        self._thread = threading.Thread(target=loop, name="joint-telemetry", daemon=True)
+        self._thread.start()
+        return self
+
+    def close(self) -> None:
+        self._stop.set()

+"""Illegal-ball guard on live camera frames, in its own thread.
+
+The plan guard otherwise looks at debug-view trips (one every ~3.4 s on the station) once per poll (check_every_s,
+3 s in v4), so a held ball could be up to ~6 s old when judged, and pick-to-bin takes a few seconds. The station
+add-on (station_addons/sitecustomize.py) serves the controller's newest frame at GET /frame/<camera>.jpg, live at
+30 fps whether or not a run is going (checked read-only on the NUC, 7 Nov: 4 ms per frame). This thread runs the
+local detector's guard on each wrist camera at `rate_hz`; `confirm_frames` yes answers in a row on new frames of the
+same camera POST /stop at once, without waiting for the runner's next poll. The runner then ends the match as for
+any guard trip.
+
+Reads only GET /frame; the one thing it sends is /stop, and only while armed (a stage the plan guard covers).
+
+Safety rules (from the Codex review of the first version):
+- The guard never waits on the runner: it sets `tripped` and sends /stop at once. The runner checks `tripped` before
+  each /start, /prompt or /home and again after it; a trip it finds afterwards may have been overtaken by its own
+  command, so it sends /stop again (Runner._motion).
+- Arming has a generation: a judgment that started before a disarm (or a re-arm for another stage) is dropped.
+- Only a new frame counts as a look: the same bytes judged again neither add a yes nor refresh `last_answer_t`, so a
+  frozen camera shows up as blindness (plan guard max_blind_s), not as "all clear".
+- Yes answers are counted per camera, so motion in one wrist view can't confirm a frozen yes in the other.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import hashlib
+import threading
+from concurrent.futures import ALL_COMPLETED, Future, ThreadPoolExecutor, wait
+import time
+import urllib.error
+import urllib.request
+from collections.abc import Callable
+from typing import Any
+
+
+class LiveFrames:
+    """GET /frame/<role>.jpg from the station API for each role, all roles at once (one slow or dead camera must not
+    hold up the others: 4 ms per frame on the NUC, so `timeout_s` is short). A role that fails this tick is missing
+    from the result and is tried again next tick (a camera that recovers is used again)."""
+
+    def __init__(self, base_url: str = "http://127.0.0.1:8091", roles: tuple[str, ...] = ("wrist_l", "wrist_r"),
+                 *, timeout_s: float = 0.25):
+        self.base_url = base_url.rstrip("/")
+        self.roles = list(roles)
+        self.timeout_s = timeout_s
+        self.last_error: str | None = None
+        self._pool = ThreadPoolExecutor(max_workers=max(1, len(self.roles)), thread_name_prefix="live-frame")
+        self._pending: dict[str, Future] = {}
+
+    def _one(self, role: str) -> bytes | None:
+        try:
+            with urllib.request.urlopen(f"{self.base_url}/frame/{role}.jpg", timeout=self.timeout_s) as resp:
+                return resp.read()
+        except urllib.error.HTTPError as exc:
+            self.last_error = f"{role}: HTTP {exc.code}"
+        except (urllib.error.URLError, OSError, TimeoutError) as exc:
+            self.last_error = f"{role}: {exc}"
+        return None
+
+    def grab(self, wait_s: float = 0.05) -> dict[str, bytes]:
+        """The frames that are in within `wait_s`. A camera whose fetch is still running is not waited for: its
+        fetch keeps going and its frame is returned by a later grab, so a dead camera never slows a live one."""
+        for role in self.roles:
+            if role not in self._pending:
+                self._pending[role] = self._pool.submit(self._one, role)
+        wait(list(self._pending.values()), timeout=wait_s, return_when=ALL_COMPLETED)
+        out: dict[str, bytes] = {}
+        for role, job in list(self._pending.items()):
+            if job.done():
+                del self._pending[role]
+                data = job.result()
+                if data is not None:
+                    out[role] = data
+        return out
+
+    def probe(self, tries: int = 3) -> list[str]:
+        """Roles that returned a frame in at least one of `tries` grabs (startup check before relying on them)."""
+        seen: set[str] = set()
+        for _ in range(tries):
+            seen |= set(self.grab(wait_s=self.timeout_s + 0.1))   # let each fetch finish or time out
+            if len(seen) == len(self.roles):
+                break
+        return [r for r in self.roles if r in seen]
+
+
+class LiveGuard:
+    """Runs `judge({role: jpeg})` per camera at `rate_hz` while armed. `judge` returns (True/False/None, why) like
+    TableDetector.guard. `confirm_frames` yes answers in a row on new frames of one camera call `stop()` once and
+    set `tripped` to the reason. A no on new frames of that camera resets its count; None leaves it alone."""
+
+    def __init__(self, source: Callable[[], dict[str, bytes]],
+                 judge: Callable[[dict[str, bytes]], tuple[bool | None, str]],
+                 stop: Callable[[], Any], *, rate_hz: float = 10.0, confirm_frames: int = 3,
+                 log: Callable[..., None] | None = None, clock: Callable[[], float] = time.monotonic):
+        if confirm_frames < 1 or rate_hz <= 0:
+            raise ValueError("confirm_frames must be >= 1 and rate_hz > 0")
+        self.source, self.judge, self._stop = source, judge, stop
+        self.period = 1.0 / rate_hz
+        self.confirm_frames = confirm_frames
+        self._log = log or (lambda *a, **k: None)
+        self._clock = clock
+        self._lock = threading.Lock()           # guards the fields below
+        self._armed: str | None = None          # the covered stage's name while armed
+        self._gen = 0                           # bumped on every arm-from-disarmed and every disarm
+        self._count: dict[str, int] = {}        # per camera: yes answers in a row on new frames
+        self._sig: dict[str, str] = {}          # per camera: signature of the last frame judged
+        self.tripped: str | None = None
+        self.last_answer_t: float | None = None   # clock() of the last yes/no answer on a new frame
+        self.ticks = 0
+        self._closed = threading.Event()
+        self._thread: threading.Thread | None = None
+
+    # -- control -------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def arm(self, stage: str) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if self._armed is None:
+                self._gen += 1
+                self._count, self._sig = {}, {}
+                self.last_answer_t = self._clock()   # max_blind_s counts from arming, as the poll guard does
+                self._log("live_guard_armed", stage=stage)
+            self._armed = stage
+
+    @property
+    def armed(self) -> bool:
+        return self._armed is not None
+
+    def disarm(self) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if self._armed is not None:
+                self._log("live_guard_disarmed", stage=self._armed, ticks=self.ticks)
+            self._armed = None
+            self._gen += 1
+
+    def start(self) -> None:
+        if self._thread is None:
+            self._thread = threading.Thread(target=self._loop, name="live-guard", daemon=True)
+            self._thread.start()
+
+    def close(self) -> None:
+        self._closed.set()
+        self.disarm()
+        if self._thread is not None:
+            self._thread.join(timeout=2.0)
+
+    # -- one look ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def step(self) -> None:
+        """One tick: grab, judge each camera, count, maybe stop. The thread calls this; tests call it directly."""
+        with self._lock:
+            stage, gen = self._armed, self._gen
+        if stage is None or self.tripped is not None:
+            return
+        frames = self.source()
+        self.ticks += 1
+        for role in sorted(frames):
+            jpeg = frames[role]
+            sig = hashlib.sha1(jpeg).hexdigest()
+            with self._lock:
+                if gen != self._gen:
+                    return                  # disarmed or re-armed while looking: these answers are stale
+                if sig == self._sig.get(role):
+                    continue                # the same frame again is not a new look
+            answer, why = self.judge({role: jpeg})
+            with self._lock:
+                if gen != self._gen:
+                    return
+                self._sig[role] = sig
+                if answer is None:
+                    continue
+                self.last_answer_t = self._clock()
+                if answer is False:
+                    self._count[role] = 0
+                    continue
+                self._count[role] = count = self._count.get(role, 0) + 1
+            self._log("guard_yes", stage=stage, count=count, reason=why, camera=role, source="live")
+            if count >= self.confirm_frames:
+                self._trip(stage, gen, why)
+                return
+
+    def _trip(self, stage: str, gen: int, why: str) -> None:
+        with self._lock:
+            if gen != self._gen or self.tripped is not None:
+                return
+            self.tripped = why   # set before /stop: the runner sends nothing that moves an arm from here on
+        try:
+            self._stop()
+            self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, reason=why, source="live", stop="sent")
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - the runner's own stop follows on its next check
+            self._log("guard_tripped", stage=stage, reason=why, source="live", stop=f"failed: {exc}")
+
+    def _loop(self) -> None:
+        while not self._closed.is_set():
+            t = self._clock()
+            try:
+                self.step()
+            except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a bad tick must not kill the guard
+                self._log("live_guard_error", error=f"{type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
+            self._closed.wait(max(0.0, self.period - (self._clock() - t)))

+"""Read-only LiveKit camera observer for Hill Climb practice and evaluation."""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import argparse
+import asyncio
+import io
+import logging
+import math
+import os
+import time
+from dataclasses import asdict, dataclass
+from typing import TYPE_CHECKING
+
+from .log import RunLog
+from .plan import Stage, load_plan
+from .verifiers import MonitorVerifier
+
+if TYPE_CHECKING:
+    from livekit import rtc
+
+# Ported from codex/hill-climb-livekit (569607e) without that branch's plan changes, so the camera-name helper
+# lives here and a stage without required_cameras needs none.
+_ROLE_NAMES = {
+    "scene": "overhead", "top": "overhead", "overhead": "overhead",
+    "left": "wrist_l", "wrist_l": "wrist_l",
+    "right": "wrist_r", "wrist_r": "wrist_r",
+}
+
+
+def camera_matches(role: str, wanted: str) -> bool:
+    """Match YAM station and simulator names, or a custom camera role substring."""
+    actual = role.removeprefix("video.").removeprefix("image_window.")
+    expected = wanted.removeprefix("video.").removeprefix("image_window.")
+    canonical = _ROLE_NAMES.get(expected)
+    return wanted in role or (canonical is not None and _ROLE_NAMES.get(actual) == canonical)
+
+
+@dataclass(frozen=True)
+class ReceivedFrame:
+    event: rtc.VideoFrameEvent
+    received_at: float
+
+    def jpeg(self) -> bytes:
+        from PIL import Image
+
+        frame = self.event.frame
+        image = Image.frombytes("RGB", (frame.width, frame.height), bytes(frame.data))
+        # The SDK enumerates clockwise rotation in quarter turns.
+        if self.event.rotation:
+            image = image.rotate(-90 * int(self.event.rotation), expand=True)
+        buffer = io.BytesIO()
+        image.save(buffer, format="JPEG", quality=85)
+        return buffer.getvalue()
+
+
+class LiveKitCameras:
+    """Keep only the latest frame for each explicitly named camera."""
+
+    def __init__(self, publisher: str, tracks: dict[str, str]):
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        self.room = rtc.Room()
+        self.publisher = publisher
+        self._roles = {name: role for role, name in tracks.items()}
+        self._latest: dict[str, ReceivedFrame] = {}
+        self._streams: dict[str, tuple[str, asyncio.Task]] = {}
+        self._tasks: set[asyncio.Task] = set()
+        self._reconnecting = False
+        self._closing = False
+        self.closed = False
+        self.room.on("track_published", self._subscribe)
+        self.room.on("track_subscribed", self._start_stream)
+        self.room.on("track_unsubscribed", self._end_stream)
+        self.room.on("track_muted", self._mute)
+        self.room.on("reconnecting", self._pause)
+        self.room.on("reconnected", self._resume)
+        self.room.on("disconnected", self._disconnected)
+
+    async def connect(self, url: str, token: str) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        await self.room.connect(url, token, rtc.RoomOptions(auto_subscribe=False, connect_timeout=10))
+        for participant in self.room.remote_participants.values():
+            for publication in participant.track_publications.values():
+                self._subscribe(publication, participant)
+
+    def _subscribe(self, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication, participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        if (participant.identity == self.publisher and publication.name in self._roles
+                and publication.kind == rtc.TrackKind.KIND_VIDEO):
+            publication.set_subscribed(True)
+
+    def _start_stream(self, track: rtc.RemoteVideoTrack, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication,
+                      participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        if participant.identity != self.publisher or role is None:
+            return
+        previous = self._streams.get(role)
+        if previous is not None:
+            sid, task = previous
+            if sid == publication.sid and not task.done():
+                return
+            task.cancel()
+        self._latest.pop(role, None)
+        task = asyncio.create_task(self._receive(role, track, publication))
+        self._streams[role] = (publication.sid, task)
+        self._tasks.add(task)
+        task.add_done_callback(self._stream_done)
+
+    def _stream_done(self, task: asyncio.Task) -> None:
+        self._tasks.discard(task)
+        if not task.cancelled() and (error := task.exception()) is not None:
+            logging.getLogger(__name__).warning("LiveKit video stream failed (%s)", type(error).__name__)
+
+    async def _receive(self, role: str, track: rtc.RemoteVideoTrack,
+                       publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication) -> None:
+        from livekit import rtc
+
+        stream = rtc.VideoStream.from_track(track=track, capacity=1, format=rtc.VideoBufferType.RGB24)
+        owner = (publication.sid, asyncio.current_task())
+        try:
+            async for event in stream:
+                if (self._streams.get(role) == owner and not publication.muted
+                        and not self._reconnecting and self.room.isconnected()):
+                    self._latest[role] = ReceivedFrame(event, time.monotonic())
+        finally:
+            if self._streams.get(role) == owner:
+                self._streams.pop(role)
+                self._latest.pop(role, None)
+            await stream.aclose()
+
+    def _end_stream(self, track: rtc.RemoteTrack, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication,
+                    participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        active = self._streams.get(role) if role is not None else None
+        if active is not None and active[0] == publication.sid:
+            _, task = self._streams.pop(role)
+            self._latest.pop(role, None)
+            task.cancel()
+
+    def _mute(self, participant: rtc.RemoteParticipant, publication: rtc.RemoteTrackPublication) -> None:
+        role = self._roles.get(publication.name)
+        active = self._streams.get(role) if role is not None else None
+        if active is not None and active[0] == publication.sid:
+            self._latest.pop(role, None)
+
+    def _pause(self) -> None:
+        self._reconnecting = True
+        self._latest.clear()
+
+    def _resume(self) -> None:
+        self._reconnecting = False
+
+    def _disconnected(self, reason: object) -> None:
+        self._latest.clear()
+        self.closed = not self._closing
+
+    def sample(self, now: float, max_age: float, previous: dict[str, ReceivedFrame]) -> dict[str, ReceivedFrame]:
+        """Return a complete set only when each view is fresh and new to the caller."""
+        if self._reconnecting or not self.room.isconnected() or len(self._latest) != len(self._roles):
+            return {}
+        if any(now - frame.received_at > max_age or frame is previous.get(role)
+               for role, frame in self._latest.items()):
+            return {}
+        return dict(self._latest)
+
+    async def close(self) -> None:
+        self._closing = True
+        tasks = tuple(self._tasks)
+        for task in tasks:
+            if not task.done() and not task.cancelling():
+                task.cancel()
+        await asyncio.gather(*tasks, return_exceptions=True)
+        if self.room.isconnected():
+            await self.room.disconnect()
+
+
+async def observe(publisher: str, tracks: dict[str, str], url: str, token: str, log: RunLog, *,
+                  duration: float, interval: float, max_age: float, monitor: MonitorVerifier | None,
+                  stage: Stage | None) -> tuple[int, bool]:
+    cameras = LiveKitCameras(publisher, tracks)
+    previous: dict[str, ReceivedFrame] = {}
+    samples = 0
+    try:
+        await cameras.connect(url, token)
+        started = next_sample = time.monotonic()
+        end = started + duration
+        while time.monotonic() < end and not cameras.closed:
+            now = time.monotonic()
+            current = cameras.sample(now, max_age, previous) if now >= next_sample else {}
+            if not current:
+                await asyncio.sleep(min(0.1, end - now))
+                continue
+            samples += 1
+            previous = current
+            next_sample = now + interval
+            frames = {role: frame.jpeg() for role, frame in current.items()}
+            files = log.save_frames("livekit", frames)
+            log.event("livekit_sample", sample=samples, frames=[file.name for file in files],
+                      cameras={role: {"rtc_timestamp_us": frame.event.timestamp_us,
+                                      "receive_age_ms": round(1000 * (now - frame.received_at), 1)}
+                               for role, frame in current.items()})
+            if monitor is not None and stage is not None:
+                request_started = time.monotonic()
+                verdict = await asyncio.to_thread(monitor.check_now, stage, frames, now - started)
+                log.event("livekit_verdict", sample=samples, stage=stage.name,
+                          request_ms=round(1000 * (time.monotonic() - request_started), 1), **asdict(verdict))
+        return samples, cameras.closed
+    finally:
+        await cameras.close()
+
+
+def positive_seconds(value: str) -> float:
+    number = float(value)
+    if not math.isfinite(number) or number <= 0:
+        raise argparse.ArgumentTypeError("expected a finite positive number")
+    return number
+
+
+def main(argv: list[str] | None = None) -> int:
+    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)
+    parser.add_argument("--publisher", required=True, help="LiveKit participant identity that owns the cameras")
+    parser.add_argument("--track", action="append", required=True, metavar="ROLE=NAME",
+                        help="published video track name for overhead, wrist_l or wrist_r")
+    parser.add_argument("--seconds", type=positive_seconds, default=30, help="observation window after connecting")
+    parser.add_argument("--interval", type=positive_seconds, default=3, help="minimum seconds between samples")
+    parser.add_argument("--max-age", type=positive_seconds, default=1, help="max seconds since local frame receipt")
+    parser.add_argument("--runs-dir", default="runs_livekit")
+    parser.add_argument("--plan", help="optional harness YAML for an advisory verdict")
+    parser.add_argument("--stage", help="stage to assess in the observer")
+    parser.add_argument("--var", action="append", default=[], metavar="NAME=VALUE")
+    args = parser.parse_args(argv)
+    tracks = {}
+    for item in args.track:
+        role, _, name = item.partition("=")
+        if role not in ("overhead", "wrist_l", "wrist_r") or not name or role in tracks or name in tracks.values():
+            parser.error("--track needs a unique overhead, wrist_l or wrist_r role and a unique track name")
+        tracks[role] = name
+    if bool(args.plan) != bool(args.stage):
+        parser.error("--plan and --stage must be used together")
+    url, token = os.getenv("LIVEKIT_URL"), os.getenv("LIVEKIT_TOKEN")
+    if not url or not token:
+        parser.error("set LIVEKIT_URL and LIVEKIT_TOKEN")
+    try:
+        from livekit import rtc
+        from PIL import Image
+    except ImportError:
+        parser.error("install livekit and pillow to use the observer")
+    monitor, stage = None, None
+    if args.plan:
+        variables = {}
+        for item in args.var:
+            name, _, value = item.partition("=")
+            if not name or not value:
+                parser.error("--var expects NAME=VALUE")
+            variables[name] = value
+        try:
+            plan = load_plan(args.plan, variables)
+            stage = next((stage for stage in plan.stages if stage.name == args.stage), None)
+            if stage is None:
+                parser.error(f"unknown stage {args.stage!r}")
+            missing = [role for role in getattr(stage, "required_cameras", []) if not any(camera_matches(r, role) for r in tracks)]
+            if missing:
+                parser.error(f"stage requires camera roles {', '.join(missing)}")
+            monitor = MonitorVerifier(plan, history_frames=2, timeout_s=5)
+        except (OSError, ValueError) as exc:
+            parser.error(str(exc))
+    log = RunLog(args.runs_dir)
+    log.update_manifest(source="livekit", publisher=args.publisher, tracks=tracks, stage=args.stage)
+    try:
+        samples, disconnected = asyncio.run(observe(args.publisher, tracks, url, token, log, duration=args.seconds,
+                                                    interval=args.interval, max_age=args.max_age,
+                                                    monitor=monitor, stage=stage))
+    except KeyboardInterrupt:
+        print(f"Observer stopped. Logs at {log.path}")
+        return 130
+    except (rtc.ConnectError, TimeoutError) as exc:
+        log.event("livekit_connect_failed", error_type=type(exc).__name__)
+        print(f"LiveKit connection failed. Logs at {log.path}")
+        return 1
+    log.write_summary({"samples": samples, "disconnected": disconnected})
+    print(f"Saved {samples} LiveKit camera sets to {log.path}")
+    return 0 if samples and not disconnected else 1
+
+
+if __name__ == "__main__":
+    raise SystemExit(main())

diff --git a/harness/operator.py b/harness/operator.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..1d6b239
--- /dev/null
+++ b/harness/operator.py
@@ -0,0 +1,171 @@
+"""The operator talks to the harness in plain words while it runs (testing and tuning only: any human input during a
+match is a DQ).
+
+Each typed line goes to Claude with the plan and the run so far. Claude answers it and says what to do about it:
+end the current stage, skip later stages, send the robot a new instruction now, or keep a lasting note ("next time
+approach the tower from the left") that the monitor follows for the rest of the run and that is saved as a lesson
+for later runs. The runner applies the actions between checks; everything goes to events.jsonl.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import base64
+import json
+import threading
+from collections.abc import Callable
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field
+from pathlib import Path
+from typing import Any
+
+from .plan import Plan
+from .verifiers import valid_jpeg
+
+MAX_CONTEXT_EVENTS = 40
+SHOWN = ("stage_start", "prompt_change", "prompt_rewrite", "verdict", "attempt_end", "stage_skipped", "recovery",
+         "retry", "auto_reset", "station_restart", "guard_yes", "operator_input", "operator_reply", "tower_prompt")
+
+INSTRUCTION = """You are the operator's assistant inside a robot test harness. The operator is watching the robot and
+typed the message below. Read it as plain speech: it may be a question ("why did it pick the pink block?"), an order
+("skip the next step", "stop stacking, go to the balls"), or feedback on how to do things ("grip the plug lower next
+time"). Reply to the operator in 1-3 short sentences, then output JSON only between <json> and </json>:
+{"reply": str,
+ "end_current_stage": bool,          // true: give up the current stage now and go on with the next one
+ "skip_stages": [str],                // names of LATER stages to skip (exact names from the stage list)
+ "prompt_now": str|null,              // a new instruction for the robot to follow right now, max 200 characters
+ "note": str|null}                    // lasting feedback for how prompts and judging should go from now on
+This is a test run to tune the harness, not a match: do what the operator asks, even where a match rule would
+forbid it, and never argue. Only set an action the operator asked for. A question alone gets only a reply. When the operator wants to skip or
+move past what the robot is doing now, set end_current_stage true (skip_stages only lists LATER stages); when they
+ask for another task, also skip the stages before it. Set
+"note" only when the operator says how the robot or the judging should do things (it is kept for later runs), never
+for a skip, a stop or another one-off order; word it as their instruction."""
+
+
+@dataclass
+class Actions:
+    end_current_stage: bool = False
+    skip_stages: set[str] = field(default_factory=set)
+    prompts: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+    notes: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+
+
+def parse_reply(text: str, stage_names: list[str]) -> tuple[str, dict[str, Any]]:
+    start, end = text.rfind("<json>"), text.rfind("</json>")
+    if start < 0 or end < start:
+        return text.strip(), {}
+    try:
+        obj = json.loads(text[start + len("<json>"):end])
+    except ValueError:
+        return text[:start].strip(), {}
+    if not isinstance(obj, dict):
+        return text[:start].strip(), {}
+    out: dict[str, Any] = {}
+    if obj.get("end_current_stage") is True:
+        out["end_current_stage"] = True
+    skips = [s for s in obj.get("skip_stages") or [] if isinstance(s, str) and s in stage_names]
+    if skips:
+        out["skip_stages"] = skips
+    for key, limit in (("prompt_now", 300), ("note", 500)):
+        v = obj.get(key)
+        if isinstance(v, str) and v.strip():
+            out[key] = " ".join(v.split())[:limit]
+    reply = obj.get("reply") if isinstance(obj.get("reply"), str) else text[:start]
+    return reply.strip(), out
+
+
+class Operator:
+    """Collects the operator's lines, asks Claude about each in a background thread, queues the actions."""
+
+    def __init__(self, plan: Plan, log: Any, *, transport: Callable[[dict[str, Any]], dict[str, Any]] | None,
+                 model: str = "claude-haiku-5-5", lessons_path: Path | None = None,
+                 print_fn: Callable[[str], None] = print):
+        self.plan, self.log, self._transport, self.model = plan, log, transport, model
+        self.lessons_path = lessons_path
+        self._print = print_fn
+        self._lock = threading.Lock()
+        self._actions = Actions()
+        self._events: list[str] = []
+        self.status: Callable[[], str] = lambda: ""   # the runner's one-line state (stage, prompt, elapsed)
+        self.frames: Callable[[], dict[str, bytes]] = dict
+
+    # -- what the runner tells it --------------------------------------------------------------
+    def record(self, event: str, fields: dict[str, Any]) -> None:
+        """A condensed copy of the run's events, the context for the operator's questions."""
+        if event not in SHOWN:
+            return
+        keep = {k: v for k, v in fields.items() if k in ("stage", "prompt", "why", "reason", "done", "progress",
+                                                           "status", "holding", "ok", "error", "text", "next")}
+        with self._lock:
+            self._events = (self._events + [f"{event} {json.dumps(keep, default=str)}"])[-MAX_CONTEXT_EVENTS:]
+
+    def take(self) -> Actions:
+        with self._lock:
+            out, self._actions = self._actions, Actions()
+        return out
+
+    # -- the operator's lines ------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def submit(self, line: str) -> None:
+        self.log.event("operator_input", text=line)
+        self.record("operator_input", {"text": line})
+        if self._transport is None:
+            self._print("[operator] logged (no Claude key, so nothing is done with it)")
+            return
+        threading.Thread(target=self._handle, args=(line,), name="operator", daemon=True).start()
+
+    def _handle(self, line: str) -> None:
+        names = [s.name for s in self.plan.stages]
+        with self._lock:
+            context = "\n".join(self._events)
+        stages = "\n".join(f"  {s.name}: {s.prompt}" for s in self.plan.stages)
+        text = (f"Task: {self.plan.task}\nStages in order:\n{stages}\nNow: {self.status()}\n"
+                f"Recent events, oldest first:\n{context}\n\n{INSTRUCTION}\n\nOperator: {line}")
+        content: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
+        for role, jpeg in sorted(self.frames().items()):
+            if valid_jpeg(jpeg):
+                content += [{"type": "text", "text": f"newest {role}"},
+                            {"type": "image", "source": {"type": "base64", "media_type": "image/jpeg",
+                                                         "data": base64.b64encode(jpeg).decode("ascii")}}]
+        content.append({"type": "text", "text": text})
+        payload: dict[str, Any] = {"model": self.model, "max_tokens": 2000, "output_config": {"effort": "low"},
+                                   "messages": [{"role": "user", "content": content}]}
+        if self.plan.system_prompt:
+            payload["system"] = self.plan.system_prompt
+        try:
+            reply = self._transport(payload)
+            raw = "".join(b.get("text", "") for b in reply.get("content", []) if b.get("type") == "text")
+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - the operator's question must never end a run
+            self.log.event("operator_reply", text=line, reply=f"error: {type(exc).__name__}: {exc}")
+            self._print(f"[operator] no answer: {type(exc).__name__}")
+            return
+        answer, acts = parse_reply(raw, names)
+        self.log.event("operator_reply", text=line, reply=answer, actions=acts)
+        self.record("operator_reply", {"text": answer})
+        self._print(f"[claude] {answer}" + (f"  -> {acts}" if acts else ""))
+        with self._lock:
+            a = self._actions
+            a.end_current_stage = a.end_current_stage or bool(acts.get("end_current_stage"))
+            a.skip_stages |= set(acts.get("skip_stages", []))
+            if "prompt_now" in acts:
+                a.prompts.append(acts["prompt_now"])
+            if "note" in acts:
+                a.notes.append(acts["note"])
+        if "note" in acts:
+            self._save_lesson(acts["note"], line)
+
+    def _save_lesson(self, note: str, said: str) -> None:
+        if self.lessons_path is None:
+            return
+        try:
+            self.lessons_path.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
+            with self.lessons_path.open("a", encoding="utf-8") as fh:
+                fh.write(f"- {note}  (operator: \"{said}\", run {self.log.path.name})\n")
+        except OSError as exc:
+            self.log.event("warning", message=f"lesson not saved: {exc}")
+
+
+def load_lessons(path: Path | None) -> str:
+    """Earlier operator lessons, for the monitor's system prompt. '' when there are none."""
+    if path is None or not path.exists():
+        return ""
+    lines = [l for l in path.read_text(encoding="utf-8").splitlines() if l.startswith("- ")]
+    return "\n".join(lines[-20:])
