+"""Darsh's operator decisions (plans/operator_notes.yaml, from docs/decisions.pdf) applied to a plan.
+
+A plan opts in with `operator_notes: operator_notes.yaml` (path relative to the plan file). Applied to the raw plan
+mapping before validation, so the result is checked like any hand-written plan:
+  - each matched stage gets `technique` (how to do it) and `watch_for` (error detection: signs it is going wrong),
+    unless the plan already sets them;
+  - the retry rule: a stage that may be skipped (on_fail skip) gets 2 attempts, or 3 if it is worth
+    high_value_points or more. Pass-bar stages (retry_in_place) keep retrying: moving on would be a DQ;
+  - extra stages from `insert_before` (e.g. push the white ball off the table before the ball stages);
+  - the last cube of tower_target turned hole-up for the stick (`tower_target_last_cube`).
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import copy
+from fnmatch import fnmatch
+from pathlib import Path
+from typing import Any
+
+import yaml
+
+
+def load(path: str | Path) -> dict[str, Any]:
+    data = yaml.safe_load(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8")) or {}
+    if not isinstance(data, dict):
+        raise ValueError(f"{path}: operator notes must be a mapping")
+    return data
+
+
+def entry_for(notes: dict[str, Any], stage_name: str) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
+    for entry in notes.get("stages") or []:
+        if any(fnmatch(stage_name, pattern) for pattern in entry.get("match") or []):
+            return entry
+    return None
+
+
+def attempts_for(notes: dict[str, Any], entry: dict[str, Any] | None) -> int:
+    d = notes.get("decisions") or {}
+    points = (entry or {}).get("points", 0) or 0
+    if points >= d.get("high_value_points", 20):
+        return int(d.get("high_value_attempts", 3))
+    return int(d.get("attempts", 2))
+
+
+def apply_raw(data: dict[str, Any], notes: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
+    """A new raw plan mapping with the notes applied. `data` is not modified."""
+    out = copy.deepcopy(data)
+    stages: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
+    # New stages send new texts: only on a plan that allows free text (prompt_technique, base Dream-1). A plan
+    # that must send only recorded texts (a fine-tune-ready plan) gets error detection and the retry rule only.
+    inserts = (notes.get("insert_before") or {}) if out.get("prompt_technique") else {}
+    names = {s.get("name") for s in out.get("stages") or []}
+    for raw in out.get("stages") or []:
+        for extra in inserts.get(raw.get("name"), []) or []:
+            if extra.get("name") not in names:          # never twice
+                stages.append(dict(extra, _inserted=True))
+        stages.append(raw)
+    for raw in stages:
+        inserted = raw.pop("_inserted", False)
+        entry = entry_for(notes, raw["name"])
+        if entry:
+            raw.setdefault("technique", " ".join(str(entry.get("technique", "")).split()) or None)
+            raw.setdefault("watch_for", " ".join(str(entry.get("watch_for", "")).split()) or None)
+            if raw.get("technique") is None:
+                raw.pop("technique")
+            if raw.get("watch_for") is None:
+                raw.pop("watch_for")
+        if not inserted and raw.get("on_fail", "retry_in_place") == "skip":
+            raw["max_attempts"] = attempts_for(notes, entry)
+            raw["retry_skip"] = True   # without this, on_fail skip moves on after the first failed attempt
+    out["stages"] = stages
+    last = notes.get("tower_target_last_cube")
+    target = out.get("tower_target") or []
+    cubes = [i for i, piece in enumerate(target) if "cube" in str(piece)]
+    if last and cubes:
+        i = cubes[-1]
+        out["tower_target"] = list(target)
+        out["tower_target"][i] = str(target[i]).split(",")[0].strip() + ", " + last
+    return out

@@ -106,6 +110,26 @@ class Watch:
     max_fixes: int = 2
 
 
+_START_POSE_KEYS = {"reference", "tolerance_rad", "home_before", "home_pose", "max_homes"}
+
+
+@dataclass
+class StartPose:
+    """Compare the arms' joints with the starts in the teleop data before a stage's prompt (harness.start_pose).
+    Every check is logged. On stages matching `home_before` (name prefixes), a start farther than `tolerance_rad`
+    from every reference start sends the arms home first, if `home_pose` is itself a reference start for that
+    prompt, at most `max_homes` times per stage. Only for stages where a homing path can't knock anything over."""
+
+    reference: str
+    tolerance_rad: float = 0.35
+    home_before: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
+    home_pose: dict[str, list[float]] = field(default_factory=lambda: {"left": [0.0] * 6, "right": [0.0] * 6})
+    max_homes: int = 1
+
+    def homes(self, stage_name: str) -> bool:
+        return any(stage_name.startswith(p) for p in self.home_before)
+
+

     task: str

     check_every_s: float = 3.0

+    prompt_level: int = 1        # harness.promptgen level for base Dream-1 (1 = the plan's prompt as written)
+    fast: dict[str, Any] = field(default_factory=dict)   # harness.fastloop settings (rate_hz, mode, weights)
     verifier: str = "manual"

     tower_target: list[str] = field(default_factory=list)
     # Optional: name the next piece in the prompt instead of the stage's generic one. Shape word -> template with
     # <piece>, e.g. {"cube": "put the <piece> on top of the stack"}. The piece is the target's next entry after the
-    # tower the monitor last reported. Off (empty) for a fine-tune that only knows its recorded texts.

     tower_prompts: dict[str, str] = field(default_factory=dict)

+    # Optional weights for harness.decide's shadow run ({done: {...}, fail: {...}}); None = today's rules as weights.

@@ -0,0 +1,69 @@
+"""Layered prompt generation for base Dream-1: the same stage prompt with more or less context added, so the cloud
+sweep can find which level of context makes the policy do the task best.
+
+Levels (each includes the ones below it):
+  0  bare        the stage's action only, no safety prefix (Dream refuses cable work without the prefix:
+                 kept as the control)
+  1  prefix      + the plan's prompt_prefix ("no power" sentence), as the plans ship today
+  2  scene       + what the scene holds right now (the monitor's last report of what each gripper holds / the tower)
+  3  state       + where the task stands (attempt number, progress, what the robot is holding)
+  4  history     + why the last attempt failed, in the monitor's words
+  5  goal        + the match goal in one sentence (rubric: why this step matters)
+
+NEVER for a fine-tuned Dream-1: a fine-tune only follows its recorded task texts byte for byte, so the plan's
+prompts must stay as recorded (level 1 = what the plan already sends). Use with base Dream-1 (plans/hybrid_v4*).
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+
+GOAL = ("The goal is to finish this step cleanly so the next one can start: unplug cables, then build a free-standing "
+        "tower over four inches, then put golf balls in the bin, never an 8-ball.")
+
+
+@dataclass
+class Context:
+    prefix: str = ""
+    holding: dict | None = None        # monitor's last `holding` (arm -> object)
+    tower: list[str] | None = None     # monitor's last tower, bottom to top
+    attempt: int = 1
+    progress: float | None = None
+    last_failure: str = ""
+    technique: str = ""                # operator technique for this stage (plans/operator_notes.yaml), or ""
+
+
+def _strip_prefix(prompt: str, prefix: str) -> str:
+    return prompt[len(prefix):].lstrip() if prefix and prompt.startswith(prefix) else prompt
+
+
+def compose(stage_prompt: str, level: int, ctx: Context) -> str:
+    """The prompt at `level`. Level 1 returns the plan's prompt unchanged."""
+    core = _strip_prefix(stage_prompt, ctx.prefix)
+    how = " ".join(ctx.technique.split())
+    if level <= 0:
+        return f"{how} {core}".strip() if how else core
+    if level == 1:
+        if not how:
+            return stage_prompt
+        return " ".join(p for p in (ctx.prefix, how, core) if p)
+    parts = [ctx.prefix] if ctx.prefix else []
+    if how:
+        parts.append(how)
+    if level >= 2:
+        held = {k: v for k, v in (ctx.holding or {}).items() if v and str(v).lower() not in ("nothing", "none")}
+        if held:
+            parts.append("Right now " + ", ".join(f"the {arm} gripper holds {obj}" for arm, obj in held.items()) + ".")
+        if ctx.tower:
+            parts.append("The tower is, bottom to top: " + ", ".join(ctx.tower) + ".")
+    if level >= 3:
+        if ctx.attempt > 1:
+            parts.append(f"This is try {ctx.attempt} of this step.")
+        if ctx.progress is not None and ctx.progress > 0:
+            parts.append(f"The step is about {round(100 * ctx.progress)}% done.")
+    if level >= 4 and ctx.last_failure:
+        parts.append("Last try went wrong: " + ctx.last_failure.rstrip(".") + ". Avoid that.")
+    if level >= 5:
+        parts.append(GOAL)
+    parts.append(core)
+    return " ".join(p.strip() for p in parts if p.strip())

+
+    def _reset_and_wait(self, why: str) -> None:
+        reset = getattr(self.station, "reset", None)
+        if reset is None:   # a stand-in station without /reset (experiments.stress SimStation)
+            self.log.event("reset_unavailable", why=why)
+            return
+        lg = self.live_guard
+        if lg is not None and lg.armed:
+            # like /home, a /reset moves both arms and may not be stoppable once begun: never while the guard is armed
+            self.log.event("reset_skipped", why=why, reason="live guard armed")
+            return
+        self.log.event("reset", why=why)
+        self._motion(reset)   # checks operator stop, the deadline and a guard trip first, like every motion command
+        self._nap(1.0)   # let the station leave idle before waiting for it to come back
+        self._wait({"idle"}, START_TIMEOUT_S, "idle after reset")
+
+    def _maybe_reset(self, error: Any) -> None:
+        """Before restarting after Dream's transient IK error: /reset and wait for idle (at most AUTO_RESET_MAX)."""
+        text = _error_text(error)
+        if self._auto_resets < AUTO_RESET_MAX and any(k in text for k in RESET_ERRORS):
+            self._auto_resets += 1
+            self.log.event("auto_reset", error=_err(error), n=self._auto_resets, prompt=self._prompt)
+            self._reset_and_wait(f"auto reset {self._auto_resets} after: {_err(error)}")
+
+    def _status_line(self) -> str:
+        return f"stage {self._stage_name or '(none yet)'}, robot prompt: {self._prompt or '(none)'}, " \
+               f"{self._clock() - self._t0:.0f} s into the run of {self.plan.time_budget_s:g} s"
+
+    def _apply_operator(self) -> tuple[bool, list[str]]:
+        """Takes the operator's queued actions: notes go to the monitor, skips are kept. Returns (end the current
+        stage, prompts to send now)."""
+        if self.operator is None:
+            return False, []
+        acts = self.operator.take()
+        if acts.skip_stages:
+            self._skip |= acts.skip_stages
+            self.log.event("operator_action", action="skip_stages", stages=sorted(acts.skip_stages))
+        monitors = {id(m): m for v in self.verifiers.values() for m in (v, getattr(v, "second", None))
+                    if hasattr(m, "operator_notes")}
+        for note in acts.notes:
+            self.log.event("operator_action", action="note", note=note)
+            for m in monitors.values():
+                m.operator_notes.append(note)
+        if acts.end_current_stage:
+            self.log.event("operator_action", action="end_current_stage", stage=self._stage_name)
+        return acts.end_current_stage, acts.prompts

+    def _shadow_look(self, stage: Stage, attempt: int, elapsed: float, verdict: Verdict, failing: bool, monitor: bool,
+                     consec_stalled: int, last_rise: float | None, *, same: bool) -> None:

+        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001

     def _stage_prompt(self, stage: Stage, verifier: Verifier) -> str:

+    def _layered(self, stage: Stage, prompt: str) -> str:
+        """plan.prompt_level (harness.promptgen): add context levels to the prompt for base Dream-1. 1 = as is."""
+        level = int(getattr(self.plan, "prompt_level", 1))
+        technique = (stage.technique or "") if getattr(self.plan, "prompt_technique", False) else ""
+        if level == 1 and not technique:
+            return prompt
+        from .promptgen import Context, compose  # noqa: PLC0415
+        v = getattr(self, "_last_verdict", None)
+        ctx = Context(prefix=self.plan.prompt_prefix or "", holding=getattr(v, "holding", None),
+                      tower=self._tower, attempt=getattr(self, "_attempt_n", 1),
+                      progress=getattr(v, "progress", None), last_failure=getattr(self, "_last_fail_reason", ""),
+                      technique=technique)
+        out = compose(prompt, level, ctx)
+        self.log.event("prompt_layered", stage=stage.name, level=level, prompt=out)
+        return out

+        """Joint angles from GET /arms (add-on, any state), else from the newest trip (only while running: an

+    def _start_pose_gate(self, stage: Stage, prompt: str, why: str) -> bool:
+        """Logs how far the arms are from the training starts for `prompt`. On a home_before stage with a far start,
+        stops (if running) and homes the arms. Returns True if it homed, so the caller must /start afresh."""
+        cfg = self.plan.start_pose
+        if cfg is None or self.start_poses is None:
+            return False
+        st = self.station.status()
+        joints, source = self._joint_state(st)
+        chk = self.start_poses.check(prompt, joints, cfg.tolerance_rad, source)
+        self.log.event("start_pose", stage=stage.name, why=why, prompt=prompt, tolerance_rad=cfg.tolerance_rad,
+                       **chk.as_event())
+        if chk.status != "far" or not cfg.homes(stage.name):
+            return False
+        if self._homes.get(stage.name, 0) >= cfg.max_homes:
+            self.log.event("start_pose_home", stage=stage.name, done=False, reason=f"max_homes {cfg.max_homes} used")
+            return False
+        home = self.start_poses.check(prompt, cfg.home_pose, cfg.tolerance_rad, "home_pose")
+        if home.status != "ok":
+            self.log.event("start_pose_home", stage=stage.name, done=False,
+                           reason="the home pose is not a training start for this prompt either", **home.as_event())
+            return False
+        left = self.plan.time_budget_s - (self._clock() - self._t0)
+        if left < HOME_RESERVE_S:
+            self.log.event("start_pose_home", stage=stage.name, done=False,
+                           reason=f"only {left:.0f} s left; /home can't be cancelled once started")
+            return False
+        self._check_global()
+        self._homes[stage.name] = self._homes.get(stage.name, 0) + 1
+        self.log.event("start_pose_home", stage=stage.name, done=True, why=why)
+        if st.get("state") != "idle":
+            self.station.stop()
+            self._wait({"idle"}, IDLE_TIMEOUT_S, "idle after stop")
+        self._check_global()   # an operator stop, the deadline or a guard trip during the stop: hold, don't home
+        if self.plan.time_budget_s - (self._clock() - self._t0) < HOME_RESERVE_S:
+            raise _Fail("not enough match time left to home safely after the stop")
+        self._motion(self.station.home)

+"""Is the robot starting a stage from a pose the policy was trained to start from?
+
+VLAs lose more to an unfamiliar start pose than to anything else measured in LIBERO-Plus (arXiv 2510.13626,
+OpenVLA-OFT: robot initial state -59.9 points, camera -37.4, language -15.6). A fine-tune only saw the starts in
+its teleop episodes. The reference (tools/start_poses.py) is the first frame of every task segment in those
+episodes, keyed by the exact task text, which v4 prompts match byte for byte.
+
+Joint state comes from GET /arms `positions_rad` (station add-on; works while idle) or else from the newest
+debug-view trip's observation `state.left_arm` / `state.right_arm` (only while a run is going).
+Grippers are not compared: open or closed both occur at every start in the data.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+import json
+from dataclasses import dataclass
+from pathlib import Path
+from typing import Any
+
+SIDES = ("left", "right")
+
+
+@dataclass
+class PoseCheck:
+    status: str                    # ok | far | no_reference | no_state
+    distance: dict[str, float]     # per arm: max |joint - reference| (rad) to the nearest reference start
+    nearest: str | None            # the reference episode it was nearest to
+    source: str | None             # where the joint state came from
+    joints: dict[str, list[float]] | None
+
+    def as_event(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
+        return {"status": self.status, "distance_rad": {k: round(v, 3) for k, v in self.distance.items()},
+                "nearest": self.nearest, "source": self.source,
+                "joints": {k: [round(x, 3) for x in v] for k, v in (self.joints or {}).items()}}
+
+
+class StartPoses:
+    """prompt -> list of {"left": [6 rad], "right": [6 rad], "episode": "dataset/episode/segment"}."""
+
+    def __init__(self, by_prompt: dict[str, list[dict[str, Any]]]):
+        self.by_prompt = by_prompt
+
+    @classmethod
+    def load(cls, path: str | Path) -> StartPoses:
+        data = json.loads(Path(path).read_text(encoding="utf-8"))
+        by = data.get("by_prompt") if isinstance(data, dict) else None
+        if not isinstance(by, dict):
+            raise ValueError(f"{path}: expected {{'by_prompt': {{prompt: [start, ...]}}}}")
+        return cls(by)
+
+    def nearest(self, prompt: str, joints: dict[str, list[float]]) -> tuple[dict[str, float], str | None]:
+        """Per-arm distance to the reference start that is nearest overall (the larger of the two arm distances)."""
+        best: tuple[float, dict[str, float], str | None] | None = None
+        for ref in self.by_prompt.get(prompt, []):
+            d = {s: _linf(joints[s], ref[s]) for s in SIDES if s in joints and s in ref}
+            if not d:
+                continue
+            worst = max(d.values())
+            if best is None or worst < best[0]:
+                best = (worst, d, ref.get("episode"))
+        return (best[1], best[2]) if best else ({}, None)
+
+    def check(self, prompt: str, joints: dict[str, list[float]] | None, tolerance_rad: float,
+              source: str | None = None) -> PoseCheck:
+        if prompt not in self.by_prompt:
+            return PoseCheck("no_reference", {}, None, source, joints)
+        if not joints:
+            return PoseCheck("no_state", {}, None, source, joints)
+        dist, ep = self.nearest(prompt, joints)
+        if not dist:
+            return PoseCheck("no_state", {}, None, source, joints)
+        status = "ok" if max(dist.values()) <= tolerance_rad else "far"
+        return PoseCheck(status, dist, ep, source, joints)
+
+
+def _linf(a: list[float], b: list[float]) -> float:
+    n = min(len(a), len(b))
+    return max((abs(float(a[i]) - float(b[i])) for i in range(n)), default=0.0)
+
+
+def joints_from_arms(arms: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, list[float]] | None:
+    """{side: [rad]} from GET /arms when the add-on reports positions_rad."""
+    out = {}
+    for side in SIDES:
+        q = (arms or {}).get(side, {}).get("positions_rad") if isinstance((arms or {}).get(side), dict) else None
+        if isinstance(q, list) and q:
+            out[side] = [float(x) for x in q]
+    return out or None
+
+
+def joints_from_observation(obs: dict[str, Any] | None) -> dict[str, list[float]] | None:
+    """{side: [rad]} from a debug-view trip observation (`state.left_arm`, `state.right_arm`)."""
+    out = {}
+    for side in SIDES:
+        q = (obs or {}).get(f"state.{side}_arm")
+        if isinstance(q, list) and q and all(isinstance(x, (int, float)) for x in q):
+            out[side] = [float(x) for x in q]
+    return out or None
diff --git a/harness/statespace.py b/harness/statespace.py
new file mode 100644
index 0000000..9c176bc
--- /dev/null
+++ b/harness/statespace.py
@@ -0,0 +1,206 @@
+"""The stage decision logic as a matrix state-space model with an observer, fast enough to run at 20+ Hz.
+
+Two layers, both plain numpy and vectorised over K parameter sets at once (so a hill-climb can score thousands of
+weight settings on one logged run in one pass):
+
+1. OBSERVER (state estimation). The task state z = [progress, holding_target, object_at_goal, illegal_in_grip,
+   cable_out] is estimated from two kinds of measurement:
+     - fast, noisy local features every tick (colour detector, cable-end classifier, gripper sensors), and
+     - slow, more reliable Claude verdicts whenever one lands (every ~5 s, 6.5-13 s old).
+   z_hat <- F z_hat + L_fast (y_fast - H_fast z_hat)        every tick
+   z_hat <- z_hat + L_slow (y_slow - H_slow z_hat)          when a verdict arrives
+   (a fixed-gain Luenberger / steady-state Kalman form; the gains L are tunable weights).
+
+2. DECISION STATE. x = [done evidence, failing evidence, stalled evidence, guard evidence (monitor), guard evidence
+   (local), best progress, seconds since progress rose, elapsed, seconds since last counted done].
+     x <- (I - diag(r(u))) A x + B u            r(u) in {0,1}^n: which states an input resets
+   and decisions are threshold tests on y = C x. With the default weights this reproduces the runner's rules
+   (confirm-in-a-row, done window, min gap, fail-after-half-timeout, stall, guard counts); the tests check that.
+
+Why matrices: the update is a gated linear recurrence, i.e. one recurrent cell
+   h' = (1 - g(u)) * (A h) + B u,   g(u) = clip(R u, 0, 1)
+so the same weights run as numpy on the CPU (microseconds a tick) or can be exported as an RNN cell (`export`) and
+learned from logged sim runs once there is data to fit.
+"""
+
+from __future__ import annotations
+
+from dataclasses import dataclass, field
+
+import numpy as np
+
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- names
+X = ("done", "fail", "stalled", "guard_m", "guard_l", "pmax", "since_rise", "t", "since_done")
+U = ("dt", "v_new", "v_done", "v_same", "v_failing", "v_stalled", "v_rise", "v_guard_yes", "v_guard_no",
+     "l_guard_yes", "l_guard_no", "obs_release")
+Z = ("progress", "holding", "at_goal", "illegal_grip", "cable_out")
+XI = {n: i for i, n in enumerate(X)}
+UI = {n: i for i, n in enumerate(U)}
+ZI = {n: i for i, n in enumerate(Z)}
+BIG = 1e6
+
+
+@dataclass
+class Weights:
+    """Everything a hill-climb may tune. Defaults = today's runner (runner.py constants and plan v4)."""
+    confirm: float = 2.0            # done looks needed
+    window_s: float = 15.0          # a second done within this passes
+    min_gap_s: float = 3.0          # dones closer than this are one look (plan confirm_gap_s)
+    fail_confirm: float = 2.0
+    fail_min_fraction: float = 0.5  # monitor failing only counts after this share of the timeout
+    stall_confirm: float = 2.0
+    guard_confirm: float = 1.0      # plan guard confirm (monitor)
+    live_guard_frames: float = 3.0  # live guard frames in a row
+    w_goal: float = 0.0             # observer: object at goal adds to done evidence per second (fast path, off)
+    w_release: float = 0.0          # observer: gripper released at the goal adds this once
+    goal_conf: float = 0.9          # observer at_goal estimate above this counts as evidence
+    # observer gains
+    l_fast: float = 0.25            # how much one local measurement moves the estimate
+    l_slow: float = 0.8             # how much one Claude verdict moves it
+    decay: float = 0.98             # per-tick pull of the estimate toward "unknown" (0.5) without measurements
+
+
+# ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------ observer
+@dataclass
+class Observer:
+    """Fixed-gain observer over z (Z). Measurements are dicts name -> value in 0..1 (None/missing = not measured)."""
+    w: Weights = field(default_factory=Weights)
+    z: np.ndarray = field(default_factory=lambda: np.full(len(Z), 0.5))
+
+    def reset(self) -> None:
+        self.z = np.full(len(Z), 0.5)
+        self.z[ZI["progress"]] = 0.0
+
+    def predict(self) -> None:
+        # F: progress holds, beliefs relax toward 0.5 (unknown) between measurements
+        prog = self.z[ZI["progress"]]
+        self.z = 0.5 + self.w.decay * (self.z - 0.5)
+        self.z[ZI["progress"]] = prog
+
+    def _update(self, meas: dict[str, float | None], gain: float) -> None:
+        for k, v in meas.items():
+            if v is None or k not in ZI:
+                continue
+            i = ZI[k]
+            self.z[i] += gain * (float(v) - self.z[i])
+        np.clip(self.z, 0.0, 1.0, out=self.z)
+
+    def fast(self, meas: dict[str, float | None]) -> None:
+        self.predict()
+        self._update(meas, self.w.l_fast)
+
+    def slow(self, meas: dict[str, float | None]) -> None:
+        self._update(meas, self.w.l_slow)
+
+
+# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- decision model
+class StateSpace:
+    """x <- (I - diag(r)) A x + B u, decisions from y = C x. Vectorised over K weight sets (x has shape (K, n))."""
+
+    def __init__(self, weights: list[Weights] | Weights | None = None):
+        ws = weights if isinstance(weights, list) else [weights or Weights()]
+        self.ws = ws
+        self.K, n, m = len(ws), len(X), len(U)
+        A = np.eye(n)
+        B = np.zeros((m, n))            # u @ B  -> increments
+        R = np.zeros((m, n))            # u @ R  -> reset mask (clipped to 0..1)
+        B[UI["dt"], XI["since_rise"]] = 1.0
+        B[UI["dt"], XI["t"]] = 1.0
+        B[UI["dt"], XI["since_done"]] = 1.0
+        B[UI["v_guard_yes"], XI["guard_m"]] = 1.0
+        B[UI["l_guard_yes"], XI["guard_l"]] = 1.0
+        R[UI["v_rise"], XI["since_rise"]] = 1.0
+        R[UI["v_guard_no"], XI["guard_m"]] = 1.0
+        R[UI["l_guard_no"], XI["guard_l"]] = 1.0
+        self.A, self.B, self.R = A, B, R
+        self.x = np.zeros((self.K, n))
+        self.x[:, XI["since_done"]] = BIG
+        self.x[:, XI["since_rise"]] = 0.0
+        self.timeout_s, self.stall_s, self.monitor = 60.0, None, True
+        self.passed = np.zeros(self.K, bool)
+        self._stalled = 0.0
+        vec = lambda f: np.array([getattr(w, f) for w in ws], float)  # noqa: E731
+        self._conf, self._window, self._gap = vec("confirm"), vec("window_s"), vec("min_gap_s")
+        self._w_goal, self._w_release, self._goal_conf = vec("w_goal"), vec("w_release"), vec("goal_conf")
+
+    def start(self, timeout_s: float, stall_s: float | None, *, monitor: bool = True) -> None:
+        self.x[:] = 0.0
+        self.x[:, XI["since_done"]] = BIG
+        self.timeout_s, self.stall_s, self.monitor = timeout_s, stall_s, monitor
+        self.passed[:] = False
+        self._stalled = 0.0
+
+    def u(self, **kw: float) -> np.ndarray:
+        v = np.zeros(len(U))
+        for k, val in kw.items():
+            v[UI[k]] = float(val)
+        return v
+
+    def step(self, u: np.ndarray, observer_z: np.ndarray | None = None) -> dict[str, np.ndarray]:
+        """One tick. u: input vector (U); v_* inputs are 1 only on the tick a real monitor verdict lands
+        (v_new), l_* on a tick the local detector judged a new frame. Returns decision arrays of shape (K,)."""
+        x = self.x
+        # linear part: integrators (time, since_rise, since_done) and the guard counters with their resets
+        reset = np.clip(u @ self.R, 0.0, 1.0)
+        x[:] = (1.0 - reset) * (x @ self.A.T) + u @ self.B
+        pass_now = np.zeros(self.K, bool)
+        if u[UI["v_new"]]:                       # the runner's per-verdict branches, vectorised over K
+            x[:, XI["stalled"]] = x[:, XI["stalled"]] * 0 + (self._stalled + 1 if u[UI["v_stalled"]] else 0)
+            self._stalled = x[0, XI["stalled"]]
+            since = x[:, XI["since_done"]]
+            in_window = since <= self._window
+            if u[UI["v_same"]]:
+                pass                             # the same frames judged twice are one look
+            elif u[UI["v_done"]]:
+                ok = since >= self._gap          # closer than min_gap_s: one look, not two
+                recent = ok & in_window
+                x[ok, XI["fail"]] = 0.0
+                x[ok, XI["done"]] += 1.0
+                x[ok, XI["since_done"]] = 0.0
+                pass_now = ok & (recent | (x[:, XI["done"]] >= self._conf))
+            elif u[UI["v_failing"]]:
+                out = ~in_window                 # one flicker after a done does not undo it
+                x[out, XI["done"]] = 0.0
+                x[out, XI["fail"]] += 1.0
+            else:
+                x[:, XI["done"]] = 0.0
+                x[:, XI["fail"]] = 0.0
+        if observer_z is not None:               # observer fast path (weights 0 by default)
+            at_goal = observer_z[ZI["at_goal"]] >= self._goal_conf
+            x[:, XI["done"]] += self._w_goal * at_goal * u[UI["dt"]] + self._w_release * u[UI["obs_release"]]
+            pass_now |= x[:, XI["done"]] >= self._conf
+        self.passed |= pass_now
+        return self.decide(pass_now)
+
+    def decide(self, pass_now: np.ndarray | None = None) -> dict[str, np.ndarray]:
+        x = self.x
+        w = self.ws
+        t = x[:, XI["t"]]
+        no_done = x[:, XI["since_done"]] >= BIG / 2
+        fmin = np.array([v.fail_min_fraction for v in w]) * self.timeout_s
+        fail = (x[:, XI["fail"]] >= np.array([v.fail_confirm for v in w])) & ((t >= fmin) | (not self.monitor))
+        stall = np.zeros(self.K, bool)
+        if self.stall_s is not None:
+            stall = no_done & ((x[:, XI["stalled"]] >= np.array([v.stall_confirm for v in w]))
+                               | ((x[:, XI["since_rise"]] >= self.stall_s) & (x[:, XI["pmax"]] < 0.95)
+                                  & (x[:, XI["pmax"]] > 0)))
+        guard = ((x[:, XI["guard_m"]] >= np.array([v.guard_confirm for v in w]))
+                 | (x[:, XI["guard_l"]] >= np.array([v.live_guard_frames for v in w])))
+        timeout = t >= self.timeout_s
+        return {"pass": pass_now if pass_now is not None else np.zeros(self.K, bool), "fail": fail, "stall": stall,
+                "guard": guard, "timeout": timeout}
+
+    def note_progress(self, p: float, rise: float = 0.05) -> bool:
+        """Feature stage: returns v_rise (progress rose by `rise` over the best so far) and keeps pmax."""
+        rose = p >= self.x[0, XI["pmax"]] + rise
+        if p > self.x[0, XI["pmax"]]:
+            self.x[:, XI["pmax"]] = p
+        return bool(rose)
+
+    # -- as a neural network ------------------------------------------------------------------------------
+    def export(self, path: str) -> None:
+        """Saves the recurrent cell: h' = (1 - clip(u R, 0, 1)) * (h A^T) + u B, plus the thresholds per weight
+        set. Loadable with numpy, or as the weights of a gated RNN cell in any framework."""
+        thr = np.array([[w.confirm, w.window_s, w.min_gap_s, w.fail_confirm, w.fail_min_fraction, w.stall_confirm,
+                         w.guard_confirm, w.live_guard_frames, w.w_goal, w.w_release, w.goal_conf] for w in self.ws])
+        np.savez(path, A=self.A, B=self.B, R=self.R, thresholds=thr, x_names=np.array(X), u_names=np.array(U))

     frames_sig: str | None = None  # digest of the newest frames this verdict was computed on
     tower: list[str] | None = None  # the tower the monitor sees, bottom to top (stacking stages with a tower_target)
+    next_prompt: str | None = None  # the monitor's own instruction for the robot (plan.rewrite_prompts)
+    latency_s: float | None = None  # how long the Claude call took

 def valid_jpeg(data: bytes) -> bool:
@@ -48,6 +52,50 @@ def valid_jpeg(data: bytes) -> bool:
     return isinstance(data, (bytes, bytearray)) and len(data) > 4 and data[:2] == b"\xff\xd8" and b"\xff\xd9" in data[-32:]
 
 
+STITCH_TILE_W = 384     # 4 tiles a row: 1536 px wide, under the API's 1568 px long edge, so nothing is rescaled
+STITCH_COLS = 4
+
+
+def stitch(images: list[tuple[str, bytes]]) -> bytes | None:
+    """All the check's frames as one labelled grid JPEG (tile n shows "n. label"), in order. One mid-size image costs
+    the model far fewer tokens than ~10 separate ones, so the answer comes sooner. None when Pillow can't decode."""
+    try:
+        import io
+
+        from PIL import Image, ImageDraw, ImageFont
+    except ImportError:
+        return None
+    tiles = []
+    for label, jpeg in images:
+        try:
+            im = Image.open(io.BytesIO(jpeg)).convert("RGB")
+        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - a bad frame is left out, not fatal
+            continue
+        h = max(1, round(im.height * STITCH_TILE_W / im.width))
+        tiles.append((label, im.resize((STITCH_TILE_W, h))))
+    if not tiles:
+        return None
+    th = max(t.height for _, t in tiles)
+    rows = (len(tiles) + STITCH_COLS - 1) // STITCH_COLS
+    cols = min(STITCH_COLS, len(tiles))
+    sheet = Image.new("RGB", (cols * STITCH_TILE_W + (cols - 1) * 4, rows * th + (rows - 1) * 4), "white")
+    draw = ImageDraw.Draw(sheet)
+    try:
+        font = ImageFont.load_default(size=15)
+    except TypeError:
+        font = ImageFont.load_default()
+    for i, (label, im) in enumerate(tiles):
+        x, y = (i % STITCH_COLS) * (STITCH_TILE_W + 4), (i // STITCH_COLS) * (th + 4)
+        sheet.paste(im, (x, y))
+        text = f"{i + 1}. {label}"
+        box = draw.textbbox((x + 4, y + 3), text, font=font)
+        draw.rectangle((box[0] - 3, box[1] - 2, box[2] + 3, box[3] + 2), fill="black")
+        draw.text((x + 4, y + 3), text, fill="white", font=font)
+    out = io.BytesIO()
+    sheet.save(out, "JPEG", quality=85)
+    return out.getvalue()
+

+OPENROUTER_URL = "https://openrouter.ai/api/v1/chat/completions"
+
+
+def to_openai_messages(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
+    """Anthropic Messages payload -> OpenAI chat messages (system first; image blocks as data URLs)."""
+    out: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
+    system = payload.get("system")
+    if system:
+        out.append({"role": "system", "content": system if isinstance(system, str) else
+                    " ".join(b.get("text", "") for b in system if isinstance(b, dict))})
+    for m in payload.get("messages", []):
+        content = m.get("content")
+        if isinstance(content, str):
+            out.append({"role": m["role"], "content": content})
+            continue
+        parts = []
+        for b in content or []:
+            if b.get("type") == "text":
+                parts.append({"type": "text", "text": b.get("text", "")})
+            elif b.get("type") == "image":
+                src = b.get("source") or {}
+                parts.append({"type": "image_url", "image_url": {
+                    "url": f"data:{src.get('media_type', 'image/jpeg')};base64,{src.get('data', '')}"}})
+        out.append({"role": m["role"], "content": parts})
+    return out
+
+
+REASONING_LEVELS = ("none", "low", "medium", "high")
+
+
+def openrouter_reasoning(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
+    """OpenRouter's unified `reasoning` field. $HARNESS_OPENROUTER_REASONING (none|low|medium|high) wins; otherwise
+    the effort the harness asks Claude for (output_config.effort, `low` in the monitor) is passed on, so every model
+    reasons at the same requested level. Measured 8 Nov: with no field, Gemini Flash-Lite does not reason at all,
+    Haiku 5.5 reasons a little and GPT-5 nano a lot; GPT-5 nano refuses `none`."""
+    level = (os.environ.get("HARNESS_OPENROUTER_REASONING") or
+             str((payload.get("output_config") or {}).get("effort") or "")).strip().lower()
+    if not level:
+        return None
+    if level not in REASONING_LEVELS:
+        raise ValueError(f"reasoning level must be one of {REASONING_LEVELS}, not {level!r}")
+    return {"enabled": False} if level == "none" else {"effort": level}
+
+
+def openrouter_request_body(model: str, payload: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
+    """The credential-free wire body, shared with offline request validation."""
+    body = {"model": model, "messages": to_openai_messages(payload),
+            "max_tokens": int(payload.get("max_tokens", 1024))}
+    reasoning = openrouter_reasoning(payload)
+    if reasoning is not None:
+        body["reasoning"] = reasoning
+    return body
+
+
+def openrouter_transport(models: list[str] | None = None, api_key: str | None = None,
+                         timeout_s: float = 60.0,
+                         on_attempt: Callable[[dict[str, Any]], None] | None = None,
+                         mode: str = "race") -> Transport:
+    """The same monitor request sent to other multimodal models through OpenRouter (OPENROUTER_API_KEY).
+
+    Models: `models`, else $HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS (comma-separated OpenRouter ids). With more than one model,
+    every call races them (harness.hedge.first_of) and takes the first reply that has text: a fast model answers
+    first when it can, a slower, more reliable one covers when it can't. The key goes only in the Authorization
+    header to openrouter.ai and is never logged. Optional on_attempt receives accounting for every HTTP attempt,
+    including empty responses and losing calls, from worker threads. It must be thread-safe and must not raise.
+    Only model, usage, response ID, provider, latency and exception type are included, never request headers."""
+    key = api_key or os.environ.get("OPENROUTER_API_KEY")
+    if not key:
+        raise ValueError("the openrouter backend needs OPENROUTER_API_KEY")
+    names = models or [m.strip() for m in os.environ.get("HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS", "").split(",") if m.strip()]
+    if not names:
+        raise ValueError("the openrouter backend needs model ids in HARNESS_OPENROUTER_MODELS (comma-separated)")
+
+    def one(model: str, payload: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
+        started = time.monotonic()
+        reply: dict[str, Any] = {}
+        error = None
+        try:
+            body = openrouter_request_body(model, payload)
+            req = urllib.request.Request(
+                OPENROUTER_URL, data=json.dumps(body).encode("utf-8"), method="POST",
+                headers={"Authorization": f"Bearer {key}", "content-type": "application/json",
+                         "X-Title": "hill-climb-harness"})
+            with urllib.request.urlopen(req, timeout=timeout_s) as resp:
+                decoded = json.loads(resp.read())
+                if not isinstance(decoded, dict):
+                    raise ValueError("OpenRouter response must be an object")
+                reply = decoded
+            text = ((reply.get("choices") or [{}])[0].get("message") or {}).get("content") or ""
+            if isinstance(text, list):
+                text = "".join(p.get("text", "") for p in text if isinstance(p, dict))
+            if not text.strip():
+                raise RuntimeError(f"{model}: empty reply")
+            return {"content": [{"type": "text", "text": text}], "usage": reply.get("usage"), "model": model}
+        except Exception as exc:
+            error = type(exc).__name__
+            raise
+        finally:
+            if on_attempt is not None:
+                on_attempt({"model": model, "usage": reply.get("usage"), "id": reply.get("id"),
+                            "provider": reply.get("provider"), "latency_s": time.monotonic() - started,
+                            "error_type": error})
+
+    if mode not in ("race", "alternate"):
+        raise ValueError("openrouter mode: race or alternate")
+    turn = {"k": 0}
+    turn_lock = threading.Lock()
+
+    def send(payload: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
+        if len(names) == 1:
+            return one(names[0], payload)
+        if mode == "alternate":
+            # Each call goes to the next model in turn. With claude.parallel 2 and confirm 2, two "done" verdicts in
+            # a row then come from two different models: a fast model proposes, the other one confirms.
+            with turn_lock:
+                k = turn["k"] % len(names)
+                turn["k"] += 1
+            return one(names[k], payload)
+        from .hedge import first_of  # noqa: PLC0415
+        it = iter(range(len(names)))
+        lock = threading.Lock()
+
+        def next_call() -> dict[str, Any]:
+            with lock:
+                k = next(it)
+            return one(names[k], payload)
+        res = first_of(next_call, len(names), timeout_s=timeout_s)
+        if res.value is None:
+            raise RuntimeError("all OpenRouter models failed: " + "; ".join(res.errors)[:300])
+        return res.value
+
+    return send
+
+

+# Hybrid Hill Climb plan for ZERO-SHOT base Dream-1 (hillclimb_rubric_v4, 7-8 Nov 2026).
+#
+# Same match structure, pass bars, guard, tower target and 295 s clock as hill_climb_v4.yaml. What differs:
+# - Specific prompts. Base Dream-1 follows short instructions that name objects by colour and position ("pick up the
+#   pink cube ... onto the blue cube") far better than the generic task texts, which are written for a fine-tune.
+# - The monitor writes the robot's next prompt (rewrite_prompts): when it sees the robot on the wrong object or step,
+#   stuck, or about to knock the tower, its diagnosis becomes the next instruction (cooldown 15 s, max 3 an attempt;
+#   runner.REWRITE_COOLDOWN_S / REWRITE_MAX). The station lessons it follows are in system_prompt below.
+# - reset_first: /reset before the first stage, so both grippers start open (a run that started shut grasped nothing).
+# - Longer stall windows on the stacking stages (20 s cut attempts short while Dream was still lining up).
+#
+# FINE-TUNE NOTE: a fine-tune only follows the texts it was trained on. If we fine-tune, the recorded task labels must
+# match whatever prompts we settle on here (stage prompts, tower_prompts and the rewrites logged as prompt_rewrite in
+# events.jsonl), or switch to hill_climb_v4.yaml and turn rewrite_prompts and tower_prompts off.
+#
+# Cable section: the stages named cable_*. In a match they are required (touching a block before a cable is out is a
+# DQ), but cables 3 and 4 don't fit in 295 s: match with --skip cable_extra. For stack + balls practice: --skip cable_
+# For all four cables with time to spare in practice: --budget 600
+#
+# Operator: while it runs you can type plain words ("skip the next step", "why did it grab that block?", "lower the
+# block more slowly next time"); see harness/operator.py. Testing only: in a match any human input is a DQ, so run
+# matches with --no-operator.
+task: >-
+  Hill Climb relay. Stage 1: unplug a USB cable from the power strip and put it down away from the strip (then one
+  more the same way). Never plug a cable in. Stage 2: stack the blocks into a free-standing tower more than 4 inches
+  (10 cm) tall. Do not touch the blocks before a cable is unplugged. Stage 3: put {ball}s into the white bin, never
+  the white ball or a black ball, without touching the tower. Do not touch the balls before the tower passes.
+# Base Dream-1 by default. The fine-tune of 7-8 Nov: --var model=dream-1-ft:bb4b9e5f-relay-final --no-rewrite
+# (only with that account's DREAM_API_KEY in dreamcli's shell).
+checkpoint: "{model}"
+time_budget_s: 295
+check_every_s: 3.0
+# Darsh's operator decisions (docs/decisions.pdf): per-stage technique + error detection, the retry rule
+# (2 tries, 3 for high points), push the white ball off first. prompt_technique adds the technique to the
+# Dream prompt: base Dream-1 only (a fine-tune needs its recorded texts byte for byte; set it false then).
+operator_notes: operator_notes.yaml
+prompt_technique: true
+verifier: monitor
+confirm: 2
+done_message: "ALL STAGES DONE: CALL STOP NOW"
+# Cable prompts. Zero-shot defaults below; for a fine-tune pass its recorded task texts (TELEOP_PLAN T1/T2), e.g.
+#   --checkpoint dream-1-ft:<account>-relay-v1 --no-rewrite
+#   --var unplug="unplug the USB cable at the end of the hub nearest the right arm"
+#   --var put_down="put the held cable down on the table away from the hub"
+vars:
+  model: dream-1-ft:bb4b9e5f-relay-final
+  stack_cam: wrist_r
+  ball: coloured golf ball   # green, yellow or orange (Darsh's decisions); the white ball and black balls are illegal
+  unplug: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires. With the right arm, grip the hard plug body of the rightmost USB cable still plugged into the power strip, pull it straight up out of its port and hold it up above the strip."
+  put_down: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires. Put the held cable down on the table to the right, away from the power strip, and open the gripper."
+# history_frames 4 not 6, one stitched grid image per check, two calls in flight: a verdict every ~5 s, not ~10
+claude: {model: claude-haiku-5-5, max_tokens: 4000, history_frames: 4, stitch: true, parallel: 2}
+reset_first: true
+rewrite_prompts: true
+prompt_prefix: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires."
+
+guard:
+  question: "Is either gripper holding the white ball or a black ball (balls that must never go in the bin), or closed or closing around one?"
+  stages: [ball_]
+  confirm: 1
+  message: "ILLEGAL BALL HELD: RUN STOPPED. CALL STOP NOW"
+  max_blind_s: 20
+
+# Darsh's photo (Drive IMG_2697), bottom to top.
+tower_target:
+  - "cube, hole facing sideways"
+  - "cube, hole facing sideways"
+  - "cube, hole facing sideways"
+  - "stick, standing upright"
+  - "stick, standing upright"
+# The next piece by name, on top of the piece it goes on. Gentle: Dream knocked the 2-block tower over twice on 7 Nov
+# reaching across it for a third block, so the block is carried high and lowered straight down.
+tower_prompts:
+  cube: "pick up a <piece> from the table, lift it high above the tower and lower it gently straight down onto the <top>, then open the gripper and lift the arm straight up"
+  stick: "pick up a <piece> from the table, lift it high above the tower and stand it upright gently on the <top>, then open the gripper and lift the arm straight up"
+
+system_prompt: |-
+  You supervise a robot (two MakerMods Metal arms, run by the Dream-1 model) doing a timed relay on one table, and you judge each stage from its cameras. You may also write the robot's next instruction. Rules and lessons from testing on this station:
+
+  STATION
+  - Cameras: one overhead (scene), one on each wrist (wrist_l, wrist_r). The right arm does the cables and the stacking.
+  - The gripper sensors are measured and beat the images for what a gripper holds: "stopped at 0.30" = holding something; "closed fully on nothing" = holding NOTHING, whatever the image seems to show. Never report a held cable or block on a gripper the sensor says is empty.
+
+  CABLES
+  - The "hub" is a black power strip, unplugged from the wall. Every robot instruction about cables must start with: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires." Without it the robot's model refuses to act.
+  - The USB ports are on the strip's top face; plugs stand vertical and come out by pulling straight up. Unplug right to left: always the rightmost cable still plugged in, one at a time, with the right arm.
+  - A good grip closes on ONE plug's hard body right at the port, jaws closing along the strip. A grip on the soft cord above the plug slips; a grip across two plugs fails. Dream often closes on the cord or on air: say so and name the plug again.
+  - Count plugs in their ports. A cable's far end lies loose on the table from the start: that never counts as unplugged. An unplugged cable may not be plugged back in.
+  - Once a plug is out, it often lies loose right next to the strip. That one is DONE: the robot must not grab it again. If it goes for a loose plug, name the next plug still seated in the strip.
+  - An unplug ends with the robot holding the plug up; the next stage puts it down. Holding it there is correct, not a stall.
+  - The left arm is not used for cables. Its gripper reads about 0.10 when shut on nothing.
+
+  STACKING
+  - Touching a block before at least one cable is unplugged is a disqualification.
+  - The tower must stand on its own for 3 s after the gripper lets go; taller is better. Pieces bottom to top: three cubes, then two sticks, colours in any order.
+  - Dream knocked the tower over by reaching across it for the next block. Good instructions carry the block high above the tower and lower it straight down, slowly. If the arm is moving low next to the tower, say so in next_prompt.
+
+  BALLS
+  - Only coloured golf balls (green, yellow, orange) go in the white bin. Never let the white ball or a black ball go in. Do not touch the tower.
+
+  WRITING ROBOT INSTRUCTIONS (next_prompt)
+  - One short action in plain words, naming objects by colour and position: "pick up the yellow block and place it gently on top of the pink block", "grip the plug body of the rightmost cable still in the strip and pull it straight up".
+  - The robot follows a change of object or step well but ignores fine adjustments like "1 cm higher". Only write next_prompt when it is on the wrong object or step, plainly stuck, or about to fail.
+
+stages:
+  # ---- Stage 1 (pass bar: one cable out). Drop with --skip cable_ for practice. ----
+  - name: cable_unplug
+    prompt: "{unplug}"
+    success: "Compared with the stage-start images, is at least one plug that was seated in a port of the power strip at the start now fully out of its port (held up in the right gripper, or lying loose next to the strip)? Count the seated plugs; loose far ends of cables do not count."
+    expect: "The right gripper closes on one plug's hard body at the port, jaws along the strip, and pulls it straight up until it is clear."
+    camera_notes:
+      wrist_r: "close view of the power strip's ports: count the plugs seated in ports and compare with the stage start"
+      scene: "top view: every cable's far end lies loose on the table all the time; do not count those as unplugged"
+    timeout_s: 60
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar: keep trying until the clock runs out
+    min_run_s: 4
+    settle_s: 4
+    stall_s: 25
+    recovery:
+      - {name: keep_pulling, kind: retry, when: "the gripper is on a plug or right next to the strip", max_uses: 4}
+      - {name: let_go_and_lift, kind: reset, prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up",
+         when: "the gripper is stuck, holds the cord instead of the plug, or has pushed the strip", settle_s: 5, max_uses: 3}
+    on_fail: retry_in_place

+# Hill Climb plan in Dream-1's own prompt style ("long" variant of hybrid_v4.yaml), for zero-shot base Dream-1.
+#
+# Why (8 Nov, after three live runs of hybrid_v4): Dream Labs' examples for Dream-1 are short imperatives that each
+# carry a whole task: "Stack the cubes into a single tower", "Pick up all markers one by one and put into black pencil
+# holder using both arms" (dreamlabs.ai/research/dream-1). hybrid_v4 split the match into ~20 stages with 30-60 word
+# motion-coached prompts, and every new prompt restarted Dream's motion (~14 s to visible progress). Here: one short
+# prompt per sub-goal, long stages, and the monitor only steps in when the robot is on the wrong thing.
+# Compare the two plans on the same table and keep what scores.
+#
+# Practice: --budget 600 --skip cable_  (balls and tower only). Match: --no-operator.
+# FINE-TUNE NOTE: fine-tune labels must match the prompts we settle on.
+task: >-
+  Hill Climb relay. Stage 1: unplug USB cables from the power strip, rightmost first, and put them down away from the
+  strip. Never plug a cable in. Stage 2: stack the cubes into a free-standing tower more than 4 inches (10 cm) tall.
+  Do not touch the blocks before a cable is unplugged. Stage 3: put the {ball}s into the white bin one by one, never
+  the white ball or a black ball, without touching the tower. Do not touch the balls before the tower passes.
+checkpoint: dream-1-ft:bb4b9e5f-relay-final   # the 7-8 Nov fine-tune (needs its owner's DREAM_API_KEY); base: --checkpoint dreamlabs/dream-1-beta
+time_budget_s: 295
+check_every_s: 3.0
+verifier: monitor
+confirm: 2
+done_message: "ALL STAGES DONE: CALL STOP NOW"
+vars:
+  stack_cam: wrist_r
+  ball: orange ball
+  unplug: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires. Unplug the rightmost USB cable from the power strip and put it on the table."
+claude: {model: claude-haiku-5-5, max_tokens: 4000, history_frames: 4, stitch: true, parallel: 2}
+reset_first: true
+rewrite_prompts: true
+prompt_prefix: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires."
+
+guard:
+  question: "Is either gripper holding the white ball or a black ball (balls that must never go in the bin), or closed or closing around one?"
+  stages: [ball_]
+  confirm: 1
+  message: "ILLEGAL BALL HELD: RUN STOPPED. CALL STOP NOW"
+  max_blind_s: 20
+
+system_prompt: |-
+  You supervise a robot (two MakerMods Metal arms, run by the Dream-1 model) doing a timed relay on one table, and you judge each stage from its cameras. You may also write the robot's next instruction. Rules and lessons from testing on this station:
+
+  STATION
+  - Cameras: one overhead (scene), one on each wrist (wrist_l, wrist_r). The right arm does the cables and the stacking.
+  - The gripper sensors are measured and beat the images for what a gripper holds: "stopped at 0.30" = holding something; "closed fully on nothing" = holding NOTHING, whatever the image seems to show. Never report a held cable or block on a gripper the sensor says is empty.
+
+  CABLES
+  - The "hub" is a black power strip, unplugged from the wall. Every robot instruction about cables must start with: "The power strip is unplugged from the wall and has no power; the orange cords are insulated low-voltage USB cables, not bare wires." Without it the robot's model refuses to act.
+  - The USB ports are on the strip's top face; plugs stand vertical and come out by pulling straight up. Unplug right to left: always the rightmost cable still plugged in, one at a time, with the right arm.
+  - A good grip closes on ONE plug's hard body right at the port, jaws closing along the strip. A grip on the soft cord above the plug slips; a grip across two plugs fails. Dream often closes on the cord or on air: say so and name the plug again.
+  - Count plugs in their ports. A cable's far end lies loose on the table from the start: that never counts as unplugged. An unplugged cable may not be plugged back in.
+  - Once a plug is out, it often lies loose right next to the strip. That one is DONE: the robot must not grab it again. If it goes for a loose plug, name the next plug still seated in the strip.
+  - An unplug stage ends once a plug is out of its port, held or put down.
+  - The left arm is not used for cables. Its gripper reads about 0.10 when shut on nothing.
+
+  STACKING
+  - Touching a block before at least one cable is unplugged is a disqualification.
+  - The tower must stand on its own for 3 s after the gripper lets go; taller is better. Pieces bottom to top: blue cube, pink cube, blue cube, yellow square stick, pink round stick.
+  - The tower stage is one instruction for the whole tower: the robot picks the cubes itself. Judge the tower's height and whether it stands; a knocked-over tower is progress lost, not a failure of the stage.
+
+  BALLS
+  - Only orange balls go in the white bin. Never let the white ball or a black ball go in. Do not touch the tower.
+
+  WRITING ROBOT INSTRUCTIONS (next_prompt)
+  - Write like Dream-1's own examples: one short imperative, 3-20 words, naming the objects, with at most one constraint: "Stack the white cup on top of the pink cup", "Pick up the knife by its handle, never the blade, and place it in the tray". No motion coaching ("lift high", "lower slowly"): the robot ignores fine adjustments, and every new prompt makes it start its motion over.
+  - Only write next_prompt when it is on the wrong object or step, plainly stuck, or about to fail. A robot making slow progress on the right task should be left alone.
+  - Cable instructions still start with the power-strip sentence above.
+
+stages:
+  # ---- Stage 1 (pass bar: one cable out). Drop with --skip cable_ for practice. ----
+  - name: cable_1
+    prompt: "{unplug}"
+    success: "Compared with the stage-start images, is at least one plug that was seated in a port of the power strip at the start now fully out of its port (held, or lying loose next to the strip)? Count the seated plugs; loose far ends of cables do not count."
+    camera_notes:
+      wrist_r: "close view of the power strip's ports: count the plugs seated in ports and compare with the stage start"
+      scene: "top view: every cable's far end lies loose on the table all the time; do not count those as unplugged"
+    timeout_s: 75
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar
+    min_run_s: 4
+    stall_s: 35
+    recovery:
+      - {name: let_go_and_lift, kind: reset, prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up",
+         when: "the gripper is stuck, holds the cord instead of the plug, or has pushed the strip", settle_s: 4, max_uses: 3}
+    on_fail: retry_in_place
+
+  - name: cable_2
+    prompt: "{unplug}"
+    success: "Compared with the stage-start images, is one more plug now fully out of its port than at the start of this stage?"
+    camera_notes:
+      wrist_r: "close view of the power strip's ports: count the plugs seated in ports and compare with the stage start"
+    timeout_s: 45
+    min_run_s: 3
+    stall_s: 30
+    on_fail: skip
+
+  - name: arms_clear
+    prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up"
+    success: "Are both grippers open and raised, clear of the power strip, the cables and the cubes?"
+    timeout_s: 8
+    min_run_s: 2
+    confirm: 1
+    on_fail: skip
+
+  # ---- Stage 2 (pass bar: tower > 4 in, free-standing 3 s after release). One prompt for the whole tower. ----
+  - name: tower
+    prompt: "Stack the cubes into a single tower"
+    success: "Is there a tower of cubes more than 4 inches (10 cm) tall, standing on its own, with no gripper touching it?"
+    expect: "The arms pick up the cubes one at a time and set each squarely on the tower, which grows until it is over 4 inches tall."
+    camera_notes:
+      "{stack_cam}": "view from the gripper doing the stacking"
+    timeout_s: 120
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar
+    min_run_s: 5
+    stall_s: 45
+    recovery:
+      - {name: let_go_and_lift, kind: reset, prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up",
+         when: "a gripper is stuck on a cube or pressing on the tower", settle_s: 4, max_uses: 2}
+    on_fail: retry_in_place
+
+  - name: tower_higher
+    prompt: "Stand the yellow stick upright on top of the tower"
+    success: "Is the yellow stick standing upright on top of the cube tower, the tower still standing, with no gripper touching it?"
+    camera_notes:
+      "{stack_cam}": "view from the gripper doing the stacking"
+    timeout_s: 45
+    min_run_s: 4
+    stall_s: 30
+    confirm: 2
+    on_fail: skip
+
+  # ---- Stage 3: one prompt for all the balls; the guard stops the run if an illegal ball is held. ----
+  - &ball
+    name: ball_all
+    prompt: "Put the {ball}s in the white bin one by one, never the white ball"
+    success: "Are ALL the {ball}s now resting inside the white bin (none left on the table), with no white or black ball in the bin and the tower (if any) still standing?"
+    expect: "An arm picks up one {ball} at a time and drops it in the bin, leaving the white ball and any black ball on the table."
+    timeout_s: 150
+    min_run_s: 3
+    stall_s: 45
+    confirm: 2
+    on_fail: skip

+# Darsh's operator decisions (8 Nov 2026), from docs/decisions.pdf, as the harness uses them.
+# Colours as on the real table, which the sim now matches (sim_scenes/hill_climb_table.py): coloured golf balls
+# (green, yellow, orange) are legal; the white golf ball and black 8-balls are illegal.
+#
+# Each stage entry is matched by stage name (fnmatch globs; first match wins) and adds:
+#   technique  how the robot should do it. Added to the Dream prompt when the plan sets prompt_technique: true
+#              (base Dream-1 only: a fine-tune must get its recorded texts byte for byte), and shown to the
+#              monitor as the expected technique.
+#   watch_for  error detection: signs the attempt is going wrong. The monitor reports `failing` and picks a
+#              recovery when it sees them.
+#   points     what the stage is worth, for the retry rule below.
+
+decisions:
+  # "If a task fails more than twice, move on." -> 2 attempts on stages that may be skipped.
+  attempts: 2
+  # "Max three times if it is worth a lot of points." -> 3 attempts at or above high_value_points.
+  high_value_attempts: 3
+  high_value_points: 20
+  # Pass-bar stages (on_fail retry_in_place) keep retrying: moving on before a pass bar means touching the next
+  # stage's objects, which the rubric disqualifies.
+
+# "On the last block, turn the hole to face up, then put a cylinder in the hole."
+tower_target_last_cube: "hole facing up"
+
+stages:
+  - match: [unplug_cable, unplug_more, cable_unplug, cable_unplug_more, "cable_extra_[0-9]"]
+    points: 10
+    technique: >-
+      Keep the other arm back to watch the cables from a different angle. Come in to the plug straight on, at a
+      right angle to the cable, and grip its hard body at full grip strength. Turn the cable vertical, then pull
+      straight up. You may take two or three cables at once.
+    watch_for: >-
+      the gripper closes on the cord instead of the plug's hard body; it comes in from the side or at a slant; the
+      plug is pulled at an angle or comes only part way out; the power strip is pushed, lifted or dragged; the other
+      arm drifts into the cables.
+
+  - match: [put_cable_down, put_more_down, cable_put_down, cable_put_more_down, "cable_extra_[0-9]_down"]
+    points: 0
+    technique: >-
+      Lay the unplugged cable flat on the table next to the big black power strip, then open the gripper.
+    watch_for: >-
+      the cable is dropped near the tower blocks, the golf balls or the bin; it is laid back across the ports; the
+      gripper stays closed.
+
+  - match: ["plug_*", "plug"]
+    points: 20
+    technique: >-
+      Pick up a loose USB-C cable by its black connector body, coming straight down from above at full grip
+      strength. Bring it to the first USB-C port from the left side of the power strip, line it up straight with
+      the port, and plug it in by moving the arm straight down. When it is seated, open the gripper and lift away.
+    watch_for: >-
+      the gripper holds the cord instead of the black connector; the connector is tilted against the port or aimed
+      at the wrong port; the gripper lets go before the connector is seated; a cable that was unplugged is being
+      plugged back in.
+
+  - match: [stack_2]
+    points: 20
+    technique: >-
+      Come down on the block from directly above, gripper square to the table. Set it squarely on the block below
+      with no overhang, hole facing sideways.
+    watch_for: &cube_watch >-
+      the gripper comes in at a slant or from the side; the block overhangs the one below or sits tilted; the hole
+      faces the wrong way; the tower wobbles or leans; the arm bumps the tower.
+
+  - match: [stack_3]
+    points: 20
+    technique: >-
+      Come down on the block from directly above, gripper square to the table. Set it squarely on the block below
+      with no overhang, and turn it so its hole faces straight up, ready for a stick.
+    watch_for: *cube_watch
+
+  - match: [tower_pass]
+    points: 20
+    technique: >-
+      Keep the other arm back as a watcher. Pick the stick up from directly above, turn it so it stands vertical
+      lengthwise, and lower it straight down into the hole of the top block, at a right angle to the table, as
+      upright as possible.
+    watch_for: &stick_watch >-
+      the stick is tilted or lying down; it misses the hole or rests on the rim; the arm bumps or shakes the tower;
+      the tower leans or a block shifts.
+
+  - match: ["higher_*"]
+    points: 3
+    technique: >-
+      Pick the next stick up from directly above, stand it vertical and lower it gently onto the top of the tower.
+      Move slowly and smoothly so the tower does not shake or topple.
+    watch_for: *stick_watch
+
+  - match: ["ball_*"]
+    points: 5
+    technique: >-
+      Leave the white ball alone. Pick up one coloured golf ball that is away from the tower and drop it into the
+      bin.
+    watch_for: >-
+      the gripper goes for the white ball or a black ball; the arm passes over or bumps the tower; the ball is
+      released outside the bin or knocks another ball toward the bin.
+
+# "Push the white balls off the table. If other objects are in the way, avoid the white balls."
+insert_before:
+  ball_1:
+    - name: ball_push_white   # ball_ prefix: the black/white-ball guard covers it
+      prompt: "push the white ball off the edge of the table, away from the bin and the tower"
+      success: "Is the white ball off the table top (it has fallen off the edge)?"
+      skip_unless: "Is the white ball on the table with a clear path to the nearest table edge that does not pass the tower, the bin or any coloured golf ball?"
+      expect: "One arm comes down beside the white ball and sweeps it in a straight line to the table edge and off, without picking it up."
+      technique: "Use the side of the open gripper to push; do not pick the ball up."
+      watch_for: "the gripper closes on the white ball; the ball rolls toward the bin or the tower; the arm sweeps through other balls."
+      timeout_s: 40
+      max_attempts: 1
+      min_run_s: 4
+      on_fail: skip
+      confirm: 1

+# Detector colours for the SIM scene (sim_scenes/hill_climb_table.py), PIL HSV scale 0..255, measured on the top
+# camera with MuJoCo's segmentation render (8 Nov): green ball H 91-96, yellow 41-43, orange 17-26 (S >= 140),
+# white ball S <= 15, black ball V <= 40. As on the real table (plans/table_colors.yaml): the green, yellow and orange
+# golf balls are legal targets; the white golf ball and the black 8-ball are illegal (avoid). The yellow sticks and
+# orange plugs share hues with balls: shapes decide (ball = round).
+annotate: true
+table_roi: [0.05, 0.15, 0.95, 0.95]
+guard:
+  wrist_roles: [wrist]
+  grip_roi: [0.25, 0.35, 0.75, 1.0]
+  grip_min_frac: 0.01
+classes:
+  white:  {hsv: [[0, 0, 200], [255, 13, 255]], min_frac: 0.00025, max_frac: 0.10, kinds: {ball: avoid}, small_ball: true}   # table S ~21
+  black:  {hsv: [[0, 0, 0], [255, 255, 55]], min_frac: 0.0005, max_frac: 0.10, kinds: {ball: avoid}, small_ball: true}
+  green:  {hsv: [[80, 90, 90], [110, 255, 255]], min_frac: 0.00025, kinds: {ball: target}, small_ball: true}
+  orange: {hsv: [[10, 140, 110], [30, 255, 255]], min_frac: 0.00025, kinds: {ball: target}, small_ball: true}
+  yellow: {hsv: [[35, 130, 120], [55, 255, 255]], min_frac: 0.00025, kinds: {ball: target, cube: info, stick: info}, small_ball: true}
+  blue:   {hsv: [[130, 90, 90], [165, 255, 255]], min_frac: 0.0003, kinds: {cube: info, stick: info}}
+  pink:   {hsv: [[195, 70, 90], [235, 255, 255]], min_frac: 0.0003, kinds: {cube: info, stick: info}}

+# PRACTICE ONLY (8 Nov): hill_climb_v4.yaml from arms_clear on, no cable stages. In a match, touching the blocks
+# before a cable is out is a DQ, so never use this plan in a match.
+# Hill Climb relay, match plan for hillclimb_rubric_v4.pdf (Physical AI Hacks, 7-8 Nov 2026).
+#
+# Points (v4): first cable out +10 (passes Stage 1), each further one +5, each LOOSE cable plugged into a free port
+# +20 (up to 4); tower > 4 in +20, +3 per full inch at the end, +10 for the taller tower (Sunday head-to-head);
+# each golf ball in your bin at the end +5; either black 8-ball in your bin at the end = match lost.
+# There is NO time bonus in v4: stopping early earns nothing, so this plan uses the whole clock.
+#
+# Every prompt is one of the six task texts in TELEOP_PLAN.md, byte for byte, because a fine-tune only follows
+# the texts it was trained on. Check before a run:  python -m harness.tasks plans/hill_climb_v4.yaml <dataset dirs>
+# Recoveries of kind `retry` re-send the stage's own prompt; the only extra prompt is T6.
+#
+# Rules that shape this plan:
+# - Touching a later stage's object before the current pass bar is a DQ, so a false "done" is a DQ: confirm 2,
+#   and the pass-bar stages (unplug_cable, stack_2, stack_3, tower_pass) are never skipped.
+# - No human input after "go": verifier `monitor` only. Never manual / monitor+manual / time in a match.
+# - The tower must stand 3 s after release: confirm 2 at check_every_s 3 gives two "done" verdicts >= 3 s apart.
+# - An unplugged cable may not be plugged back in: T2 puts it down away from the hub.
+# - on_fail skip ends a stage after its first failed attempt (no recovery), so skip stages are time-boxed by
+#   timeout_s alone.
+#
+# Order: unplug -> put it down -> one extra unplug -> stack to the pass bar -> more height -> balls.
+# No plug-in (decided 7 Nov 17:50): base Dream-1 never seated a plug, no teleop demo completed one, and every failed
+# try cost up to 40 s of the match. The +20 per cable is not reachable this weekend; the clock goes to the tower and balls.
+# Before the match: measure the blocks. tower_pass keeps stacking until the tower is over 4 in, and is skipped at
+# once if stack_3 already got there.
+task: >-
+  Hill Climb practice without the cables. Stage 2: stack blocks into a free-standing tower more than 4 inches (10 cm) tall. Stage 3: put golf balls into the bin, never either black 8-ball, without touching the tower.
+  Do not touch the golf balls before the tower passes.
+checkpoint: dream-1-ft:bb4b9e5f-relay-final   # the 7-8 Nov fine-tune (needs its owner's DREAM_API_KEY); base: --checkpoint dreamlabs/dream-1-beta
+time_budget_s: 295          # 5-minute match, a little margin for the start
+check_every_s: 3.0
+verifier: monitor
+confirm: 2
+done_message: "ALL STAGES DONE: CALL STOP NOW"
+# Camera role of the arm that stacks (the right arm), for the monitor's notes only.
+# The prompts never name an arm, so changing these does not change what the model is asked.
+vars: {stack_cam: wrist_r}
+claude: {model: claude-haiku-5-5, max_tokens: 4000, history_frames: 6}   # 600 cut off every reply: Haiku 5.5 thinks first
+# Black-ball guard: on every ball check the monitor also answers this. One yes stops the run at once (POST /stop:
+# the arms hold where they are, so a held black ball stays in the gripper, out of the bin). Then call "stop".
+# confirm 1, because a ball goes from gripper to bin in a few seconds; a false yes only costs the balls still to come.
+guard:
+  # The white ball is the illegal one on our table (Darsh, 8 Nov); the rubric's black 8-balls stay in the question.
+  question: "Is either gripper holding the white ball or a black 8-ball (balls that must never go in the bin), or closed or closing around one?"
+  stages: [ball_]
+  confirm: 1
+  message: "ILLEGAL BALL HELD: RUN STOPPED. CALL STOP NOW"
+  # No guard answer for this long on a ball stage (Claude API down, bad frames) also stops the run: balls are +5
+  # each, a black ball in the bin loses the match, and nobody would be looking for one.
+  max_blind_s: 20
+
+stages:
+  - name: arms_clear
+    prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up"
+    success: "Are both grippers empty and raised, clear of the hub, the cables and the blocks?"
+    timeout_s: 10
+    min_run_s: 2
+    confirm: 1
+    skip_if_done: true        # usually already true after T2 or T3
+    on_fail: skip
+
+  # ---- Stage 2 (pass bar: tower > 4 in, free-standing 3 s after release) ----
+  - name: stack_2
+    prompt: "put a block on top of the stack"
+    success: "Is one block resting on top of another block, standing on its own, with no gripper touching either?"
+    expect: "An arm picks up a block, sets it squarely on another block, opens and lifts clear."
+    camera_notes:
+      "{stack_cam}": "view from the gripper doing the stacking"
+    timeout_s: 40
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar: nothing later may be touched, so keep trying until the clock runs out
+    min_run_s: 4
+    stall_s: 20
+    recovery:
+      - {name: keep_going, kind: retry, when: "a block is held, or the tower is standing and the arm is near it", max_uses: 3}
+      - {name: let_go_and_lift, kind: reset, prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up",
+         when: "the gripper is stuck on a block or pressing on the tower", settle_s: 5, max_uses: 2}
+    on_fail: retry_in_place
+
+  - name: stack_3
+    prompt: "put a block on top of the stack"
+    success: "Is the tower three blocks tall, standing on its own, with no gripper touching it?"
+    expect: "An arm picks up a third block and places it on the two-block tower without knocking it, opens and lifts clear."
+    camera_notes:
+      "{stack_cam}": "view from the gripper doing the stacking"
+    timeout_s: 40
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar: nothing later may be touched, so keep trying until the clock runs out
+    min_run_s: 4
+    stall_s: 20
+    recovery:
+      - {name: keep_going, kind: retry, when: "the tower is standing and a block is held or near its top", max_uses: 3}
+      - {name: let_go_and_lift, kind: reset, prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up",
+         when: "the gripper is stuck on a block or pressing on the tower", settle_s: 5, max_uses: 2}
+    on_fail: retry_in_place
+
+  - name: tower_pass
+    prompt: "put a block on top of the stack"
+    success: "Is the tower more than 4 inches (10 cm) tall, standing on its own, with both grippers open and clear of it?"
+    expect: "The arm adds blocks to the tower until it is over 4 inches tall, then lifts clear; the tower stays standing."
+    camera_notes:
+      "{stack_cam}": "view from the gripper doing the stacking"
+    timeout_s: 40
+    max_attempts: 99    # pass bar: nothing later may be touched, so keep trying until the clock runs out
+    min_run_s: 4
+    stall_s: 20
+    skip_if_done: true        # stack_3 may already be over 4 in: then no extra block is risked here
+    recovery:
+      - {name: keep_going, kind: retry, when: "the tower is standing and shorter than 4 inches", max_uses: 4}
+      - {name: let_go_and_lift, kind: reset, prompt: "open both grippers and move the arms up",
+         when: "the gripper is stuck on a block or pressing on the tower", settle_s: 5, max_uses: 2}
+    on_fail: retry_in_place   # never skip: the balls are off limits until the tower passes
+
+  # ---- After the pass bar: +3 per full inch, then balls ----
+  - &higher
+    name: higher_1
+    prompt: "put a block on top of the stack"
+    success: "Does the tower have one more block on top than in the first image of this stage, standing on its own with no gripper touching it?"
+    expect: "The arm adds one block to the top without knocking the tower, opens and lifts clear."
+    camera_notes:

+"""Hill Climb relay table for Dream's YAM MuJoCo sim, modelled on the real station, plus a rubric v4 referee.
+
+patch(yam_env) wraps yam_env.build_xml(): the fruit, bowl, plate and bottle are removed and the relay objects added,
+keeping Dream's calibrated arms, cameras and lights. Every object follows the real table (Darsh's photos, Drive
+IMG_2680/2698/2702-2704, 7 Nov) and its parts' published sizes; positions are a plausible layout, not a measured one.
+The scale reference is the power strip's 25.7 cm length (a golf ball measures 4.3 cm against it, as it should):
+
+  Power strip  ALESTOR 12-outlet surge protector with 4 USB ports (Amazon B08P5LRY37: 10.1 x 3.8 x 1.2 in,
+               "4 USB ports (1 USB C)"): black body, two raised outlet panels of 6 outlets, red rocker switch,
+               Protected/Grounded lights and the black power cord at one end, the USB ports in a row on the top of
+               the end nearest the robots: USB-C first from the robot's left, then three USB-A. Ports are real slots:
+               plugs go in vertically.
+  Cables       orange USB-A to USB-C cables (~20 cm): silver USB-A tongue, orange metal shells, black ribbed strain
+               reliefs, an orange cord as a chain of ball-jointed segments. Two start plugged into USB-A ports (their
+               USB-A end), two lie loose on the table.
+  Blocks       foam, 6 cm cubes with a through hole (2.8 cm, fits both sticks): blue, magenta, blue (the target tower
+               order), a yellow 2 x 2 x 10 cm square stick and a magenta 2.5 x 11 cm round stick.
+  Balls        golf balls (4.27 cm): green, yellow and orange are legal; the WHITE golf ball is illegal (any eight_*
+               in the bin loses the match). The photos show no black 8-ball; SIM_BLACK_8BALLS=N adds N (rubric v4).
+  Basket       white plastic slotted basket, ~26 x 18 x 9 cm.
+  Table        light grey-white wood laminate.
+
+The arms are Dream's YAM arms, not the station's MakerMods Metal arms: Dream's sim ships only YAM.
+
